# AutoGPT → Claude Shepherd — feature-mining report

> Source: `/Users/adam/Programming/check out/AutoGPT` (AutoGPT Platform: blocks, visual graph builder, agent store, library/folders, scheduling, triggers/webhooks, executions/monitoring, integrations/credentials, human-review, notifications, copilot + the classic autonomous agent).  
> Target: **Claude Shepherd** — local single-user macOS console that WATCHES/STEERS/SPAWNS Claude Code GUI sessions (no graph/block execution, no credential store, no server, no marketplace).  
> Method: 12 finders → dedup → adversarial FOR/AGAINST judge (double mismatch: graph/block executor + multi-user web platform) → skeptic verify, cross-referencing queued L1–L6.  
> Counts: **175 raw → 84 candidates → 59 ADAPT, 25 DON'T-ADD, 0 pure-ADD** (all verified).

**Headline:** strong corroboration — 58 of 59 ADAPT reinforce L1–L6 (L1×28, L6×8, L5×6, L2×6, L3×2, L4×2), plus a 5-item **scheduling** cluster (cron / one-shot / recurring / visual cron builder / timezone / next-run / activity digest) and 2 small new items (per-block cost rate card; loop-detection watchdog).

---

## ✅ ADAPT — 59 items, grouped

### 🆕 SCHEDULING (multi-source new thread → proposed L7)  (5)

#### Timezone-aware scheduling with explicit resolution  `[H/S]`  · reinforces scheduling
- **For:** A scheduled-spawn feature IS emerging in Shepherd (cline mining), and any cron/at-time spawn has to answer "in which clock?" — getting that wrong means a 9am standup agent firing at 1am. The candidate's good defaults ("evaluate cron in local tz, display next-run in that tz, preserve tz across reschedules, warn when ambiguous") are exactly the operator-facing contract a local scheduler should honor. AutoGPT proves this is a real footgun worth designing for up front rather than retrofitting. And it's cheap on a single Mac: there is no per-user-tz plumbing to build — the machine's local tz is the answer — so the transferable part is just "use os.date local time for next-run display and cron evaluation," which aligns with how Shepherd already formats every other timestamp.
- **Against:** Almost the entire feature is a multi-user web-platform artifact that Shepherd's hard scope deletes. _resolve_timezone, UTC-fallback, per-user-timezone persistence on the trigger, and "link to settings to set your timezone" all exist BECAUSE AutoGPT stores schedules server-side for many users in many zones and must reconcile them; a single-user local Mac has exactly one tz (the machine's) and Shepherd already runs everything in local time via os.date, going UTC only to parse ISO transcript stamps (isoToEpoch). So the "explicit timezone resolution + no-tz-set warning + settings link" — the feature's actual substance and name — is dead weight: there is no tz to resolve, no missing-tz state to warn about, no settings page. Worse, the host feature (a cron/scheduled spawn) does not yet exist in Shepherd; this is a sub-detail of a system still only emerging, so adopting it now is premature optimization of a non-existent surface.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch applies hard on the platform axis: timezone RESOLUTION exists to reconcile many users' zones against UTC-stored schedules on a server — Shepherd has one user, one machine, no server, no UTC storage. The engine axis is neutral (cron-against-a-clock is just date math, fine for cc-core). What survives the local reduction is tiny and already true: Shepherd formats timestamps in local time everywhere (os.date at cc-core L695/L704) and reserves UTC only for parsing transcript ISO stamps. So I won't queue tz "resolution/warning/settings-link" as a feature — there's nothing to resolve. But the underlying operator NEED (when does my scheduled spawn actually fire, and is it ambiguous?) is real and reinforces the emerging "scheduling" idea. Verdict ADAPT, downscoped to one rule for whenever scheduled-spawn lands: evaluate the schedule and render next-run in machine-local time, and surface a DST-boundary caveat near a scheduled time only if cron is supported. Not ADD (the feature as-shipped is mostly inapplicable plumbing); not DONT_ADD (the local-clock contract is a legitimate, if small, default to bake into the future scheduler).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into the emerging "scheduled spawn / scheduled agent" idea (not a new Lx). When that lands, the scheduler in cc-core evaluates fire-times in MACHINE-LOCAL time (os.date, matching existing L695/L704 formatting) — no _resolve_timezone, no per-schedule timezone field, no UTC fallback, no settings link, since the Mac has exactly one tz. Show each schedule's next-run as a local "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM" on its tile/preset. Optionally, IF cron expressions are supported, show a small caveat when a scheduled time lands inside a DST transition. The saved tz/warning/resolution machinery from AutoGPT is dropped as inapplicable to single-user local.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT ruling already absorbs the double-mismatch and survives. I verified the judge's factual basis in cc-core.lua: human-facing timestamps render in MACHINE-LOCAL time via plain os.date (L704 renderNarrative "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M"; L695 comment notes it's plain-lua safe), and the "!" UTC form is reserved ONLY for timezone-independent civil-date math — ledger file naming/comparison (L617 ledgerDayIsPast os.date("!%Y-%m-%d"); L587-593 ledgerFileEpoch via isoToEpoch; L1625-1626 isoToEpoch parsing ISO-Z transcript stamps; dashboard L2154 redaction-day). There is NO UTC storage of user-facing schedules and NO timezone field anywhere. grep across todos.md/context.md confirms NO existing scheduling/cron feature and confirms the hard scope (context.md L9/L13: local, single-user, no network, no multi-user, no secrets). The feature AS SHIPPED is pure AutoGPT mismatch-(b) plumbing: timezone RESOLUTION (_resolve_timezone, a saved per-schedule tz field, the "will run in UTC" warning, the settings link, reschedules preserving the saved tz) exists only because a MULTI-USER SERVER stores schedules in UTC and must reconcile each user's zone. Shepherd is one user / one Mac / one tz / no server / no UTC storage, so every piece of that machinery is inapplicable — the DONT_ADD case. But the judge ALREADY made that reduction and did NOT keep the feature: it dropped the entire resolution/warning/settings-link machinery and retained only a one-line LOCAL-CLOCK CONTRACT folded into the EMERGING "scheduled spawn" idea (explicitly not a new Lx) — evaluate fire-times and render next-run in machine-local time (matching existing L704 formatting), with a DST caveat only IF cron is supported. That is the correct disposition: the operator NEED ("when does my scheduled spawn actually fire?") is real and reinforces the emerging scheduler, so it is not DONT_ADD; and the as-shipped feature is mostly inapplicable plumbing, so it is not ADD. That is the textbook ADD->ADAPT reinforcement. My only skeptical reservation is the "high" confidence — the retained residue is so thin (local-time formatting is already true) it's nearly a no-op — but the verdict itself (ADAPT, reinforces the emerging scheduling idea, strip the tz-resolution/warning/settings machinery) stands. Refutation does not succeed. Relevant: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (L587-593, L609-618, L694-710, L1625-1626); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (L2154-2158).

#### Periodic activity digest (daily / weekly / monthly summary)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces scheduling
- **For:** The CONTENT and DELIVERY of this feature already exist in Shepherd and are proven; the only genuinely new capability — a scheduled, push-cadence delivery — is small, useful, and fits the local model exactly. Shepherd already computes nearly the entire stat set the digest wants: fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) and fleetStandup/standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1137/1201) produce sessions active, prompts, tool requests, spawns, allow/deny split, who-decided provenance, escalations, stalls, error recoveries, blocked-time, and a most-active + per-project breakdown — all as pure deterministic aggregation over the local ledger. Token/context usage tracking exists separately. Delivery via ntfy is already wired (FX.push, claude-dashboard.lua:973), and there's an established periodic-timer loop (hs.timer.doEvery, e.g. line 831) plus PANEL_START_TS windowing. So the ENTIRE marginal cost is: a cron-like cadence (daily/weekly), a window anchor, and firing the existing standupMarkdown through FX.push instead of only the manual open-shift pull (line 1991). That turns the shift report from pull-only into the 'what did the fleet do overnight/this week' push the operator actually wants without opening the panel. It also concretely advances the emerging 'scheduling' primitive with a real first consumer, and the stat set is a ready content template.
- **Against:** It risks being redundant: the shift report ALREADY answers 'what did the fleet do over a window' on demand with 8h/24h/since-opened presets, and notification history already surfaces alerts — so a scheduled digest is arguably just an auto-fire of something a single operator can pull in one click whenever they sit down. For a single-user local console that's usually open on the operator's own machine, the multi-user value proposition behind AutoGPT's digest (re-engage absent users via email) evaporates entirely. Worse, the AutoGPT implementation's center of gravity — an APScheduler cron that finds ACTIVE USERS in a DB and queues a SUMMARY PER USER, then emails a Jinja template — is pure multi-user-platform plumbing (the (b) half of the double mismatch) that Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host; copying it wholesale is a non-starter. And anything 'scheduled' adds an always-on background timer and a fired notification, which cuts against the off-by-default, the-panel-is-the-surface ethos unless carefully gated.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: AutoGPT's digest value splits cleanly. Part (a/b) — cron iterating ACTIVE USERS in a DB, queuing one SUMMARY per user, emailing a Jinja template (notifications.py:106, scheduler.py:892) — is multi-user platform plumbing Shepherd cannot host; that half is DONT_ADD and the candidate's own note correctly drops it ('No multi-user iteration/DB'). What survives is the OPERATOR PATTERN: a periodic run-history rollup pushed on a cadence. That pattern is real and partly already built — Shepherd has the aggregation (fleetStandup/fleetStats), the markdown renderer (standupMarkdown), the delivery (ntfy FX.push), and the timer loop. The single missing piece is the SCHEDULE: today the shift report is pull-only (open-shift, line 1991). Adding a daily/weekly auto-fire is a genuine, in-scope extension, not a re-add, and it lands squarely on the emerging 'scheduling' idea rather than warranting a new Lx. Hence ADAPT/reinforces=scheduling, not DONT_ADD (not redundant — push cadence is new) and not ADD (must be reshaped off the cron/DB/email plumbing and built on existing primitives). Note the AutoGPT 'credits/billing/cost' fields map only loosely — Shepherd has no API-cost ground truth (it makes no API calls), so the cost-breakdown column degrades to token/context usage, which is fine.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a scheduled-digest consumer: a daily/weekly (cron-like) timer, off by default, configured via notifications.* / a new schedule key. On fire it calls the EXISTING core.fleetStandup(ledgerSnapshot(), {sinceTs=lastFireOrWindow, untilTs=now}) + core.standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1137/1201) and pushes a short headline (sessions run/completed/errored via error-recoveries proxy, allow/deny, most-active, escalations, token usage) through FX.push (claude-dashboard.lua:973), with the full markdown also available in the 📋 Shift tab. Reuse the open-shift window logic (line 1991) for the anchor; persist last-fire ts in hs.settings like ccNotifySeen. The cron cadence becomes the first concrete consumer of the 'scheduling' primitive (also future home of scheduled spawns). Cost column → token/context usage, since Shepherd has no API-spend ground truth.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not. Applying the double mismatch: AutoGPT's digest is half platform plumbing (a cron that iterates ACTIVE USERS in a DB, queues one SUMMARY per user bypassing batching, and emails a Jinja template — notifications.py/scheduler.py). That half is DONT_ADD and the candidate note already drops it ("No multi-user iteration/DB"). It needs none of Shepherd's forbidden surfaces — no graph/block executor, no server, no OAuth/credentials, no marketplace. What survives is a pure operator pattern: a periodic run-history rollup pushed on a cadence. I verified Shepherd already owns every ingredient: aggregation in core.fleetStandup/fleetStats (cc-core.lua:1137/954), the renderer core.standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1201), ntfy delivery FX.push (claude-dashboard.lua:973), the timer loop (hs.timer.doEvery), and hs.settings persistence (ccNotifySeen pattern). I checked for an existing scheduled/digest fire and found none — the shift report is strictly PULL-ONLY, fired only by the UI 'open-shift' handler (claude-dashboard.lua:1991), reusing PANEL_START_TS for the window anchor. So the single genuinely missing piece is the SCHEDULE/cadence: an off-by-default daily/weekly timer that auto-calls fleetStandup+standupMarkdown over a since-last-fire window and pushes a headline through FX.push, with the full markdown in the existing 📋 Shift tab. That is not redundant (push cadence is a new behavior — today nothing fires it unattended) and not a full ADD (it must be reshaped off the cron/DB/email and built on existing primitives), so ADAPT is correct. It lands on the emerging 'scheduling' idea (the cline-mined scheduled-spawn/scheduled-agent primitive's first concrete consumer), not a new Lx. One honest degradation I confirmed: AutoGPT's credits/cost-breakdown fields have no analog — Shepherd makes no API calls and has no cost/billing/credit ground truth (grep shows only 'zero API cost' token tracking, claude-dashboard.lua:314/5790). So the cost column degrades to local token/context usage. That weakens the content mapping but does not sink the feature. Verdict ADAPT (high), reinforces = scheduling.

#### Opt-in / default-off telemetry & onboarding-funnel signals + schedule-health diagnostics  `[H/S]`  · reinforces scheduling
- **For:** Two strands have local value. (1) The "instrumentation is opt-in, default-off" principle is sound operator hygiene and is the exact stance Shepherd should keep — useful as a guardrail when evaluating any future automation. (2) The schedule-health "count + upcoming" rollup (system-vs-user split dropped) is a clean, server-free, pure-aggregation readout — "N scheduled spawns, next at …" — that slots straight into the existing fleetStandup/insights footer (cc-core.lua:1137, the 📋 Shift tab) and gives the operator an at-a-glance answer to "what's about to spawn while I'm away," directly extending the emerging cline-derived scheduling idea. It is read-only diagnostics, exactly the kind of management/run-history pattern that transfers.
- **Against:** The headline of the candidate is a DOUBLE MISMATCH on the multi-user-platform axis. Consent-gated telemetry, Sentry user tagging, and onboarding-funnel milestones (complete_onboarding_step(TRIGGER_WEBHOOK), first-marketplace-add flips) exist to instrument a hosted SaaS funnel across accounts and ship diagnostics to a vendor — Shepherd has no server (HARD SCOPE forbids one), no accounts, no marketplace, no vendor endpoint. The opt-in/default-off philosophy is not a feature to port: Shepherd already embodies it (opt-in audit ledger, every automation off-by-default, spawn.live dry-run default), so porting it is redundant. And the one salvageable crumb (schedule-health) presupposes a scheduled-spawn primitive that does not exist yet — confirmed zero matches in core/todos/context — so it cannot stand on its own today.
- **Ruling:** The candidate bundles three things that split cleanly. The telemetry/Sentry/onboarding-funnel bulk is DONT_ADD: it is pure multi-user-platform plumbing (vendor diagnostics, account tagging, marketplace/funnel milestones) that Shepherd structurally cannot host and that is redundant with the opt-in/default-off stance Shepherd already lives by. The only transferable element is the schedule-health "count + upcoming" rollup, with the user-vs-system split dropped as meaningless single-user. That rollup is read-only aggregation that belongs in the existing insights/shift-report panel — but it only becomes meaningful once a scheduled-spawn primitive exists, and that is an EMERGING idea (cline mining), not yet built (verified: no scheduling/cron primitive anywhere in cc-core.lua, todos.md, or context.md). So the honest disposition is ADAPT-LITE, reinforcing the emerging "scheduling" idea rather than standing as its own system; the bulk of the candidate is rejected. Per the rubric, an item that extends the emerging scheduling idea is ADAPT with reinforces=scheduling — not a parallel telemetry system.
- **How (Shepherd):** Drop telemetry/Sentry/onboarding-funnel entirely (no server, redundant with the opt-in audit ledger + off-by-default automations). Salvage only the schedule-health rollup AS PART OF the emerging scheduling feature: when scheduled spawns land, add a pure cc-core aggregator (sibling to fleetStandup at cc-core.lua:1137) that returns {scheduledCount, nextAt, perProject} and surface it as a one-line readout in the 📋 Shift tab / 📊 insights footer ("N scheduled spawns, next at HH:MM"). No user-vs-system split (single-user). No new flag — it inherits the scheduling feature's own opt-in.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds, reinforcing the emerging "scheduling" idea. The candidate splits cleanly, and I verified each pillar. REJECTED BULK (DONT_ADD, correctly): telemetry opt-in/Sentry user tagging and onboarding-milestone/funnel signals (TRIGGER_WEBHOOK, "first marketplace add flips a step") are pure multi-user-platform plumbing. Shepherd has NO server, NO accounts, NO vendor telemetry, NO marketplace — structurally cannot host any of it, and the "instrumentation is opt-in/default-off" philosophy is already embodied (verified: README.md:501 opt-in JSONL audit ledger; context.md:12 + README.md:778 off-by-default automations like spawn.live, auto-respawn, routing). So that half is both impossible and redundant. SALVAGED CRUMB (the ADAPT): the schedule-health "count + upcoming" rollup, with the user-vs-system split dropped as meaningless single-user. This is read-only aggregation that fits Shepherd's pure-cc-core pattern — a sibling to M.fleetStandup (verified at cc-core.lua:1137, returns rollups like {totals, byProject, mostActive}) returning {scheduledCount, nextAt, perProject}, surfaced as one line in the existing 📋 Shift tab / 📊 insights footer ("N scheduled spawns, next at HH:MM"). It needs no graph/block executor, no credentials, no server. WHY NOT DONT_ADD (the strongest refutation path): I confirmed there is NO scheduling/cron primitive anywhere — grep for "schedul|cron|nextAt|scheduledCount|upcoming|recurring|daily|hourly" across todos.md, cc-core.lua, context.md, README.md yields nothing (the only "schedule" hits are the GC-safe timer scheduler at cc-core.lua:433/2422, unrelated). One could argue a schedule-health readout aggregates over a non-existent primitive and is therefore unbuildable/non-transferable. But the orchestrator framing explicitly recognizes "scheduled spawn / scheduled agent" as an emerging idea from cline mining and instructs that matching scheduling features be treated as reinforcing it ("call it 'scheduling'"). So the crumb is a dependent reinforcement of that emerging feature, not nothing. WHY NOT ADD: the rollup is meaningful only once the scheduled-spawn primitive lands; it cannot be added standalone today and inherits the scheduling feature's own opt-in (no new flag). That dependence is the definition of ADAPT-LITE / reinforcement, not a parallel additive system. Disposition: ADAPT (reinforces=scheduling). Drop telemetry/Sentry/onboarding-funnel entirely; salvage only the single-line schedule-health readout as part of the emerging scheduling feature, no user-vs-system split, no new flag.

#### Scheduled spawn / cron + one-shot + recurring runs  `[H/M]`  · reinforces scheduling
- **For:** The operator pattern here is the single strongest scheduling pattern in the whole AutoGPT mining set, and it lands squarely on primitives Shepherd already owns. The fire-action ("at time T, in folder F with provider/model/permMode and seed prompt P, start the agent") is exactly spawnEditorWindow(core.spawnSpec(...)) — Shepherd's spawn-preset / provider / respawnSpec machinery — fired by a timer instead of a button. The "resume an EXISTING session vs spawn a BRAND-NEW one" fork maps 1:1 onto Shepherd's two existing delivery paths: enqueue-a-nudge-into-a-live-tile (the queue + feedTask/nudge + dispatchSerialized chokepoint) vs spawn-a-fresh-session. The concurrency-cap deferral ("a blocked one-shot is pushed out a few minutes, bounded retry") maps onto fleet-size backpressure that Shepherd can read off the live tile grid. The one-shot ("check CI in 20 min, then delete the job") is genuinely additive — Shepherd has no deferred-action concept today. And it directly serves the user's stated priority: saved agents (L1) become far more useful when they can be launched unattended on a cadence ("daily 9am brief clean-slate; resume the long review at 14:00"). Implementation cost is low: Shepherd already runs hs.timer.doEvery/doAfter, the GC-safe after() retained-timer wrapper, the bridge-sync timer pool, and a startup-sweep pattern (remoteControl.sweepOnStartup) it can copy for "re-arm schedules on launch."
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's described mass is AutoGPT double-mismatch plumbing that Shepherd structurally cannot and must not host: the SQLAlchemyJobStore is a server-side database, add_graph_execution RUNS a graph in the in-process block executor, create_chat_session spins a Copilot/server session, GraphExecutionJobArgs carries persisted credentials, and max_instances/per-user quotas are multi-user serving concerns. APScheduler with a SQL jobstore is overkill for a single-user local panel. There is a real correctness hazard too: a macOS box asleep/closed at fire time silently misses runs — coalesce/missed-run semantics AutoGPT gets free from a daemon are nontrivial under Hammerspoon, and a launchd plist (the only way to fire while Hammerspoon is down) reintroduces persistence/idempotency that the "just a timer" framing waves away. Unattended spawning also quietly bypasses the human-in-the-loop posture that is Shepherd's core — a scheduled fresh session can start work with nobody watching — so it must ship OFF by default and inherit the gate, or it erodes the safety story. The recommendedScheduleCron prefill is graph metadata with no analog here.
- **Ruling:** Textbook ADAPT. Stripping the AutoGPT plumbing (SQL jobstore, graph executor, credential args, per-user quota, Copilot session sentinel — all DONT_ADD) leaves a pure operator pattern — "fire a saved spawn/nudge on a cron or one-shot timer" — and that pattern lands on primitives Shepherd already has: spawnSpec/respawnSpec + providers + presets for the fresh-session fire, the queue + feedTask/nudge + dispatchSerialized chokepoint for the resume-existing fire, the tile grid for concurrency backpressure, the GC-safe after()/doEvery timers + sweepOnStartup pattern for the scheduler runtime, and the JSONL ledger for fire history. The prompt itself flags an emerging 'scheduling' candidate from cline mining and says to reinforce it rather than open a new Lx — so reinforces='scheduling'. It does NOT require running a graph, executing block code, storing OAuth creds, or serving multiple users. It also pays off the saved-agents priority (L1). Build small and OFF by default: schedules in local JSON (cc-schedules.json) fired by Hammerspoon timers, with an optional launchd plist as the asleep-miss backstop — NOT APScheduler, NOT a SQL jobstore. The one-shot deferred action is the genuinely new bit; everything else is wiring a timer to existing buttons.
- **How (Shepherd):** New cc-schedules.json holding entries {name, kind: cron|oneShot, when (cron expr or epoch), tz, target: spawn|resume, spawnSpec-or-agentRef (folder/provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt — reuse L1 agent profiles + cc-presets.json), resumeProjectKey?+nudgeText?, enabled:false}. A scheduler module beside the 1s loop: a dedicated hs.timer.doEvery evaluates due entries via pure core.dueSchedules(schedules, now, liveTiles) — cron-next-fire + one-shot expiry are pure with injected now. Fire path forks: spawn -> spawnEditorWindow(core.spawnSpec(...)) (existing spawn machinery + providers); resume -> push nudgeText into the target tile's queue + nudge via feedTask/handleAction/dispatchSerialized (skip-on-no-window contract already enforced). One-shot self-deletes after a delivered fire (gate on the FX delivery return, like queue pops). Concurrency cap = pure core.scheduleBackpressure(liveTiles, cap) -> defer N min, bounded retries (mirror _reschedule_one_shot_after_cap). On startup a schedule.sweepOnStartup re-arms cron timers and runs/skips coalesced missed one-shots (copy remoteControlSweepTargets pattern). Optional launchd plist (sibling to install.sh/make machinery) as the asleep-while-due backstop that just opens Shepherd / writes a trigger file — NOT a second executor. Ledger each fire as scheduled_spawn/scheduled_resume (feeds Alerts + shift report). DONT_ADD: SQLAlchemyJobStore, add_graph_execution, create_chat_session sentinel, GraphExecutionJobArgs credentials, max_instances/per-user quota, recommendedScheduleCron graph metadata. ⚙ Settings toggle, OFF by default; fired sessions inherit the gate.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on all four common-error tests; verified against the codebase. (a) No graph/block executor needed: the fire-action is "spawn a Claude Code GUI session" (existing core.spawnSpec at cc-core.lua:3061 + spawnEditorWindow) or "nudge an existing tile" (existing core.handleAction nudge at cc-core.lua:138 + feedTask + the dispatchSerialized chokepoint). Shepherd runs nothing; the timer just presses an existing button at a time. The AutoGPT add_graph_execution/create_chat_session sentinel is correctly carved out as DONT_ADD. (b) No server/credential/multi-user surface: every AutoGPT plumbing piece — SQLAlchemyJobStore, GraphExecutionJobArgs credentials, max_instances per-user quota, recommendedScheduleCron graph metadata, account-wide schedule lists — is explicitly DONT_ADD in the ruling. Persistence-across-restart (the one real requirement) is met locally via cc-schedules.json + a sweepOnStartup re-arm + optional launchd plist, NOT a SQL jobstore. This matches Shepherd's local-only/no-secrets scope (context.md:13) and the existing local-JSON-file pattern (cc-presets.json, cc-agents.json, cc-groups.json). (c) NOT redundant and NOT a misplaced new system: grep for cron/scheduled_spawn/cc-schedules/time-of-day across cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua returned EMPTY — no scheduling exists today. Crucially, every existing autonomy trigger (auto-respawn shouldAutoRespawn, auto-continue stepAutoContinue, stuck watchdog isHung) is EVENT-EDGE-triggered (reacts to a session dying/freezing/going stale), never wall-clock/cadence-triggered, so a cron/one-shot scheduler is genuinely net-new capability. The prompt itself flags an emerging "scheduling" candidate from cline mining and instructs that an AutoGPT scheduling match should reinforce it rather than open a new Lx — the ruling does exactly that (reinforces='scheduling'). (d) The platform plumbing IS stripped; the operator residue ("fire a saved spawn/nudge on a cron or one-shot timer, off by default, ledgered") is a real local-console feature — a 'check CI in 20 min' one-shot and a 'daily 9am brief' fresh-spawn are precisely watch/steer/spawn automation for a single-user fleet console. Verified the runtime primitives all exist: the doEvery(POLL_SECONDS, refresh) main loop (claude-dashboard.lua:5786) for a sibling scheduler timer, GC-safe after()/pendingTimers (cc-core comment at 1184), the remoteControlSweepTargets sweep-on-startup pattern (cc-core.lua:2250) to copy for re-arming missed fires, providers/presets for the fresh-spawn target, and the JSONL ledger (NOTIFY_TYPES, fleetStandup) for fire history. It also pays off the user's #1 priority (L1 saved agents — the fire target reuses agent profiles). Build small, off by default, with the concurrency-cap deferral mapped onto fleet-size backpressure and the one-shot self-deleting on a delivered fire (gated on the FX delivery return, same skip-on-no-window contract at context.md:62-67). ADAPT holds; reinforces the emerging 'scheduling' candidate.

#### Visual cron builder + human-readable cadence + next-run display  `[H/M]`  · reinforces scheduling
- **For:** The AutoGPT double mismatch does not bite here at all: this is a pure client-side UI pattern (a frequency state machine + makeCronExpression/parser round-trip + a humanized cadence string + a localized next-run label) with zero dependence on the graph/block executor, block-code execution, OAuth credential storage, or multi-user/marketplace serving. It is exactly the MANAGEMENT/SCHEDULING-display layer the framing says CAN transfer. The need is genuine and fits Shepherd's local single-user model precisely: once a "scheduled spawn" exists, hand-writing cron is operator-hostile, and a frequency picker (hourly/daily/weekly/monthly + weekday/day-of-month multi-select with Weekdays/Weekends/All shortcuts) is strictly better UX. The two display halves are nearly free given primitives Shepherd already owns — a humanized "Every Monday at 9:00 AM" cadence string and a "Next spawn: Mon, Jun 16, 9:00 AM (America/New_York) / Next in 2 hours" label are pure cc-core derivations rendered into the existing tile/detail surface, consistent with sparklines/insights/fmtDuration (cc-core.lua:902) and the resets-in countdown already shown in renderUsageFoot. The natural host already exists: the New-session modal + spawn presets registry (cc-presets.json, core.presetList/presetPush at cc-core.lua:3640) is exactly where a per-preset schedule field and its cadence/next-run labels would attach. The user's saved-agents/templates interest is favorable: a "saved agent that spawns on a schedule" is the obvious extension of L1 profiles + presets.
- **Against:** The candidate's value is entirely parasitic on a firing mechanism that does not exist in Shepherd today. A grep for cron/schedule/recurr across the codebase finds only the beat-list keystroke scheduler (runSequence) and the rsync cadence — there is NO scheduled-spawn feature. AutoGPT computes next_run_time via APScheduler (a server-side scheduler holding the actual timers); Shepherd has no equivalent, and standing up the local timer/edge-discipline that actually FIRES a spawn at the right cron tick (de-dup across reloads, missed-fire-while-asleep semantics, bounded per-folder budgets, off-by-default) is the load-bearing work — the candidate itself concedes it is "lower priority than the firing mechanism." Building the pretty cron builder + cadence labels first would be a UI on top of a no-op: nothing to schedule, nothing to display a next-run for. It is therefore not ADD (it cannot ship as a standalone improvement to today's watch/steer console) and risks scope creep if mistaken for a self-contained feature.
- **Ruling:** Splitting vendor-from-need: the AutoGPT artifact carries none of the disqualifying plumbing (no graph/blocks, no OAuth store, no marketplace, no multi-user server) — it is pure React state + cron string utils + Intl/date-fns formatting, all of which transfer cleanly to a local console. So this is not DONT_ADD; the double mismatch does not apply. But it is also not ADD, because its worth depends on a scheduled-spawn FIRING mechanism Shepherd does not yet have (confirmed: grep finds only the keystroke beat-scheduler and rsync cadence, no timed spawn). The candidate is the authoring + display surface for the emerging "scheduling" idea (cline-mined), not a parallel system — its own note says it is lower priority than the firing mechanism. That is the definition of ADAPT/reinforces: real need, reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, extending a queued/emerging item. Confidence high because both the fit (pure-local, no proxy/credential dependency) and the dependency (no firing mechanism yet) are concretely verifiable in the tree.
- **How (Shepherd):** Treat this as the AUTHORING + DISPLAY layer of the emerging "scheduled spawn" feature, built AFTER (or alongside) its firing mechanism. (1) Schedule model: add an optional schedule field to a spawn preset / saved agent (cc-presets.json, core.presetPush at cc-core.lua:3673; or an L1 agent profile) holding either a cron string or the structured frequency object. (2) Builder UI: a frequency picker in the New-session modal (mirror AutoGPT's CronFrequency state machine in panel JS) that produces/round-trips the cron string — pure, no server. (3) Cadence + next-run as PURE cc-core derivations: core.humanizeCron(cron) -> 'Every Monday at 9:00 AM' and core.nextRun(cron, now) -> epoch, rendered via the existing fmtDuration (cc-core.lua:902) for the relative 'Next in 2h' and an absolute localized stamp with the local tz, shown in the detail panel beside the schedule (sibling to the resets-in countdown in renderUsageFoot). next_run computed locally from cron + injected now (NOT APScheduler), unit-tested with zero hs.*. (4) The actual timer that fires the spawn is the separate 'scheduling' firing-mechanism work (off by default, edge-disciplined like auto-respawn, ledger a scheduled_spawn event) — this candidate plugs into it as the picker + labels.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every downgrade vector; ADAPT holds, reinforcing the emerging "scheduling" idea (cline-mined, itself ruled ADAPT reinforcing L6). (a) No executor/graph/block mismatch: the candidate is a pure cron BUILDER plus two pure display derivations — humanizeCron->'Every Monday at 9:00 AM' and nextRun(cron,now)->epoch. No agent loop, no block exec, no graph state. The mining doc independently confirms the portable pieces (buildCronPattern/parseCronPattern/expandCronDays, cline lines 266-336) "drop in as pure functions" and nextRunAt "is pure math that belongs in cc-core" (docs/feature-mining/cline.md:24-27); the execution-heavy parts (RoutineExecution/maxIterations/timeoutSeconds/maxParallel/last-result) are exactly what gets dropped and the cron builder carries none of them. (b) No server/OAuth/marketplace: React state + cron string + Intl/date-fns formatting, pure client-side single-user; the judge mirrors it onto panel JS + pure cc-core rendered through the existing fmtDuration (verified cc-core.lua:902). (c) The hardest lever — redundant/queued — confirms ADAPT, not a further move. grep over the repo finds ZERO clock/cron/next-run/firing primitive: every Shepherd automation (auto-respawn cc-core.lua:2116, auto-continue 2200, autopilot, project routing, queue) is level/event-triggered; the only "schedule" in code is the keystroke beat-scheduler (cc-core.lua:433) and rsync cadence (claude-dashboard.lua:159). So it is NOT redundant with anything shipped (rules out DONT_ADD), but it is NOT a clean standalone ADD either: its entire value is gated on a scheduled-spawn FIRING mechanism Shepherd does not have, which is itself only an emerging/queued candidate (cline's "Cron scheduling engine"/"Event-triggered agent runs", ruled ADAPT reinforcing L6 at docs/feature-mining/cline.md:453-458, 475-478). The candidate's own note concedes it is "lower priority than the firing mechanism" — authoring+display surface for a queued/emerging primitive is the definition of ADAPT/reinforces. (d) Not DB plumbing: a frequency picker with live cron preview + a per-rule "next spawn at..." label (sibling to the existing resets-in countdown, verified resetsIn/renderUsageFoot at claude-dashboard.lua:4871-4897) is an operator-console authoring surface in Shepherd's preset-modal/insights idiom. All judge-cited anchors verified in-tree (presetPush 3673, spawnFlags 3001, spawnSpec 3061, fmtDuration 902). Cannot push to DONT_ADD (need is real, fully local, no executor/credential/server dependency) nor to ADD (gated on the not-yet-built firing mechanism, extends a queued item). ADAPT is correct.

### 🆕 GENUINELY NEW  (2)

#### Per-block cost / pricing model exposed to UI (rate card)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** Shepherd already has the entire substrate this needs and one missing layer the rate card supplies. cc-core's `sumUsage` returns a per-model token breakdown (`byModel` with input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate), `claude-dashboard.lua`'s `FX.computeUsage` aggregates it fleet-wide + per-session + per-window incrementally every 60s at zero API cost, the fleet footer already renders per-model token detail, and the `providers` registry already stores per-model attributes keyed by `model` (it holds `contextLimit` today). AutoGPT's transferable kernel is exactly the DISPLAY shape the brief calls out: a per-model rate card ($/1M input / output / cache) multiplied against counts to show an estimated $ figure. That is a thin, pure, additive function — `core.estimateCost(byModel, rateCard)` — feeding numbers that already flow to the footer and detail panel. It directly serves the operator question "how much is this fleet costing me right now," which the current token-only view answers only obliquely, and it stays inside hard scope: no network, no billing backend, no per-user metering — just a local rate map applied to locally-parsed transcript counts.
- **Against:** Almost everything in the AutoGPT feature is the wrong half of the double mismatch. BlockCost / BlockCostType (RUN / BYTE / SECOND / ITEMS), the `cost()` decorator, the `BLOCK_COSTS` AutoRegistry that back-fills from provider rate cards, `GET /blocks` serialization, and "dynamic types return 0 pre-flight and reconcile against execution stats" all only have meaning inside an in-process block/graph executor that runs blocks and tracks node execution state — which Shepherd structurally is not. The billing/credit-metering purpose (the platform charges users per run) is pure multi-user-platform plumbing Shepherd cannot and must not host (no server, no accounts, no secrets/credit store). Even the surviving display kernel risks being a vanity number: estimated $ from local transcript tokens is an approximation that can mislead on a flat-rate Claude Code plan (where the plan window, not $, is the real constraint Shepherd already shows officially via /api/oauth/usage), so it adds maintenance (a hand-kept rate map that drifts as Anthropic repricing happens) for a metric that doesn't gate any operator action.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the feature's machinery (BlockCost types, cost() decorator, BLOCK_COSTS registry, GET /blocks serialization, reconciliation against execution stats) is graph/block-executor + multi-user billing plumbing — squarely DONT_ADD. But the brief itself prescribes a thin ADAPT, and it is genuinely well-targeted: Shepherd already owns the hard part (a zero-cost per-model token aggregation pipeline with a per-model footer and a model-keyed providers registry). All that's missing is a rate map and one pure multiply. So this is not DONT_ADD (the underlying need — visibility into per-model spend — is real and the primitives already exist) and not ADD (you must NOT import BlockCost/cost()/the registry; you reshape only the rate-card display onto existing token totals). That is the textbook ADAPT. It doesn't cleanly map to an L1-L6 backlog item — it extends the shipped token/usage observability subsystem rather than the agent-profiles/policy/template/routing/event-rule tracks, and it isn't scheduling — so reinforces is empty. Confidence high: the attach points are concrete and the in-scope kernel is small. Guard against the against-side by making it opt-in, sourcing rates from the existing providers profiles (so gateway/local models can carry their own $/1M), and keeping the official plan-window display as the primary signal — the $ estimate is a secondary, clearly-labelled approximation.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure `core.estimateCost(byModel, rateCard)` in cc-core.lua next to `sumUsage`/`formatTokens` (~line 1684-1727): for each model multiply input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate by the matching $/1M rates and sum to a fleet + per-session $ total. Store rates on the existing `providers` registry entries (already keyed by `model`, already holds `contextLimit` per `contextLimitFor` ~1765) as optional `rate = {in, out, cacheRead, cacheCreate}`; ship a small default map for native Claude models. Surface in claude-dashboard.lua's existing fleet footer per-model detail (the `renderUsageFoot` path / `ccUsage` payload ~4864-4908) and the detail panel as a labelled "est. $" line beside the existing token totals, secondary to the official plan-window % which stays primary. Gate behind a config flag (off by default), purely local, no network, reusing the 60s `FX.computeUsage` aggregation already running. Mirror the JS fmt twin and add ui.test/core.test pins like the existing barLevel/contextBand twin guard.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute; could not. The AutoGPT machinery is correctly DONT_ADD — BlockCost types, the cost() decorator, the BLOCK_COSTS registry, GET /blocks serialization, and reconciliation against execution stats are all graph/block-executor + multi-user billing plumbing, and Shepherd runs no graph / executes no blocks / serves no endpoint. But the brief prescribes a THIN display-only ADAPT, and I verified its attach points are real, not aspirational: (1) cc-core.lua:1684 sumUsage already returns s.byModel keyed by model, each carrying input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate — the exact shape a $/1M rate map multiplies; (2) the providers registry (contextLimitFor ~1765) is model-keyed and already holds optional per-model fields like contextLimit (cc-config.example.json:104), so an optional rate={in,out,cacheRead,cacheCreate} fits the existing pattern; (3) the dashboard already renders a per-model footer (renderUsageFoot, claude-dashboard.lua:4882) and per-model detail rows iterating ps.byModel (renderDetailUsage ~4920), so a labelled 'est. $' line slots beside existing token totals; (4) the zero-API-cost 60s FX.computeUsage aggregation (~331) already runs locally off transcripts; (5) a formatTokens/barLevel/contextBand twin-guard js-pin pattern already exists (tests/ui.test.lua:586, core.test.lua:1160) for a new estimateCost to mirror. The in-scope kernel is one pure estimateCost(byModel, rateCard) (multiply+sum), an optional config field, a small default map, and one secondary UI line — purely local, no network/server/credentials/marketplace, so the AutoGPT double mismatch does not touch the reshaped display. Not redundant and not queued: grep confirms cost/pricing/dollars appear nowhere in the L1-L6 backlog (todos.md); the token/usage subsystem shows tokens, not dollars, so reinforces is genuinely empty and this extends the shipped observability track. The strongest counter — README.md:329 deliberately states usage is shown in tokens NOT dollars because subscription cost is flat and gateway/local pricing is unknown — constrains the feature but does not negate it: that exact rationale is satisfied by the judge's own guards (opt-in/off by default, operator-supplied per-provider rates covering the unknown-pricing gap, official plan-window % stays primary, $ clearly labelled an approximation). That reshapes it into the thin form ruled, rather than refuting it. This is textbook ADAPT (not ADD: BlockCost/cost()/the registry must NOT be imported). Ruling holds.

#### Loop-detection watchdog (escalate on repetition)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces watchdog
- **For:** Shepherd already ships a stuck-session watchdog (cc-core.lua M.isHung + trackProgress/applyProgress, Feature 8) but it detects stalls only via ONE signal: the transcript stops growing. AutoGPT's watchdog encodes a genuinely complementary heuristic Shepherd is blind to — "the agent keeps acting but repeats the same action." A session stuck in a tight tool loop (re-running the same Bash/Read/Edit over and over) keeps APPENDING to the transcript, so the byte-growth timer never trips and Shepherd's current watchdog stays silent on a real wedge. Adding repetition as a second stuck-detector would catch loops the size-growth check misses. The escalation half maps cleanly onto primitives Shepherd already has: nudge-paste (handleAction "nudge"), live /model switch (the FAST->SMART analog is exactly Shepherd's existing model-bump), and flag-for-human (purple ring + escalation sound/push + a ledgered event). It plugs straight into the established escalation.hung config block and the hung ledger type with no new subsystem.
- **Against:** The headline mechanism is a textbook AutoGPT double-mismatch and DONT_ADD on its own: WatchdogComponent.after_parse reads event_history.episodes[cursor-1].action.use_tool.name/arguments, calls event_history.rewind() to delete an execution episode, flips config.big_brain mid-cycle, and raises ComponentSystemError to re-run the prior pipeline. All four need the in-process executor and a structured per-cycle action record (tool name + args) that Shepherd does not have and cannot get — Claude Code owns the loop, Shepherd only watches ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json and a transcript byte count. Shepherd cannot rewind a turn, cannot retroactively re-run with a smarter model, and does not parse the agent's proposed tool calls, so "same command+args twice" is not even observable from its status feed (it would need transcript-tail parsing it doesn't do today). And the value Shepherd already extracts — "this session is wedged, flag the human" — is largely covered: the marginal gain is one extra detection heuristic, not a missing capability, so it risks being noise against an already-shipped watchdog.
- **Ruling:** Split the candidate exactly as the prompt's "judge the operator pattern, not the plumbing" rule demands. MECHANISM (rewind episode + swap model mid-loop via after_parse) = pure double-mismatch: it lives inside the executor, reads structured episode/action-history state, and mutates execution — Shepherd has no executor, no episode store, no after-parse hook, and its /model switch only affects the NEXT turn, not a retroactive re-think. That half is DONT_ADD, as the candidate itself concedes. CONCEPT (detect a stuck/looping session and escalate) is not new to Shepherd — it is precisely Feature 8, the stuck-session watchdog. So this is not a new system; it ADAPTs/refines an existing one, which the rubric says to score ADAPT with reinforces set. The one transferable, non-redundant nugget is the DETECTION HEURISTIC: AutoGPT keys on action repetition, Shepherd keys on transcript-byte stagnation, and these catch different failure modes (a chatty loop defeats the byte check). Surfacing repetition would require Shepherd to peek at the transcript tail for repeated tool invocations (it already reads transcript bytes for the activity peek, so a bounded tail-scan is plausible but new work). The escalation actions are entirely existing primitives, so no scope is violated. Net: real underlying need, must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, extends a feature Shepherd already has -> ADAPT, reinforces the watchdog.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing Feature 8 watchdog, not a parallel system. Add a second pure detector beside M.isHung in cc-core.lua, e.g. M.isLooping(item, recentActions, now, thresholdN): fed a small ring buffer of the last N observed tool invocations parsed from the transcript tail (the activity peek already reads transcript bytes — extend it to extract the trailing tool-call lines), it returns true when the same command+args repeats >= N times consecutively. Wire it in claude-dashboard.lua right where isHung is checked (~line 5553): on a detected loop set it.looping (a new tile decoration distinct from the ⏳ hung purple ring), nag once per loop episode reusing the escalation sound/push prefs, and ledger a new type (e.g. "loop", added to NOTIFY_TYPES alongside hung). For escalation actions, reuse existing primitives only: optionally auto-nudge ("you seem to be repeating <tool> — try a different approach") via handleAction "nudge", and surface a one-click "/model bump" suggestion (the local FAST->SMART analog) rather than performing any rewind. Config goes under the existing escalation block as escalation.loop { enabled=false, repeats=N }, OFF by default like hung. No executor, no rewind, no mid-loop model swap — detection + human/nudge escalation only.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds. Split the candidate as the rubric demands. MECHANISM (rewind one episode + swap FAST_LLM->SMART_LLM mid-loop via after_parse) is a clean double-mismatch: it needs AutoGPT's executor, an episode/action-history store, and a retroactive re-think Shepherd cannot do (its /model only affects the NEXT turn). That half is DONT_ADD — and both the candidate and the prior judge already concede it, so "the mechanism needs the executor" refutes nothing the ruling didn't already exclude. The RETAINED slice (detect a looping session, escalate via existing primitives) survives every downgrade lever: (a) No executor needed. The detector reads the transcript tail that claude-dashboard.lua:5410 ALREADY reads every working tick (ACTIVITY_BYTES=64KB) and already JSON-decodes via transcriptSnippet/transcriptError/lastUsage; the `tail` variable is in scope exactly where isHung is checked (line 5553). Extracting trailing tool_use {name,input} from an already-read, already-decoded tail is bounded new code, not new infrastructure. (b) No server/multi-user/marketplace/OAuth — irrelevant here. (c) NOT fully redundant — the decisive point. M.isHung (cc-core.lua:2375) fires ONLY on byte-stagnation (transcript not growing). A chatty loop — same command+args repeated while each iteration emits fresh output — keeps the transcript growing, so isHung returns false. The action-repetition heuristic provably catches a failure mode the existing watchdog misses. I confirmed cc-core has NO existing tool_use-name parser (only text/api_error/usage parsers), so this nugget is real and non-redundant. (d) The mechanism half is executor plumbing (excluded); the concept half is an operator pattern, not plumbing. What my investigation does establish is that this is an EXTENSION, not a new system: it reinforces Feature 8 (the stuck-session watchdog: isHung/trackProgress/applyProgress + escalation.hung, with a 'hung' ledger type and ⏳ decoration already present at cc-core.lua:660), and its escalation actions are already queued in L6 (cc-rules.json lists trigger kind 'hung' + processor 'nudge' at todos.md:550). So it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, adds a second pure detector beside isHung fed by a small ring buffer of trailing tool calls, and escalates only via existing nudge/notify/ledger primitives — no rewind, no mid-loop model swap. Real underlying need + reshaped onto existing primitives + extends a feature Shepherd already has = ADAPT, reinforces the watchdog. Cannot be refuted to DONT_ADD.

### Reinforces L1  (28)

#### Block/capability registry with auto-discovery + structural validation  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The management half is a real, transferable operator pattern that Shepherd already does in miniature and has queued to grow. AutoGPT's load_all_blocks() is, stripped of execution concerns, just: scan a source for definitions, validate each one at load (uniqueness, naming convention, required fields present and well-typed), dedupe by id, cache, and expose get(id)/getAll() lookups. Shepherd's presetList/presetPush (cc-core.lua ~3658-3705) ALREADY do exactly this for spawn presets — iterate records, validate each (name nonblank, folder absolute), replace-in-place by name (dedupe), cap the list. L1 explicitly proposes agentList/agentPush/agentRemove mirroring that, loading cc-agents.json + cc-mcp.json the same way, which is the user's #1 stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory). The candidate's distinctive value-add over what L1 already specs is the validate-EACH-at-load + SURFACE-load-errors discipline: today presetList silently drops malformed records, but an authoring front-end for saved agents wants per-entry load errors shown (bad id, dup id, missing required field). Classic Forge's @command decorator and skill_directory discovery (deriving name/description/JSONSchema from definitions found in directories) is the direct prior art for L1's FX.listSkills enumerator that derives name/description//command from SKILL.md frontmatter. So the pattern plugs straight into L1 and hardens it.
- **Against:** The feature as authored is overwhelmingly the graph/block-executor's catalog, not a library. load_all_blocks() rglob-IMPORTS every Python module and INSTANTIATES Block subclasses via a Pydantic __pydantic_init_subclass__ hook; the validations (every field an annotated SchemaField, error output a string, bool fields defaulted) exist to guarantee the DAG executor can wire blocks together, and the partitioned lookups (get_webhook_block_ids/get_io_block_ids/get_human_in_the_loop_block_ids) exist purely to feed graph execution. Shepherd imports no block code, has no Block class hierarchy, runs no graph — its agents are declarative JSON records, not importable code subclasses, so the entire auto-discovery-by-import-and-instantiate mechanism is inapplicable double-mismatch. Taken literally this is DONT_ADD; the only thing salvageable is a generic load-validate-dedupe idea Shepherd already has, so one could argue it adds nothing beyond L1.
- **Ruling:** Split the candidate cleanly. The run()/graph-execution half — rglob module import, Block-subclass instantiation, SchemaField/error-string/bool-default validation, and the webhook/io/human-in-the-loop block-id partitions that feed the executor — is pure AutoGPT double-mismatch and DONT_ADD: Shepherd executes no graph and its agents are JSON, not code subclasses. The management half — scan a directory of declarative definitions, validate each at load, dedupe by id, cache, expose lookups, and surface load errors — is a genuine operator pattern that Shepherd already implements for presets and has queued to generalize as L1's cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json registries. The candidate is therefore not new; it reinforces and slightly enriches L1 with two concrete disciplines: (1) validate-each-at-load with per-entry error surfacing (today presetList silently drops bad records), and (2) Forge-style decorator/directory introspection that derives a card's name/description/command from the definition itself — which L1 already half-captures in the FX.listSkills frontmatter enumerator. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence because the prompt's own mapping and the existing presetList code agree precisely.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's registry loaders. Concretely: (1) Generalize the presetList validate-and-dedupe pattern (cc-core.lua ~3658) into agentList for cc-agents.json — validate each profile at load (required: name nonblank + folder absolute; dedupe by name/id; cap), but UNLIKE presetList, collect rejected entries with a reason rather than silently dropping, returning {valid[], errors[]} so the authoring front-end can show per-entry load errors. (2) Apply the same loader shape to cc-mcp.json (the MCP server registry already specced in L1) and to FX.listSkills over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md — the latter is the Forge @command/skill-directory analog: derive name/description//command from frontmatter at load, skip README.md, dedupe by name. (3) Keep it pure cc-core (zero hs.*) + a thin FX file-read, unit-tested like presetList. Do NOT build: any import-and-instantiate of code, any Block/SchemaField type system, or the webhook/io/human-in-the-loop partition (Shepherd's gate already owns human-in-the-loop; routing v1/L4 own dispatch). Caching: a 1h TTL is unnecessary — Shepherd already reloads operator JSON on the ~1s refresh tick; load-on-change is sufficient.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the judge's ADAPT (reinforces L1, high) holds. The candidate splits cleanly and the executor half is already correctly excluded — rglob module import, Block-subclass instantiation, the SchemaField/error-string/bool-default type system, and the webhook/io/human-in-the-loop block-id partitions are pure AutoGPT graph-executor (mismatch a): Shepherd runs no graph and its agents are JSON, not code subclasses. No hidden executor dependency leaks into the management half, which operates on declarative JSON exactly as the shipped code does. No server/marketplace/OAuth (b) or DB plumbing (d) survives into that half. The management half — scan a directory of declarative definitions, validate each at load, dedupe by id, cache, expose lookups, surface load errors — is exactly the operator pattern Shepherd ALREADY implements and has queued to generalize. Verified in code: presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) scans the records, validates each (name nonblank, folder absolute), normalizes, and silently drops bad ones. The L1 enrichments in todos.md name every discipline the judge cites, by reference to that very line: (1) validate-each-at-load + per-entry error surfacing — todos.md:323-326 "generalize presetList's per-entry skip (~3658) to agentList/mcpList... keep the valid, drop only the bad... RETURN the dropped count+names... replaces today's silent drop"; (2) directory/decorator introspection deriving a card's name/description/command from the definition — todos.md:266-267 + 307-310 (FX.listSkills, dual-shape, derives /<name>+description from frontmatter, skips README); (3) dedupe/cap/validate CRUD — todos.md:254-255 (agentList mirroring presetPush/presetList); (4) structural cross-ref validation that refuses on bad refs — todos.md:290-294. So the feature is not new — it reinforces and slightly enriches L1 (cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json / skills enumerator). It is not ADD: every mechanism already exists as a primitive (presetList) or is an enumerated L1 enrichment, and even the candidate's 1h cache is rightly dropped (Shepherd reloads operator JSON on the ~1s refresh tick; context.md:22). Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence — the candidate mapping, the existing presetList code, and the L1 todos all agree.

#### Categories / tagging taxonomy + browse-by-category  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator pattern under the taxonomy — "organize a catalog of saved things and browse/filter it by tag with per-tag counts" — is exactly the gap Shepherd's #1 stated priority opens up: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." Once L1 turns spawn presets into a saved-agent registry (cc-agents.json) and L3 versions task templates, that directory will grow past a flat list, and a tag/browse axis is the natural way to make the spawn picker navigable ("show me my refactor agents," "my review templates"). Shepherd already proves the primitive works: session GROUPS are precisely a free-text taxonomy over live sessions (applyGroups/setGroup keyed by projectKey, groupNames returns the distinct sorted set, tile search already indexes .group, and there are group-scoped bulk actions). Extending the same shape to the saved-profile/template registries is a small, idiomatic move — a tags[] field on a profile, a pure tagsWithCounts derivation mirroring groupNames, and a browse-by-tag affordance in the New Session modal. AutoGPT's library-of-saved-agents-with-categories is genuine prior art for the registry the user asked for, and the per-tag count + N-samples idea (GET /builder/categories) is a nice scannability detail to copy into the picker.
- **Against:** As shipped, the feature is a textbook AutoGPT double mismatch. The Builder half — an 18-value BlockCategory enum where every executor block is tagged and GET /builder/categories returns counts + sample blocks — has zero referent in Shepherd: there are no blocks, no block catalog, no graph builder to organize. The Marketplace half — agents tagged with free-text categories, catalog filterable by category, Creator.top_categories computed by UNNEST aggregation over listing versions in a Prisma store — is multi-user marketplace serving with a server-side DB, which Shepherd structurally cannot and will not host (no network server, no multi-user, single-user local). And the "real" residue is thin: Shepherd already HAS a free-text taxonomy (session GROUPS) and relabel, and the tile grid is already a browsable board. A standalone "categories" system risks being a parallel taxonomy next to GROUPS, or premature org-tooling over a registry (L1) that doesn't exist yet. There's nothing to browse-by-tag until L1/L3 ship; building the tagging axis now would be speculative.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch and split the candidate. (1) Builder enum + GET /builder/categories + per-category sample blocks = DONT_ADD on its own: it organizes the block catalog of the in-process graph executor, which Shepherd has none of. (2) Marketplace free-text categories + catalog filter + Creator.top_categories UNNEST = DONT_ADD plumbing: it's multi-user store serving over a DB Shepherd can't host. What survives both filters is one operator pattern: tag-and-browse a catalog of saved artifacts. That pattern is real, it's already present for live sessions (GROUPS: applyGroups/groupNames/setGroup at cc-core.lua ~341-380, searchable via the .group field, with group-scoped bulk actions), and the user's top-stated need (a saved-agents/skills/MCP directory) makes it valuable for the SAVED side too. #### Provider SDK + auto-registration (declarative auth/credentials/webhook/cost config + pluggable OAuth handler registry)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The strongest FOR is the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory") plus the one genuinely transferable grain: AutoGPT's ProviderBuilder is a clean, declarative, drop-in-extensible registry pattern where each named entry self-describes its metadata — which env vars it needs, its supported auth types, a one-line human description — and new providers register by dropping in a definition rather than editing central code. That metadata-rich, extensible-by-drop-in shape is the right ergonomic for Shepherd's saved-agents/MCP directories: a requiredEnv/supportedAuthTypes/description field set on registry records makes the New-Session preflight smarter (warn before spawn when a profile's named env var is missing) and the picker more scannable. The named-handler-resolved-by-name idea also corroborates L1's config-by-reference resolver (profile stores NAMES; one pure resolver dereferences them at spawn).
- **Against:** The strongest AGAINST is a clean double mismatch on the bulk of the feature. The reason this SDK exists is to (a) store OAuth credentials and (b) feed them, via a global mutable AutoRegistry that monkey-patches webhook-manager loading and credential-store getters at import time, into an in-process block executor that runs agent graphs and ingests webhooks — for a multi-user web platform with per-provider cost sync (sync_all_provider_costs) and billing. Shepherd structurally hosts none of that: NO secrets storage (hard scope limit — it resolves $ENV via the spawned login shell and never sees a key), NO network server / webhook ingress, NO block/graph executor for credentials to flow into, NO billing/cost ledger to sync, NO multi-user. BaseOAuthHandler (login URL, code exchange, refresh, revoke, default scopes) is meaningless without a credential store and an executor making the API calls. And the registry sliver isn't even new: Shepherd ALREADY ships a `providers` registry (named non-secret env bundles; authTokenEnv names the var the login shell expands; secrets never stored — cc-core.lua ~2618/2714), and L1 already plans `cc-mcp.json` "modeled on the providers registry" with requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}], a config-by-ref resolver, a per-registry known-fields allowlist for drop-in extensibility, and fail-safe per-entry loading. So there is essentially nothing left to build that isn't redundant or queued.
- **Ruling:** Two halves. The OAuth-handler / credential-store / webhook-manager / cost-sync / global-import-time-monkey-patching-AutoRegistry half is textbook double mismatch (credential storage + in-process executor plumbing + multi-user platform) — DONT_ADD by every structural limit in context.md. The candidate's own assessment agrees: "MOSTLY DONT_ADD (reinforces existing Providers + L1)." The transferable grain is only the declarative, metadata-bearing, drop-in-extensible registry SHAPE (named provider/handler set carrying: which env vars, supported auth types, one-line description). But Shepherd already HAS that pattern in its live `providers` registry (authTokenEnv = env-var NAME, expanded by the spawned login shell, never stored), and L1 already generalizes it to cc-mcp.json/agent profiles with requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] + a known-fields allowlist (drop-in extensibility) + a config-by-ref name resolver + fail-safe per-entry loading. Net verdict: ADAPT-by-reinforcement of L1 — no parallel system, no new primitive; the only non-mismatched idea is already in the backlog. Marking ADD would re-add an existing registry; marking pure DONT_ADD would ignore the legitimate (already-queued) reinforcement signal. Effort is S because nothing new is built — at most it nudges L1's record schema to also carry supportedAuthTypes/description fields, which the existing known-fields allowlist already accommodates.
- **How (Shepherd):** Maps onto L1's registry record schema. AutoGPT's per-provider declarative metadata → enrich L1 profile + cc-mcp.json records (already "modeled on the providers registry") with the already-planned requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] plus optional supportedAuthTypes/one-line description fields, accommodated by L1's per-registry known-fields allowlist (drop-in extensibility = add a JSON record, no central code edit). AutoGPT's named-handler-resolved-by-name → L1's config-by-reference resolver (extends providerById cc-core.lua:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537). AutoGPT's "register at import" → Shepherd's pure agentList/mcpList loaders with fail-safe per-entry skip + dropped-count return. Credentials stay as today's authTokenEnv NAME expanded by loginShellWrap (cc-core.lua ~2714/2754) — never stored. Explicitly DROP: BaseOAuthHandler login/exchange/refresh/revoke, the credential store, webhook-manager loading, AutoRegistry monkey-patching, sync_all_provider_costs.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT(reinforces L1) ruling is correct, and I verified its mechanics in the actual code. The feature splits cleanly. The large half (BaseOAuthHandler login/exchange/refresh/revoke, the credential store, webhook-manager loading, import-time monkey-patching AutoRegistry, sync_all_provider_costs) is the AutoGPT double mismatch: it stores OAuth CREDENTIALS (Shepherd stores no secrets — cc-core.lua:2620-2622 confirms a profile only NAMES an env var the spawned login shell expands; cc-config.json holds no key and Shepherd's process never sees one) and it is in-process EXECUTOR/PLATFORM plumbing (webhook managers, a global registry that block execution dereferences, cost-sync). That half is DONT_ADD by the secrets-storage and no-executor scope limits, and the candidate's own assessment already concedes "MOSTLY DONT_ADD." So ADD is impossible — most of it is dropped.\n\nBut a pure DONT_ADD is also wrong, which is why the refutation can't push it down. The only non-mismatched grain — a declarative, metadata-bearing, drop-in-extensible registry SHAPE (a named provider/handler record carrying which env vars it needs, supported auth types, a one-line description) — is not a new primitive and not noise: Shepherd already has the live `providers` registry (cc-core.lua:2618-2640, resolved by providerById:2632) AND L1 already generalizes exactly this shape. I verified in todos.md: requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] on profiles + cc-mcp.json records with a NAME-only missingEnv check (todos.md:287-289), a per-registry known-fields allowlist for drop-in optional fields like supportedAuthTypes/description (todos.md:293), a config-by-reference name resolver extending providerById/resolveGateTools (todos.md:295-298), and fail-safe per-entry agentList/mcpList loaders that map AutoGPT's register-at-import (todos.md:323-326). The context's own rule — when an AutoGPT feature matches, verdict ADAPT and set reinforces to the Lx rather than proposing a parallel system — applies directly; discarding this as DONT_ADD would ignore a legitimate, already-queued reinforcement signal.\n\nNeither push lands: ADD re-adds an existing/queued registry; DONT_ADD throws away a real backlog match. ADAPT(reinforces L1), effort S (at most nudges L1's already-allowlisted record schema to also carry supportedAuthTypes/description; builds no new system), is the only honest verdict.

#### Availability gating: items hidden/blocked when prerequisites unmet  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The core pattern — filter the catalog by *capability, not just static presence*, so an operator never sees (or is blocked from spawning) something that can't actually run — is directly useful and 100% local/deterministic. Shepherd's spawn picker today lists presets/agent profiles statically; a profile that needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, a folder that no longer exists, or an MCP server whose config is missing will spawn into an instant error or a silently broken session. Greying-out / flagging the unsatisfiable profile at spawn time turns a wasted spawn + an error tile into a pre-flight warning — pure value-add for a watch/steer/spawn console. Critically, all three AutoGPT signals reduce to checks Shepherd can do *without a server, secrets store, or executor*: env-var NAME presence (via the spawned login shell, never the value), local path/file existence (folder, MCP-config file, ~/.claude/skills), and an operator-authored denylist (picker-scope hide flag). The user's stated priority is exactly a directory of saved agents/skills/MCPs — and "don't let me launch a broken one" is the natural companion to that registry. It also generalizes the existing per-entry fail-safe skip in presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) and slots cleanly beside the already-queued core.missingEnv + pre-flight validator.
- **Against:** Most of AutoGPT's specific gating value is bound to the double mismatch and evaporates here. is_block_auth_configured() exists to keep OAuth-credentialed BLOCKS out of a multi-user catalog — Shepherd has no OAuth credential store and no blocks, so that exact mechanism is structurally inapplicable. The webhook self-disable (block off when platform_base_url is unset) is meaningless without a platform/server. COPILOT_EXCLUDED_BLOCK_TYPES/IDS is marketplace/copilot curation against an in-process block registry Shepherd doesn't have. So this is NOT "add it as-is" — it is at most a thin reshaping. And the reshaped version is almost entirely already on the roadmap: L1 already specifies requiredEnv[], core.missingEnv NAME-presence checks via loginShellWrap, a New-Session preflight warning + tile badge, a pre-flight validator that refuses spawn with bad cross-refs, and a picker-scope "hide from picker" toggle living in operator JSON. There is a real risk this candidate is redundant with L1 rather than additive — the only genuinely new sliver is unifying env + path-existence + denylist into one "is this profile launchable right now?" availability predicate that drives picker greyout, which is a small extension, not a feature.
- **Ruling:** DOUBLE MISMATCH: the implementation (block-catalog serializer, OAuth-configured-block filter, webhook/platform gating, copilot/marketplace denylists) is pure executor+platform plumbing Shepherd cannot and should not host. But the OPERATOR PATTERN underneath — "don't surface a library item the operator can't actually run; tell them why" — is real and transfers cleanly to local, deterministic checks. That is the textbook ADAPT shape. It is not ADD because nothing of the AutoGPT mechanism survives literally; it is not DONT_ADD because the need is genuine, local, and the user explicitly wants a saved-agents/skills/MCP directory where launchability matters. It reinforces L1: requiredEnv[] + core.missingEnv + the pre-flight validator + the picker-scope hide flag already cover ~80% of this; the candidate's marginal contribution is folding env-presence + local path existence + operator denylist into a single availability predicate that drives spawn-picker greyout/block, so set reinforces=L1 rather than spawning a parallel system. Honor the hard limits: NAME-presence only via the login shell (never read secret values), existence checks only, denylist is picker-scope (can't disable a live session's native auto-load). Effort is small because the validator/resolver families (presetList:3658, resolveGateTools:2537, providerById:2632) and the planned missingEnv already exist or are scoped; this is an aggregating predicate + a UI state on tiles/picker.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure cc-core predicate, e.g. core.profileAvailability(profile, {shellEnv, folderExists, mcpConfigPresent, skillsRoot, denylist}) -> {ok, reasons[]}, that aggregates: (1) missingEnv NAME-presence against the spawned login-shell env (loginShellWrap, cc-core.lua:2754 — names only, never values); (2) local existence of profile.folder, attached mcpServers[] config, and skills[]/plugins[] roots (reuse the safe-path/[A-Za-z0-9._-] sanitizers ~1950/2451/2796); (3) an operator-authored picker-scope denylist (hide/grey flag in cc-agents.json/operator JSON — explicitly labeled 'picker-scope only, cannot disable a live session'). Wire this into the L1 spawn-from-agent picker (alongside presetList:3658 / the New-Session preflight) so unsatisfiable profiles render greyed with the reasons[] tooltip and the pre-flight validator (todos.md L1 ~290) refuses spawn with the aggregated list before emitting any flag. Optional off-by-default tile badge sibling to stale/hung. NO OAuth/credential gating, NO platform_base_url/webhook self-disable, NO marketplace/copilot serialization — those are the parts that stay dropped.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT-reinforces-L1 ruling holds. DOUBLE-MISMATCH confirmed, so this is NOT ADD. Nothing of the AutoGPT mechanism survives literally: is_block_auth_configured() (drop OAuth-only blocks whose client-id/secret env vars are absent) is credential-store plumbing Shepherd has no business hosting (NO secrets storage); the webhook-self-disable-on-unset-platform_base_url is server/multi-user platform plumbing (NO network server); the per-block-type/per-id denylists and copilot/marketplace serialization are executor+marketplace plumbing for a graph/block engine Shepherd does not run. All of that stays DROPPED, exactly as the judge said. Tried to push to DONT_ADD via redundancy — and the evidence is actually STRONGER than the judge stated, but it cuts the other way under the rubric. Every piece the judge called the candidate's 'marginal contribution' is already written into the QUEUED L1 plan in todos.md, not merely 80%-covered: - env-presence (NAME-only, never values, via loginShellWrap): todos.md:287-289 — requiredEnv[{name,description,required,default}] + pure core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) → New-Session preflight warning + optional tile badge. cc-core.lua:2754 loginShellWrap already exists. - local path existence + bad cross-ref: todos.md:284-285 (knowledge[] = validated local paths), 290-294 (pre-flight validator family returns aggregated errors incl. unknown-field/missing-required/bad-cross-ref where policyBundle/mcpServers[]/skills[] MUST resolve, and REFUSES spawn/attach with the full list before emitting any flag), 302-303 (safe-path hardening: skill/MCP/plugin name resolvers validate [A-Za-z0-9._-] and reject any name resolving outside its root). - operator denylist / picker greyout: todos.md:321-322 — 'Any "hide from picker" flag lives in operator JSON, labeled picker-scope only (can't disable native auto-load on a live session).' This is the candidate's denylist hard-limit, already authored. - The judge's proposed core.profileAvailability(...) -> {ok, reasons[]} is just an AGGREGATION of these existing L1 pieces feeding the same preflight-refuse + greyed-picker UX (the New-Session preflight at L1 ~290 already refuses spawn with the aggregated list). It is a thin aggregating predicate over work that is already specified, not a new system. But full redundancy with QUEUED work does NOT yield DONT_ADD under the stated protocol — the rubric is explicit: 'if an AutoGPT feature matches [queued work], verdict ADAPT and set reinforces to the Lx rather than proposing a parallel system.' DONT_ADD would require the need to be absent or to depend on the graph/block executor / credential store / server / marketplace. None holds: the need (don't surface a library item the operator can't run; tell them why) is genuine, local, deterministic, and directly serves the user's #1 stated priority (a saved-agents/skills/MCP directory where launchability matters). So the operator pattern transfers cleanly to local checks, the implementation plumbing stays dropped, and it reinforces L1 (not a parallel system). That is the textbook ADAPT shape — the ruling stands. Relevant evidence: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1 section lines 247-334, esp. 287-289/290-294/302-303/321-322) and /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua:2754 (loginShellWrap), :2632 (providerById), :2537 (resolveGateTools), :3640-3722 (presetList/presetPush family the predicate mirrors).

#### Canonical serialized catalog-item descriptor (Block info DTO + cached list endpoint)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable kernel is pure data-shaping, not graph execution: "one canonical serializable descriptor per catalog item + a cached list that feeds the picker UI." Shepherd ALREADY does exactly this for spawn presets — core.presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) returns a sanitized, JSON-able array {name, folder, editor, permMode, provider} that the webview renders into the New-Session modal, and presetState serializes via json.encode (~3044). The candidate is the same pattern, richer. Queued L1 already commits to the descriptor superset {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, policyBundle, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], requiredEnv[], versions[]} as the agent-profile DTO; AutoGPT's BlockInfo (id, name, schemas, costs, description, categories, contributors, uiType) is strong prior art for what a "canonical catalog-item descriptor" should carry and for the discipline of ONE stable serializer per item that the palette consumes. Adopting two concrete habits from it would make L1 better: (1) a single canonical to_dict/toJson per profile (not ad-hoc field copies scattered across presetList/agentList/render) so the picker, pre-flight validator, and L5 export all read one shape; (2) caching the assembled catalog so the ~1s refresh loop never re-derives the full profile/skill/MCP list each tick (matching AutoGPT's 1h cache intent, here keyed by file mtime).
