The cached-list need is genuine: L1's skills enumerator globs ~/.claude/skills, MCP registry reads cc-mcp.json, profiles read cc-agents.json — assembling that catalog every tick is wasteful, so a cached, mtime-invalidated "catalog descriptor" feeding the picker is a real win that fits the user's #1 stated interest (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory, rendered into a palette).
- **Against:** Most of the literal feature is double-mismatched and must be dropped, not copied. (a) inputSchema/outputSchema/staticOutput/uiType/costs describe an EXECUTABLE graph node — they exist so the engine can validate node wiring and the executor can run the block; Shepherd runs no graph and executes no blocks, so a profile/skill has no input/output port schema to serialize. (b) GET /blocks is an HTTP route on a multi-user FastAPI backend serving a Next.js frontend; Shepherd has NO network server (hard scope limit) — the "endpoint" must collapse to a local pure function returning JSON to an embedded webview, which is just plumbing it already has, so there's no endpoint to "add." (c) The ~226-item public catalog is marketplace/discovery-shaped; L1 explicitly DROPs marketplace.json / /discover / /registry.json. (d) optimized_description is an LLM-rewritten summary — it requires an API call, which Shepherd structurally cannot make (no proxying, no API calls per context.md); that sub-feature is DONT_ADD/defer outright. So if you weigh the feature as shipped (block schemas + cached HTTP catalog + AI-rewrite), almost none of it lands; only the abstract "canonical descriptor + cached picker list" survives, and Shepherd already has the primitive (presetList) and the queued plan (L1) — risking a redundant parallel "catalog" system if taken too literally.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch field-by-field. The value of BlockInfo and GET /blocks splits cleanly: the OPERATOR pattern (a stable, canonical serializer per catalog item + a cached list that populates a picker) is exactly Shepherd's presetList→webview pattern and exactly what L1 generalizes; the EXECUTION/SERVER plumbing (port schemas for runnable nodes, an HTTP route, a multi-user public catalog, an LLM-rewritten field) is all non-transferable. That asymmetry is the definition of ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (the candidate's own SHEPHERD note already says ADAPT/L1, and the codebase confirms the mapping: presetList at cc-core.lua:3658, json.encode serialization at ~3044, L1's DTO + config-by-ref resolver + pre-flight validator at todos.md:247-334). It also touches L5 (export/meta.json) but L1 is the primary home. Concrete value-add beyond what L1 already says: bless ONE canonical per-profile toJson serializer reused by picker + validator + export, and cache the assembled catalog by file mtime rather than re-deriving it on the tick. The optimized_description is the one piece to defer/drop (no API calls). Not DONT_ADD because the underlying need (canonical descriptor + cached picker feed) is real and roadmapped; not ADD because the shipped form is server/executor/marketplace/LLM-bound and must be reshaped, not built as-is. Category is Library & Organization (the saved-agents/skills directory + its picker), not Blocks. Effort is S — it's a serialization+caching refinement folded into L1's already-planned registry work, not a new subsystem.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's agent-profile registry as the canonical-descriptor + cached-catalog discipline. (1) Define ONE pure serializer core.agentToDescriptor(profile) → stable JSON record {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, role/goal/backstory, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], knowledge[], requiredEnv[], versions[]} — the L1 analog of BlockInfo's to_dict/get_info — and route the New-Session picker, the pre-flight validator, and L5 export through it (single shape, sibling to presetList at cc-core.lua:3658). (2) core.agentCatalog(state) assembles {profiles, skills (from ~/.claude/skills glob), mcpServers (cc-mcp.json)} into one descriptor array for the webview; cache it keyed by source-file mtime (cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json / skills dir) so the ~1s refresh loop and the modal don't re-derive it — AutoGPT's cached /blocks intent, realized as a local cached pure derivation, NOT an HTTP route. Serialize via the existing json.encode (~3044) into the embedded webview; no server. (3) DROP inputSchema/outputSchema/staticOutput/uiType/costs (no executable ports), the HTTP endpoint, the public-catalog/marketplace shape, and optimized_description (defer — no LLM call; if ever wanted, it's an off-by-default session-authored summary written to the profile's memoryFile per L1's /remember plan, not an API call by Shepherd).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling stands. I tried each downgrade lever against the actual codebase and none breaks it. (a) Executor dependency? The descriptor's transferable core — a stable canonical per-item serializer plus a cached list feeding a picker — carries NO graph/block execution. The execution-bound fields (inputSchema/outputSchema/staticOutput/uiType/costs are runnable-node ports) are correctly DROPPED by the judge, but their removal does not gut the pattern; it leaves a clean serialization+caching discipline. So (a) cannot force DONT_ADD. (b) Server/marketplace/OAuth? GET /blocks is an HTTP route serving a multi-user public catalog — genuine server plumbing — and the judge drops the endpoint and the marketplace/public-catalog shape, reshaping it into a LOCAL cached pure derivation feeding the embedded webview. The codebase confirms this exact prior art: claude-dashboard.lua:1704 already does hs.json.encode({ presets = core.presetList(pstate), ... }) to feed the webview with NO server route, and cc-core.lua:3658 presetList is precisely the safe-copy/stable-serializer pattern (drops invalid entries, emits a fixed-shape record). So (b) kills the HTTP/marketplace surface but not the underlying picker-feed pattern. (c) Redundant or already-queued? This is the strongest lever and it lands on ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. The need is squarely inside L1 (todos.md:247-334), which already specifies the agent-profile DTO {name,folder,provider,model,permMode,seedPrompt,policyBundle,skills[],mcpServers[],plugins[],requiredEnv[],versions[]}, a pre-flight validator family (sibling to presetList ~3590-3722), and a config-by-ref resolver. But L1 as written does NOT verbatim bless ONE canonical agentToDescriptor serializer reused by picker+validator+export, nor mtime-keyed catalog caching — both are real, non-trivial refinements, and mtime-cache prior art already exists (claude-dashboard.lua:5272/5282 caches the ledger by file mtime). So it's a reinforcement folded into already-queued L1, not a fully-redundant re-add and not a new parallel system. Could not push ADAPT->DONT_ADD because the transferable residue is non-zero and not duplicated word-for-word; could not push ADAPT->ADD because the shipped form is server/executor/marketplace-bound and must be reshaped. LLM piece: optimized_description (AI-rewritten field) requires an API call, which Shepherd never makes — the judge correctly defers/drops it. That single DONT_ADD sub-piece does not change the verdict for the descriptor+cache pattern. Net: double mismatch applied correctly — drop ports/HTTP/marketplace/LLM, keep the canonical-serializer + mtime-cached picker feed, home it in L1. Category Library and Organization, effort S. ADAPT holds.

#### Fork / duplicate with lineage (copy + provenance, strip secrets)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator NEED is real and fits Shepherd's local model exactly. The user's #1 stated priority is "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory" — i.e. the L1 Agent Profiles registry (cc-agents.json). In any such registry the very first thing an operator wants is "take this working profile and make a tweaked variant" without retyping provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle. Cloning is the cheapest way to author profile #2..N. It plugs into surface that already exists or is queued: presetPush/presetList (cc-core.lua ~3657-3722) is the exact pure-Lua CRUD pattern a profileFork helper mirrors (validate name, append "(copy)", replace-or-prepend, cap, zero hs.*); L1 already specifies an optional local-only versions[] field "reuse the session-lineage mindset; no remote object"; and Shepherd already ships session lineage (lineageByProject ~1061). Recording forkedFromName/forkedFromVersion on a clone is the profile-template analogue of the lineage Shepherd already renders for sessions — same mindset, new object class. Carrying the L2 policy bundle and L1 selections through the copy is trivial deep-copy of plain Lua tables, fully unit-testable in cc-core with no effects.
- **Against:** The bulk of AutoGPT's implementation is DONT_ADD plumbing, so the ADD-as-is value is near zero. fork_graph()+reassign_ids() exist to clone a DAG of executable BLOCKS and re-key every node/link UUID so the in-process executor can run the copy — Shepherd runs no graph and executes no blocks, so node/link cloning and UUID reassignment have no referent. stripped_for_export() removes stored OAuth credentials and webhook refs — Shepherd's hard scope forbids secrets storage; it persists only env-var NAMES (authTokenEnv/providerEnv, cc-core.lua ~2710) expanded by the spawned login shell, so there is literally nothing secret to strip (the feature's headline "strip secrets" is N/A here). The Prisma AgentGraph.forkedFromId/forkedFromVersion self-relation 'AgentGraphForks' and routes/agents.py fork_library_agent are multi-user-DB + per-user-library serving that Shepherd structurally cannot host. So a faithful copy would be over-engineering; only the thin "duplicate a saved profile + remember its parent" pattern survives, which is why this is ADAPT, not ADD — and it cannot ship before L1 exists to clone.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch decomposes cleanly: (engine side) the graph/node/link deep-copy + UUID reassignment is graph-executor plumbing with no referent in a watch/steer/spawn console = DONT_ADD; (platform side) the Prisma self-relation + fork_library_agent route + credential/webhook stripping are multi-user DB + OAuth-store serving Shepherd cannot host, and secret-stripping is N/A because Shepherd never stores secrets. What survives the mismatch is a pure MANAGEMENT/LIBRARY pattern: "duplicate a saved definition as a starting point, name it (copy), and remember what it was forked from." That maps 1:1 onto L1 Agent Profiles, which already queues an optional local versions[] field framed in the session-lineage mindset, and reuses the existing presetPush/presetList CRUD shape plus the shipped session-lineage concept. The user's saved-agents-in-a-directory priority weighs favorably and authoring-by-cloning is a core ergonomics win for that registry. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L1; do not stand up a parallel fork system. Effort S because it is one pure cc-core helper (deep-copy a profile table, dedupe/append "(copy)" to the name, stamp forkedFromName/Version, carry policyBundle + selections) plus a "Duplicate" button in the profile authoring UI — but it is gated on L1 landing first.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add to L1 (cc-agents.json registry): a pure cc-core helper, e.g. M.agentFork(state, name) -> newState, sibling to presetPush/presetList (~3657-3722). It deep-copies the named profile table, sets name = unique "<name> (copy)" (collision-dedupe like presetPush), prepends it, caps the list, and stamps forkedFromName + forkedFromVersion (the parent's versions[] head, per L1's local-only versions[] note) onto the clone. It carries through provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]/knowledge[]/requiredEnv[] and the attached L2 policyBundle by value. No credential handling is needed (Shepherd stores only env-var NAMES, never values), so the "strip secrets" step is a no-op/N/A. UI: a "Duplicate" action on each profile card in the L1 authoring front-end that opens the clone in the editor; show a small "forked from <name>" provenance line, reusing the session-lineage detail-row presentation (lineageSummary). DONT_ADD: graph/node/link cloning + UUID reassignment, the Prisma forkedFromId/AgentGraphForks self-relation, fork_library_agent route, and credential/webhook stripping.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 ruling holds. The double mismatch decomposes cleanly and I confirmed each leg against the code: (engine) deep-copy nodes/links + UUID reassignment is graph-executor plumbing — Shepherd has no graph (no nodes/links/agentFork-of-a-DAG anywhere; cc-core.lua is pure preset/lineage CRUD) → correctly DONT_ADD; (platform) the Prisma forkedFromId self-relation, the fork_library_agent route, and credential/webhook stripping serve a multi-user DB + OAuth store Shepherd cannot host, and secret-stripping is genuinely N/A — context.md/MEMORY confirm Shepherd stores only env-var NAMES, never values → DONT_ADD/no-op. What SURVIVES is a pure management/library pattern: "duplicate a saved definition as a starting point, name it (copy), remember what it was forked from." I tried to kill the survivor as redundant (error c) but it is not: L1 does not exist yet (no agentList/agentPush/cc-agents.json in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua — purely queued at todos.md:247) and its spec enumerates CRUD (push/list/remove) + a "Save as agent" authoring front-end but NOT authoring-by-cloning. Fork-as-starting-point (copy "backend-dev", retarget model → sibling profile) is a distinct, real registry ergonomic — different from versioning-in-place (L1's queued local-only versions[] at todos.md:258 tracks edits to ONE profile; a fork mints a sibling) and squarely serves the user's #1 priority "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory." So it is not plumbing and improves the local console. It is also not a parallel system or a standalone ADD: it's a sibling helper to presetPush (cc-core.lua:3673, same validate/dedupe/cap shape), it lands INSIDE L1, reuses the queued versions[] lineage concept, and the provenance row reuses the shipped lineageSummary presentation (cc-core.lua:1098). That is exactly ADAPT/reinforces L1. The refutation rubric says downgrade ADD->ADAPT only when it is an extension/reinforcement — this is already at ADAPT, the surviving kernel is real and non-redundant, so DONT_ADD is unwarranted. Carve-outs (graph/UUID clone, Prisma self-relation, fork route, secret-stripping) stay DONT_ADD. Gated on L1 landing first; effort S (one cc-core agentFork helper + a "Duplicate" button on the profile card).

#### Favorite / pin a saved agent + favorites view  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A pin/favorite flag plus a favorites-only filter is exactly the kind of cheap organizational affordance a fleet console wants, and it lands squarely on the user's #1 stated priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory." The L1 Agent Profiles registry (cc-agents.json, agentPush/agentList CRUD modeled on presetPush) is already queued; once you have a saved-agent directory, the most-used profiles need to float to the top, and a boolean isFavorite + a favorites view is the obvious, near-zero-cost way to do that. Critically, Shepherd ALREADY ships every primitive this needs: filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322, free-text token-AND filter, JS-mirrored) and the session-groups machinery (setGroup/applyGroups/groupNames, cc-core.lua:350-385) are an immutable, projectKey-keyed, JSON-persisted (cc-groups.json) tag system. A "favorite" is just a degenerate one-value tag: a pure boolean keyed by profile name (or projectKey for the "favorite a live tile" variant), persisted exactly like cc-groups.json/cc-labels.json, with the favorites filter being a one-line predicate added to filterTiles' JS twin and the registry list. AutoGPT's own impl confirms how small this is — a boolean column + PATCH + a separate query path (LibraryAgentFilter.IS_FAVOURITE). The private per-profile "how well did this preset work" rating/note is the genuinely adaptable kernel of store reviews (the multi-user review system itself is DONT_ADD) and it dovetails with L5's already-queued post-run self-summary callback, giving the registry a sort key beyond recency.
- **Against:** It is arguably redundant with what Shepherd already has: session groups can already cohort and filter tiles, and tile search already surfaces any subset by free text — an operator can make a "fav" group today and filter to it, so a dedicated favorite boolean is a convenience, not a capability. The flying-heart animation and a separate "favorites view" are pure cosmetic/marketplace-library polish carried over from a multi-user product where discovery across thousands of public agents matters; Shepherd's saved-agent set is small and single-user, so the curation payoff is marginal. The store-review kernel is the more dangerous half: a "rating" invites scope creep toward an outcome-ledger that Shepherd has deliberately avoided framing as authoritative (the L5 self-summary is explicitly "Shepherd typed a prompt," never a verdict). If L1 hasn't shipped yet, building favorites first is premature — there is nothing to favorite — and it risks spawning a parallel meta-store instead of one column on the profile record.
- **Ruling:** DOUBLE MISMATCH applied: AutoGPT's value here splits cleanly. The multi-user store review upsert (store/db.py:1084) depends on marketplace serving + user accounts — structurally impossible in Shepherd, correctly DONT_ADD. But LibraryAgent.isFavorite is pure library/management metadata: a boolean on a saved record plus a query filter, with NO graph execution, NO block code, NO OAuth, NO server. That is precisely the transferable "library agent = saved agent profile" management pattern the rubric says to judge. It maps onto a queued primitive (L1) and reuses shipped ones (setGroup/filterTiles), so it is an extension, not a new system — ADAPT, reinforces L1. It is not DONT_ADD (no plumbing dependency) and not ADD (must be reshaped onto Shepherd's projectKey-keyed JSON-tag/filter primitives rather than copied as a Prisma column + PATCH endpoint, and a chunk of the feature — flying-heart, separate favorites view, store reviews — drops). I weighted the user's saved-agents-directory priority favorably, which tips this from "redundant with groups" toward "worth a first-class pin on the L1 registry." The rating/note half rides L5's self-summary, not a new ledger.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add to L1's Agent Profiles registry (cc-agents.json): a `favorite` boolean on each profile record, toggled by a pure cc-core helper mirroring setGroup (immutable return, keyed by profile name) — e.g. agentSetFavorite(profiles, name, bool) beside agentPush/agentList. The "favorites-only" view is a one-predicate filter in the registry list and a flag added to filterTiles' searchable set (it already concatenates group at cc-core.lua:330; add a synthetic "fav" token when favorite) plus its JS twin. For the optional "favorite a LIVE tile" variant, reuse the exact cc-groups.json pattern: a projectKey-keyed boolean map (cc-favorites.json) applied like applyGroups, surfaced as a header filter chip alongside the existing group chips, and a tile star toggle next to relabel/set-group (handleAction already whitelists relabel/set-group for remote tiles at claude-dashboard.lua:2241 — add the favorite toggle there). Sort the registry/board favorite-first. The private per-profile rating/note maps onto L5's queued post-run self-summary: store an optional note/score field on the profile (local, never authoritative) as a secondary sort key. DROP entirely: the flying-heart animation (optional cosmetic, not load-bearing), any separate favorites *page* (a filter chip suffices), and the multi-user store review upsert.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. Both halves of the judge's double mismatch verify against actual AutoGPT source. (1) LibraryAgent.isFavorite (schema.prisma:551) is `isFavorite Boolean @default(false)` — a pure column whose only consumers are a query filter (list_favorite_library_agents, where isFavorite=True), a sort key, and an update field; NO graph/block execution, NO OAuth, NO server-run dependency. So the (a) executor-dependence DONT_ADD ground does not apply. (2) The store review create_store_review (store/db.py:1083) upserts a StoreListingReview keyed by (storeListingVersionId, reviewByUserId) — it structurally needs user accounts + the marketplace listing surface, impossible in Shepherd; the judge already DROPS it, so the (b) server/marketplace ground touches only the excised half. The surviving kernel (a private boolean + optional note/score on a saved profile, plus a favorites filter) is local/single-user/no-secrets. (c) Redundancy is the strongest challenge — groups (setGroup/applyGroups/groupNames), tile search (filterTiles), relabel, and the queued L1 registry already cohort and filter sessions — but a one-tap binary pin with favorite-first sort and a single 'fav' filter token is a distinct affordance from a typed cohort name, and the user's saved-agents-directory priority weights it favorably; it is an extension, not a duplicate. It is not (d) DB plumbing — it reshapes onto operator-data JSON, not a schema migration. And it is not ADD: it cannot be copied as a Prisma column + PATCH endpoint; it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's projectKey-keyed JSON + the existing filterTiles/setGroup primitives, with the flying-heart animation, the separate favorites page, and the store reviews all dropped — that reshape is the ADD->ADAPT downgrade the judge correctly made. Mapping primitives verified in-repo: filterTiles concatenates group into its haystack at cc-core.lua:330 (add a synthetic 'fav' token); setGroup is the immutable projectKey-keyed pattern at cc-core.lua:377 to mirror for agentSetFavorite; applyGroups at cc-core.lua:350; the remote-tile handleAction whitelist permits relabel/set-group at claude-dashboard.lua:2241 (add a favorite toggle there); L5's post-run self-summary (todos.md:534) is the correct home for the note/score half. L1's agentPush/agentList/cc-agents.json are confirmed queued-not-shipped (todos.md:254; absent from cc-core.lua), so the favorite is an extension of the queued registry rather than a new system. Accepted precedent exists: cline's model favorites already reinforce L1 (docs/feature-mining/cline.md:173-177) and cline's history favorite-pin reinforces L5 (cline.md:300-305), both reshaped onto the same projectKey-keyed persistence. ADAPT (high), reinforces L1, note/score rides L5.

#### Hide / archive / soft-delete saved agents (declutter, recoverable)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A saved-agent registry (L1, the user's #1 stated interest) is a list that grows monotonically — every saved profile sticks around forever, cluttering the New Session / "Spawn from agent" picker. The three-tier lifecycle the candidate ships is genuinely useful operator UX on that list: a "hide" flag to tidy a long picker, an "archive" state to retire a config without losing it, and "soft-delete with restore" so a mis-click never destroys a hand-tuned profile. The idempotent re-add (UniqueViolation -> update {isDeleted:false}) is exactly the right behavior — re-saving an agent you'd deleted should resurrect it, not duplicate it, and Shepherd's presetPush already does name-keyed replace-in-place (cc-core.lua:3686-3691), so it is one short step from "un-tombstone on re-add." This is cheap, pure cc-core state (booleans + a filter), composes with the existing filterTiles/applyGroups primitives, and the L1 spec ALREADY explicitly anticipates it: todos.md:321 says "Any 'hide from picker' flag lives in operator JSON, labeled picker-scope only." The run-history half also maps cleanly: Shepherd's audit ledger is precisely a "hide-not-erase" store, so dismissing a past session from a list while keeping the JSONL record is the documented pattern, not a new system.
- **Against:** The feature is small and arguably premature. Shepherd's existing preset registry has a hard CAP that drops the oldest entry (presetPush: while #out > cap do table.remove(out)) and a flat presetRemove — for a single-user local list of a handful of saved agents, "just delete it" plus the existing cap may be sufficient, and three lifecycle states (hidden/archived/deleted) is more machinery than the problem warrants until the list actually gets long. AutoGPT's heavy version of this (Prisma boolean columns + compound partial indexes for not-deleted-by-recency at scale, soft-deletes to preserve referential integrity across a multi-user DB) is pure database-at-scale plumbing Shepherd structurally does not have and should NOT copy — the index/UniqueViolation framing is a multi-tenant Postgres concern, not a JSON-file concern. And the run-history soft-delete piece is partly redundant: Shepherd already has ledger redact/export/purge and a notification-history list, so "soft-delete a history row" risks re-implementing what the audit overlay does. So the FOR case only holds for the registry-tidy slice, and only once L1 exists.
- **Ruling:** No double mismatch on the operator pattern: this is a LIBRARY/management feature (list lifecycle), not graph/block execution, not credential storage, not marketplace serving — exactly the management layer the brief says CAN transfer. It cleanly clears the FIT question. But it is not ADD because it is not a standalone capability that fits the watch/steer/spawn console on its own — it is a property of a list that does not exist yet. Its entire value is parasitic on the L1 Agent Profiles registry (the user's stated #1 priority), which is already queued and whose spec at todos.md:321 explicitly reserves a "hide from picker" flag. So it reinforces L1 rather than standing alone. Strip the AutoGPT DB plumbing (Prisma booleans, partial compound indexes, UniqueViolation semantics) — those are multi-user-Postgres-at-scale artifacts Shepherd cannot and should not host. Keep the OPERATOR pattern: hidden/archived state + a filter + un-tombstone-on-re-add, as pure cc-core fields on cc-agents.json records. The run-history soft-delete is already covered by the audit ledger's redact/purge + filterLedger, so that half is mostly redundant and need not be rebuilt. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L1.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's cc-agents.json profile schema as optional operator-JSON fields: add `hidden` (boolean, picker-scope tidy) and `archived` (boolean, retired-but-kept) — exactly the "hide from picker" flag the L1 spec already reserves (todos.md:321). The "spawn from agent" / New Session picker filters these out by default with an only-hidden/only-archived/all toggle, composed via the existing pure filter style (filterTiles cc-core.lua:322 / applyGroups:350). Soft-delete-with-restore: generalize presetPush's name-keyed replace-in-place (cc-core.lua:3686-3691) into agentPush so re-saving a previously archived/hidden/deleted profile by name clears those flags (update, not duplicate) — the idempotent restore. A `deleted` tombstone field plus a "recently deleted / restore" view in the agent-builder authoring front-end gives no-loss-on-mis-click. Drop entirely: Prisma columns, partial compound indexes, UniqueViolation/DB framing. Run-history half: do NOT build a new soft-delete table — route "dismiss a past session, keep the record" through the existing audit ledger (filterLedger + redact/purge in the 📜 Audit overlay), which is already the hide-not-erase store.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not. The ruling stands as ADAPT reinforcing L1. (1) No double mismatch: this is a list-lifecycle / library-management feature (hidden/archived/soft-delete + filter + restore), not graph/block execution, not credential storage, not marketplace serving — exactly the management layer the brief says CAN transfer onto Shepherd's primitives (library agent ~= saved agent profile). FIT is cleanly cleared. (2) Correctly NOT ADD: it has no standalone value — it is a property of a list that does not exist yet. Its worth is entirely parasitic on the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry, the user's stated #1 priority. Confirmed in the spec: todos.md:320-322 already explicitly reserves a "hide from picker" flag living in operator JSON, picker-scope only. So it reinforces L1; it cannot stand alone. (3) Correctly NOT DONT_ADD/redundant: the L1 spec only RESERVES the hide flag; this feature adds incremental operator value beyond that — a distinct archived (retired-but-kept) state, soft-delete-with-restore + a recently-deleted view, and idempotent un-tombstone-on-re-add. Minor but real. (4) Mapping verified against the code: presetPush (cc-core.lua:3686-3691) already does name-keyed replace-in-place — the exact idempotent restore-on-re-add primitive to generalize into agentPush; filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) / applyGroups (350) give the pure filter style for the picker toggle; filterLedger (cc-core.lua:523) + purge/redact (565-580) already exist, so the run-history soft-delete HALF is genuinely redundant with the audit ledger and should route through it, not a new table — judge said exactly this. (5) The plumbing-strip is correct: dropping Prisma booleans, partial compound indexes, and UniqueViolation/DB semantics removes multi-user-Postgres artifacts Shepherd cannot host (todos.md:275-279 already lists "DROP: all Prisma tables..."). Keep only pure cc-core JSON fields on cc-agents.json. Cannot downgrade to ADD (no standalone value) or DONT_ADD (not fully redundant with the reserved flag).

#### Sort the library (created vs updated / last-used)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd's #1 stated priority is a directory of saved, pre-configured agents (L1 Agent Profiles registry: cc-agents.json {name, folder, provider, model, ..., versions[]}). Once that list grows past a handful of profiles, a "sort by" affordance (created / last-updated / last-used) is the cheapest possible ergonomic win for picking the right profile to "Spawn from agent". The AutoGPT double mismatch does NOT bite here at all: the value is purely in ORGANIZING a saved-item list, not in running graphs, executing blocks, storing OAuth creds, or serving a marketplace. The implementation transfers almost 1:1 in spirit — AutoGPT's enum-bounded sort key into Prisma orderBy becomes a pure, unit-testable comparator in cc-core over the local JSON registry (sibling to agentList, mirroring presetList ~3640-3722), with the dropdown rendered in the existing webview list header. "Last-used" even has free local signal: Shepherd already tracks per-project last-used presets (todos.md:90) and could stamp a lastSpawnedAt on a profile when "Spawn from agent" fires, with created/updated from the JSON record's own timestamps. Tiny, deterministic, fits the architecture exactly.
- **Against:** This is a micro-ergonomic on a registry that does not yet exist — L1 is queued, not built — so building a sort dropdown now is premature; it should ride along when the registry list view is authored, not as a standalone task. The win is marginal: a single local user with a modest set of saved profiles can scan or fuzzy-filter (Shepherd already has core.fuzzyFilter / tile search) faster than they can reach for a sort menu, and a plain alphabetical-by-name order is usually the most useful for a hand-curated directory. "Last-used" requires adding and persisting a new lastSpawnedAt timestamp + write path, which is more surface than "trivial" implies. It is also largely subsumed by L1's broader authoring/list front-end and by the emerging catalog filter/sort surface — adding it as its own item risks fragmenting the L1 list-view design into a pile of one-liner sub-features.
- **Ruling:** Clear ADAPT. The candidate is self-describing as "ADAPT (trivial)" and the analysis holds: it is a pure client-side list-ordering affordance over local JSON, with no graph-execution / block-code / OAuth-credential / multi-user / marketplace dependency, so the AutoGPT double mismatch never engages. The genuine need (find the right saved agent quickly) is real and aligns with the user's stated #1 priority, but it is a sub-feature of the L1 Agent Profiles registry, not a new system — hence reinforces L1 rather than a parallel item. It should be folded into L1's list/authoring front-end. Not DONT_ADD (nothing structurally blocks it; it is genuinely useful and cheap). Not ADD (it is not standalone — it extends a queued primitive that doesn't exist yet, and must be reshaped from Prisma orderBy onto a cc-core comparator over cc-agents.json). Effort is genuinely small once L1's list view exists.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's registry list/authoring front-end. Add a pure cc-core comparator (e.g. core.agentSort(list, key)) bounded to an enum {name, created, updated, lastUsed}, sibling to agentList/presetList (~3640-3722, zero hs.*, unit-tested) — this is the local analog of AutoGPT's LibraryAgentSort enum → Prisma orderBy. Read created/updated from the cc-agents.json record's own timestamps; for lastUsed, stamp a lastSpawnedAt on the profile when the "Spawn from agent" action fires via spawnSpec (reusing the existing per-project last-used-preset signal, todos.md:90). Render a "sort by" <select> in the registry list header in the embedded webview JS (the New Session modal / L1 profile list). No new file, no FX, no network.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted on all four axes and failed; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. (a) Executor mismatch does NOT engage. Sorting a saved-agent list is pure list ordering over local JSON. AutoGPT's analog (LibraryAgentSort enum -> Prisma orderBy) is itself enum-driven sort, not graph/block execution; no DAG, no block code, no execution state needed. (b) Server/multi-user/marketplace/OAuth mismatch does NOT engage. A client-side comparator over a local file needs no network, users, or credentials. (c) Not redundant, and not DONT_ADD. I checked the strongest refutation: Shepherd already has sortByStatus (cc-core.lua:83) and tile search/filter — but those sort the LIVE tile grid, a different surface from the saved-profile registry. Crucially the registry it would sort, L1 Agent Profiles, is QUEUED NOT BUILT: cc-agents.json does not exist and there is no agentList/agentSort/agentPush anywhere in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua. The closest existing analog, presetList/presetPush (cc-core.lua:3658-3717), stores only {name, folder, editor, permMode, provider} with NO created/updated/lastUsed timestamps — so 'sort by created/updated/last-used' is not merely re-exposing existing data; L1's profile schema must grow those fields plus a lastSpawnedAt stamp on the Spawn-from-agent action. That is real (small) reshaping work, which is exactly why it is a sub-feature/extension of L1 (ADAPT) rather than DONT_ADD (redundant) or ADD (standalone — it cannot stand alone over a registry that does not yet exist). (d) Not DB plumbing mistaken for a console feature. The operator value (find the right saved agent fast via a 'sort by' dropdown) transfers cleanly to a local single-user console and aligns with the user's #1 stated priority. The Prisma orderBy underneath is plumbing, but the dropdown pattern is the transferable operator feature. The candidate self-describes as 'ADAPT (trivial)' and the judge's cc-core mapping (a pure enum-bounded core.agentSort(list,{name,created,updated,lastUsed}) sibling to agentList/presetList, zero hs.*, unit-tested via tests/core.test.lua; lastUsed via a lastSpawnedAt stamp reusing the per-project last-used signal at todos.md:90) is sound and needs no new file, FX, or network. The candidate's own 'largely covered by the catalog filter/sort candidate' is overstated — no such sort candidate exists in todos.md (line 366 is the L2 model-catalog DROP item) — but that cuts toward keeping the feature, not dropping it. The judge's 'high' confidence is slightly strong (candidate flags low priority; work is contingent on L1 landing first), but that is a confidence quibble, not a verdict change. ADAPT, reinforces L1, stands.

#### Import / add a saved agent from a file/dir (config-by-ref ingest) + export  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the user's stated #1 priority expressed as a concrete mechanic: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." AutoGPT's import-from-file and download-as-portable-JSON are the textbook prior art for the CREATE/PORTABILITY side of the saved-agents registry that L1 already plans but currently under-specifies. L1 today is rich on the schema (knowledge[]/skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle), the config-by-ref resolver, and spawn-from-agent, but its "Authoring front-end" sub-item only covers "Save as agent" inside the panel — there is no story for ingesting a profile someone hands you as a JSON file, or for exporting one to share/back up/move between machines. The adapted notion fits Shepherd's primitives cleanly and uses only infrastructure that already exists: FX.readFile (cc-core.lua glue) reads the file; the cc-presets.json/presetPush CRUD pattern (cc-core.lua:3658-3699, zero hs.*) is exactly what agentPush extends; and L5's "Export session archive" already establishes the cc-exports write path (mkdir/write/optional-zip/Reveal) that profile export reuses verbatim. Import is a pure decode + the same pre-flight validator L1 already mandates (refuse on unknown-field/missing-required/bad cross-ref), so a hand-edited or hostile JSON gets the same safety treatment as an in-panel edit. It is a small, additive, declarative-data feature with no executor, no credential store, no network — a near-perfect single-mismatch survivor.
- **Against:** Most of what AutoGPT actually ships here is on the wrong side of the double mismatch and must be amputated, which raises the question of whether enough remains to be worth a distinct line item. AutoGPT's "import" resolves a StoreListingVersion into a runnable graph and creates a LibraryAgent; "download" serves dumps(graph_data) of an executable DAG. The portable artifact is a RUNNABLE agent (graph + blocks); for Shepherd it can only ever be a DECLARATIVE preset (names + flags) that a human spawns — the marketplace/store leg and the n8n external-workflow-to-graph conversion are pure DONT_ADD (they need the block executor and multi-user serving Shepherd structurally lacks). What's left — read a JSON file into a registry, write a JSON file out — is arguably just two thin FX calls already implied by L1's authoring story and L5's export path, not a feature in its own right; folding it into L1 as a bullet risks being redundant rather than additive. There's also a real footgun: importing a profile that references skills/MCP servers/plugins/knowledge paths that don't exist on THIS machine (config-by-ref portability is brittle across machines), so import must do more than decode — it must resolve-and-warn, which is non-trivial UX. And a profile JSON can encode a permMode (e.g. bypass) or a seedPrompt; an imported, blindly-trusted profile is a mild local-trust vector that the in-panel author path doesn't have.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: split the candidate into its three AutoGPT legs. (1) Marketplace/store add — DONT_ADD; needs a public marketplace + StoreListingVersion serving Shepherd cannot host, and the artifact is a runnable graph. (2) External-workflow (n8n) → graph conversion — DONT_ADD; needs the in-process graph/block executor. (3) Import a definition from a local file/dir + export a definition as portable JSON — this is the survivor, and it's real. It is precisely the portability/CREATE side of L1 (the Agent Profiles registry), which is already queued and is the user's explicitly favored direction. So verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1 — not a new system. The fit is strong because every piece lands on an existing primitive: registry CRUD mirrors presetPush/presetList; ingest is FX.readFile + pure decode + L1's pre-flight validator (the validator is the load-bearing part — import must NOT trust the file, it must run the same unknown-field/missing-required/bad-cross-ref checks and the safe-path hardening L1 already specifies, then resolve-and-warn on references missing on this host); export reuses L5's cc-exports side-effect path. The strongest objection (it's just two FX calls / redundant with L1) is correct that it's small, but that argues for effort, not for DONT_ADD — and the portability + cross-machine-resolve-warn + trust-on-import nuances make it a worthwhile, explicitly-spec'd L1 bullet rather than something to leave implicit. No graph execution, no block code, no OAuth/credential storage, no server, no marketplace survives — the part that does is declarative operator data, which is exactly what Shepherd is allowed to hold.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add as a sub-item under L1 (Agent Profiles registry), alongside its "Authoring front-end" bullet, NOT a parallel system. IMPORT: a new FX.importProfile(path) reads the file via FX.readFile (cc-core.lua:277), pure-decodes, runs L1's pre-flight validator + safe-path hardening (reject unknown-field/missing-required/bad cross-ref before write), then calls agentPush (the cc-core CRUD that mirrors presetPush, cc-core.lua:3673) — and additionally runs the config-by-ref resolver in dry-run to surface references (skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]/knowledge[]/policyBundle/provider) that don't resolve on THIS host, raising a one-time alert + a by:"import" ledger event (reuse the config-drift alert path). Source can be a single JSON file or a directory of profiles (glob, keep-valid/drop-bad per the schema-drift fail-safe pattern). EXPORT: a per-profile "Export as JSON" action serializes the cc-agents.json record (optionally the resolved cc-mcp.json refs) and writes via the L5 cc-exports side-effect path (mkdir ~/.claude/cc-exports, write, optional zip, alert/Reveal). Same idea extends to exporting a single MCP record from cc-mcp.json and the L5 session/profile export. Keep all pure assembly/decode/validate in cc-core (unit-tested, zero hs.*); only file read/write/alert go through fx. DROP entirely: marketplace/store add, URL fetch, and any external-workflow→graph ingest.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. I verified every primitive the judge cites is real in this tree: FX.readFile is a trivial io.open read (claude-dashboard.lua:277); presetPush/presetList CRUD that L1's agentPush mirrors exists (cc-core.lua:3658-3722); the audit-export side-effect path the export leg reuses exists (claude-dashboard.lua:2125-2126 — mkdir LEDGER_DIR/exports + write timestamped file); and L1 already queues the pre-flight validator, config-by-ref resolver (extends providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537), schema-drift keep-valid/drop-bad loader with a by:"config-drift" alert (todos.md:290-326), and safe-path hardening. Apply the double mismatch: (a) no graph/block executor — import is readFile+pure-decode+validate+agentPush, export is serialize-record+write; both hold only declarative profile JSON, Shepherd runs nothing. (b) no server/marketplace/OAuth — the judge correctly DROPs the marketplace/store add, URL fetch, and external-workflow(n8n)->graph conversion (that leg WOULD need the executor); L1 stores env-var NAMES, never secrets. (d) it is declarative operator data, exactly what Shepherd is allowed to hold, not DB/platform plumbing. The only viable refutation is (c) redundancy: every component already exists in L1/L5, so import = ~two FX calls. But that is the exact objection the judge names and rebuts correctly — small effort != DONT_ADD. The capability is genuinely absent today: L1's 'Authoring front-end' CREATES a profile via a session interview (it does not INGEST an external file/dir), and L5's export is a SESSION ARCHIVE (transcript+meta.json), not a portable DECLARATIVE PRESET. The cross-machine resolve-and-warn (refs present on the authoring host, missing here) and the trust-on-import posture (run the validator + safe-path checks against an untrusted file before write) are load-bearing behaviors that don't fall out of plain CRUD and deserve an explicit L1 bullet. It is not a new system (so not ADD) and not redundant capability or infeasible (so not DONT_ADD). ADAPT under L1 is correct. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1 247-334, L5 522-525), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (3658-3722), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (277-280, 2125-2126).

#### Per-saved-agent settings + safe-mode flag (policy bundle attached to a profile)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator NEED is real and lands squarely on Shepherd's primitives plus the user's #1 stated interest (saved agents in a directory). AutoGPT attaches a per-LibraryAgent settings bundle (GraphSettings) that seeds the run's safety posture, with human_in_the_loop_safe_mode default ON and sensitive_action_safe_mode default OFF, plus a one-time pre-run acknowledgement popup. Shepherd already has every receiving primitive: L1 profiles carry a policyBundle field (todos.md:256, 359), L2 turns the gate into named attachable bundles with autoDeny/autopilot/lockedPermMode and starter bundles like read-only/no-Bash (todos.md:336-359), the headless approval gate + autopilot already exist, and a per-session risk score already exists as an indicator (cc-core.lua:1343). So 'a saved agent carries safe-mode settings' = an L1 profile with a default L2 bundle; 'HITL safe-mode ON' = the gate's escalation list active by default (Shepherd's default-on stance for the human-in-the-loop gate); 'sensitive-action -> require approval' = adding the sensitive tool set to the bundle's autoDeny/escalation list; the one-time 'this profile pauses for approval on X' acknowledgement, remembered per profile, is a genuinely light, on-brand UI add that reinforces transparency. Sane-default selection (HITL gate on, hard-gating off) matches Shepherd's existing posture. No executor, no server, no credentials needed.
- **Against:** The headline computation — has_human_in_the_loop / has_sensitive_action — is derived from the BLOCK TYPES in an agent GRAPH (AutoGPT inspects the DAG's nodes to decide whether HITL/sensitive blocks are present, model.py:227, and the popup is gated on those computed flags). Shepherd has no graph and no blocks: a Shepherd 'agent' is an already-running Claude Code GUI session it cannot statically introspect for 'contains a sensitive block.' So the automatic computation that powers the warning/gate cannot transfer at all — Shepherd would have to substitute a human-declared policyBundle, which is just the existing gate restated. The remaining novel piece — a one-time per-profile acknowledgement popup with seen-state in localStorage — is a thin UI nicety, not a new capability; and Shepherd's gate semantics already mean the human is in the loop on every escalated tool, so a separate 'safe-mode flag' risks being a redundant second knob over the gate/autopilot it already ships. Risk of building a parallel safety toggle that duplicates L2 rather than extending it.
- **Ruling:** Applying the double mismatch: the value of has_*-computed flags depends on RUNNING/inspecting a graph of blocks, which Shepherd structurally lacks — that specific mechanism is DONT_ADD territory. But the underlying operator pattern (a saved agent carries its own safety policy; warn the human once before a riskier agent runs) is exactly a library/management pattern, which the brief says transfers. Shepherd already has the receiving primitives queued (L1 profile.policyBundle + L2 named bundles + the live gate/autopilot + per-session risk score), so this is not a new system — it is a small enrichment of L1/L2: (1) make 'safe-mode' a named starter bundle posture rather than a free-floating flag, defaulting the HITL gate ON and sensitive hard-gating OFF (matches both AutoGPT's defaults and Shepherd's off-by-default-automation stance), and (2) add the one-time per-profile pre-run acknowledgement as a light confirmation remembered in operator state. Substitute the un-portable graph-introspection with the human-declared bundle the profile already carries. Hence ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (and L2's bundle machinery), not ADD and not a parallel safe-mode subsystem.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1 profile schema: the policyBundle field a profile already carries (todos.md:256) gains a 'safe-mode' default posture; ship L2 starter bundles 'hitl-safe' (sensitive/mutating tools in the gate escalation list, on by default) and an optional 'sensitive-gate' (autoDeny tier, off by default) per AutoGPT's ON/OFF defaults. Floating safe-mode toggle -> reuse the existing per-session autopilot/gate toggle surfaced on the tile/detail; no new control system. has_sensitive_action -> a pure cc-core derivation over the resolved bundle (does the profile's bundle escalate/deny any sensitive tool?) feeding a tile/detail badge, NOT graph introspection. One-time acknowledgement: a new pure 'profileNeedsAck(profile, ackState)' in cc-core + an FX-persisted seen-set (operator JSON, mirroring AutoGPT's localStorage), rendered as a one-time 'this profile will pause for approval on X' confirm in the New Session / Spawn-from-agent modal, then a by:'profile-ack' ledger event. Forge's per-agent permissions.yaml overrides -> the per-session opt-out + bundle attachment already specified in L2 (todos.md:351). DROP: the graph/block-type computation, GraphSettings DB row, and any server-side merge-at-run.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT ruling holds, reinforcing L1 (profile.policyBundle) + L2 (named bundles). I verified each receiving primitive in the actual code/spec, not just the judge's prose: (1) L1 profiles already carry policyBundle (todos.md:256) and spawn-from-agent "applies the profile's policy bundle" (todos.md:262) — this IS "a saved agent carries its own safety policy." (2) L2 already specs named bundles `{autoAllow[], autoDeny[], gateTools, autopilot, lockedPermMode?}` with per-rule `action: deny|allow|flag` (todos.md:342-343) AND already-planned starter bundles read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only tied to L1 (todos.md:357-359). A "hitl-safe"/"sensitive-gate" posture is just one more entry in that planned starter list, not a new system. (3) The "sensitive/mutating action" set already exists and is gated ON by default: DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS = "Bash Write Edit MultiEdit NotebookEdit" (cc-core.lua:2525) on the gate ESCALATION (hold-for-approval) list — Shepherd already defaults the HITL gate ON, matching AutoGPT's human_in_the_loop_safe_mode=ON default. (4) has_sensitive_action collapses to a pure cc-core derivation over the resolved bundle ("does this bundle escalate/deny any sensitive tool"), riding the existing resolveGateTools resolver (cc-core.lua:2537) + the planned config-by-ref resolver (todos.md:295-298). It does NOT need graph/block introspection. (5) The "floating safe-mode toggle" is redundant with the existing per-session autopilot/gate toggle already on the tile (b-auto button, claude-dashboard.lua:3067; "Autopilot: ON" tile state at 4375) — reuse, no new control. Double-mismatch correctly handled by the judge: the un-portable AutoGPT mechanism (has_* computed by inspecting a GRAPH OF BLOCKS at run/inspect time — executor territory Shepherd lacks) and the GraphSettings DB row + server-side merge-at-run (multi-user/server surface) are explicitly DROPPED. What remains is the management/library pattern (saved agent carries a posture; warn the human once before a riskier agent runs), which the brief says transfers. Why not DONT_ADD (the strongest refutation I could mount): I grepped cc-core.lua and todos.md for any existing one-time acknowledgement / seen-set / "warn before first run" pattern and found NONE. So the one-time per-profile pre-run ack (pure profileNeedsAck + an FX-persisted seen-set + a confirm in the spawn-from-agent modal + a by:'profile-ack' ledger event) is a genuinely new, if small, local-console affordance — it is not pure redundancy, so it does not collapse to DONT_ADD. Why not ADD: there is nothing to downgrade FROM ADD — both the original Shepherd verdict and the judge already landed on ADAPT. The residue is an enrichment of queued primitives (extend L1's policyBundle default posture + add one L2 starter bundle + reuse the existing toggle + one light pure-derivation badge + one one-time confirm), not a parallel safe-mode subsystem. That is exactly ADAPT, reinforcing L1/L2.

#### Empty-state onboarding for the registry  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The need is real and lands squarely on Shepherd's stated #1 priority: a directory of saved agents (L1). Any registry needs a first-run state, and an empty list reading "no saved agents yet — create one from a running session / import a profile" with a clear call-to-action makes a new feature discoverable instead of a blank void. Shepherd already proves it values this pattern: the tile grid ships an empty-state today ("Waiting for Claude Code sessions… Start a session in any project." at claude-dashboard.lua:3038, styled by the #empty CSS at :2665). Mirroring that proven idiom onto the agents card is trivial, zero-risk, pure presentation JS/CSS — no new primitive, no effect, no cc-core logic. It costs almost nothing and improves the one feature the user most wants.
- **Against:** This is not really a "feature" — it's a sub-detail of building L1, and L1 doesn't exist yet (no agentList/cc-agents.json in cc-core.lua). An empty state for the saved-agent registry is meaningless until there IS a registry; it can't ship on its own. AutoGPT's actual implementation pairs the empty UI with onboarding-step TELEMETRY (complete_onboarding_step(MARKETPLACE_ADD_AGENT) at routes/agents.py:164) — that half is pure platform plumbing (multi-user accounts, a marketplace, server-side progress tracking) that Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host. Stripped of the telemetry, what remains is a one-line empty <div>, below the threshold of a tracked backlog item and already implied by L1's "authoring front-end" work ("Save as agent", the add_agent/onboarding prompt set). Logging it separately is over-bookkeeping.
- **Ruling:** Classic AutoGPT double-mismatch split, and the candidate's own SHEPHERD note nails it. (a) The empty-state UI is a pure, platform-agnostic management pattern that transfers cleanly: "library agent" ≈ saved agent profile, and Shepherd already ships the identical empty-state idiom for its session grid, so this is copy-paste of an existing pattern onto a new card. (b) The onboarding-step flip is multi-user/marketplace/server telemetry — exactly the (b)-side platform plumbing Shepherd has no server/accounts/marketplace to host; DONT_ADD that half (correctly flagged in the candidate). The transferable half is real but tiny and entirely subordinate to L1 (Agent Profiles registry), which is queued, not built (todos.md:247). So the honest verdict is ADAPT-reinforcing-L1: don't open a parallel track — note that when L1's authoring front-end lands, the agents card gets the same #empty treatment the tile grid already has, with a CTA to "create from a running session / import a profile." It does NOT depend on graph/block execution, OAuth storage, or marketplace serving, so it clears the fit question — it just isn't standalone.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's "Authoring front-end" bullet (todos.md:327). When the saved-agent registry card renders (the L1 authoring UI), give it an empty-state mirroring the existing tile-grid pattern: reuse the #empty div + CSS (claude-dashboard.lua:2665 / :3038) for the agents card, copy text "no saved agents yet — create one from a running session, or import a profile", and wire the CTA to the existing "Save as agent" / New-Session-modal spawn-from-agent action. Pure panel JS/CSS, no cc-core change, no fx. DROP the onboarding-step flag entirely (no accounts/marketplace to track against).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT ruling stands as reinforcing L1. Verified against the codebase: (1) The empty-state is pure DOM/CSS with no dependence on the graph/block executor — Shepherd's existing one (claude-dashboard.lua renderGrid:4980-4994) fires purely on lastItems.length===0, no execution. So mismatch (a) doesn't kill the transferable half. (2) The marketplace/onboarding-step half IS pure (b) platform telemetry — no server/accounts/marketplace exists (context.md:13 "no multi-user, no secrets") — but the candidate already correctly split it off as DONT_ADD, so attacking that half changes nothing. (3) Not redundant: the empty-state idiom exists for the SESSION tile grid only (#empty div at :3038, CSS at :2665, EMPTY_WAITING at :4977), but the agents registry it would decorate does NOT exist — confirmed no cc-agents.json and zero agentList/registry/agents-card references in claude-dashboard.lua or cc-core.lua. (4) Cannot be ADD/standalone precisely because that agents card is L1's queued authoring front-end (todos.md:327), not built. The transferable half is real (library agent ≈ saved agent profile) but tiny and wholly subordinate to L1. So: clears the fit question, but is a fold-in not a track — ADAPT reinforcing L1, drop the onboarding flag. Cannot downgrade to DONT_ADD (the UI half genuinely transfers); cannot upgrade to ADD (not standalone).

#### Management dashboard with status filters + aggregate stat tiles  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator pattern — a management table of 'things I own' with status-filter columns, sort, pagination, per-row run/usage counts, and an aggregate stat header — is exactly the missing front-end for Shepherd's queued L1 Agent Profiles registry. The user's #1 stated priority is 'pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory.' A registry needs a place to SEE and manage those profiles: list them with status filters, show how many times each was spawned / how many runs it produced (derivable from the ledger), and edit/delete. The aggregate-stat tiles (total profiles, active, total spawns/runs) give an at-a-glance fleet-library health read, mirroring the insights overlay's tiles. The 'compute over ALL rows, not the visible page' correctness lesson is a clean, transferable engineering point. And it slots in cheaply: Shepherd already has fleetStats/fleetStandup aggregators, filterTiles, and sortByStatus to reuse, so this is a thin new view, not new machinery.
- **Against:** Taken literally, this is a STORE/MARKETPLACE creator dashboard: its columns (submission status submitted/approved/in-review, rating), its edit-only-on-PENDING rule, its 'my unpublished agents not yet listed', and its server-computed SubmissionStats (store/db.py:610, GET /store/submissions) are pure multi-user-marketplace-serving plumbing — the exact (b) half of the AutoGPT double mismatch that Shepherd has no server/users/marketplace to host. Worse, the generic 'management table + aggregate stat tiles + status filter + sort' is already LARGELY BUILT for sessions: fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) and fleetStandup (cc-core.lua:1122) compute the aggregate totals + per-project rollup, filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) does status/group search, sortByStatus (cc-core.lua:80) sorts by status, and the tile grid IS the board. So most of the candidate is either un-hostable (marketplace) or redundant (insights/shift report already do it), leaving only a modest new view over L1's profiles — risk of re-adding a parallel stats system.
- **Ruling:** The candidate has a double identity. LITERALLY it is the AutoGPT store/marketplace creator-dashboard: a table of submitted store agents with submission STATUS (submitted/approved/in-review), a rating column, edit-only-on-PENDING (a submission state machine), and a SubmissionStats header computed server-side over all submissions (store/db.py:610, GET /store/submissions). That literal feature is squarely the (b) MULTI-USER WEB PLATFORM / public marketplace half of the AutoGPT double mismatch — submission review lifecycle, server-side serving, ratings/approval — which Shepherd structurally cannot and will not host (no server, no users, no marketplace). On its own that is DONT_ADD. But the prompt explicitly asks me to judge the OPERATOR PATTERN, not the marketplace plumbing, and pre-frames this as ADAPT. The transferable pattern is real and strong: a management table of 'things I own' + status-filter columns + sort + aggregate stat tiles + per-row usage/run counts. That maps directly onto Shepherd's already-queued L1 Agent Profiles registry — a table of 'my saved agent profiles' (cc-agents.json) with status filters, usage/run counts per profile, and edit/delete of profiles. This serves the user's explicitly-weighted #1 priority: 'pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory.' So verdict ADAPT, reinforces=L1. Crucially, the STATS-TILE / status-rollup half is largely PRIOR ART Shepherd already has and must not re-add: fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) already computes totals {events, sessions, prompts, toolRequests, spawns, decisions} + decision allow/deny/fallback + provenance + blocked-time; fleetStandup (cc-core.lua:1122) already produces a per-project rollup, sorted, with lifecycle/problem tallies — that IS an aggregate stats header + management table for SESSIONS. filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) is token-AND search over status/group/label; sortByStatus (cc-core.lua:80) sorts by status priority; the tile grid is already the board. So 'aggregate stat tiles + status filter + sortable management table' over sessions is mostly DONE. The genuinely NEW, worthwhile slice is bringing that SAME shape to the saved-PROFILES registry (which has no view yet) plus per-profile spawn/run counts derived from the ledger. The one transferable engineering INSIGHT — compute stats over ALL non-deleted rows server-side so pagination doesn't undercount — is a correct lesson but near-trivial at Shepherd's local single-user scale (a handful of profiles, no real paging, aggregate the full ledger not a visible page). Worth keeping in mind, not a feature. What I DON'T transfer (DONT_ADD slivers, per the mismatch): the submission state machine (submitted/approved/in-review), edit-only-on-PENDING gating, store ratings, 'my unpublished agents not yet listed in the store', and any server-side serving — all marketplace-only. Net: ADAPT the dashboard/stats-tile PATTERN onto L1's profiles registry (reusing the existing insights/shift-report aggregators rather than building a parallel stats system). Effort S: it is a new table view over data Shepherd already holds, leveraging existing pure aggregators and filter/sort helpers; no new executor, no server, no credential store.
- **How (Shepherd):** Build the L1 Agent Profiles registry VIEW: a 'My Agents' management table over cc-agents.json (one row per saved profile {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, skills[], mcpServers[]}) with (1) status/scope filter chips reusing the filterTiles token-AND helper (cc-core.lua:322) and sortByStatus-style ordering (cc-core.lua:80); (2) per-profile usage/run columns — a pure cc-core aggregator (sibling to fleetStats:954) that walks the ledger for by:"batch"/spawn events stamped with the profile name to count spawns + sessions produced; (3) an aggregate stat-tile header (total profiles, profiles spawned this window, total spawns/runs) reusing the insights-overlay tile rendering rather than a new stats engine; (4) edit/delete wired to the existing agentPush/agentRemove CRUD planned in L1 (mirroring presetPush/presetList ~3640-3722). Apply the 'aggregate over the full registry/ledger, not the visible slice' lesson so counts never undercount. DROP entirely: submission status states (submitted/approved/in-review), ratings, edit-only-on-PENDING, 'unpublished/not-yet-listed', and all server-side serving.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute; the ADAPT holds. I verified every prior-art and mapping claim in the codebase. The LITERAL feature is the AutoGPT store/marketplace creator dashboard: submission STATUS states (submitted/approved/in-review), a ratings column, edit-only-on-PENDING (a submission state machine), server-side SubmissionStats, and a "my unpublished agents not yet listed in the store" list. That is squarely the (b) multi-user web platform / public marketplace half of the double mismatch — Shepherd has no server, no users, no marketplace, no submission lifecycle — so the literal feature is DONT_ADD and cannot be ADDed verbatim. The strongest refutation I could mount is (c) redundancy: the "aggregate stat tiles + status filter + sortable management table" pattern is largely PRIOR ART Shepherd already ships. I confirmed this in source: fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) computes totals {events, sessions, prompts, toolRequests, spawns, decisions} + decision allow/deny/fallback + provenance + blocked-time; fleetStandup (cc-core.lua:1137) is a per-project rollup, sorted, with lifecycle/problem tallies — i.e. an aggregate stats header + management table for SESSIONS; filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) is token-AND status/group/label filtering; sortByStatus (cc-core.lua:83) is status-priority sort; and the tile grid is already the board. So that shape over SESSIONS is done, and the one engineering insight (aggregate over all non-deleted rows server-side so paging doesn't undercount) is near-trivial at local single-user scale. But redundancy does not cover the whole candidate. I confirmed there is NO cc-agents.json, NO agentPush/agentRemove, and NO Agent Profiles registry VIEW yet — L1 is queued (todos.md:247, effort L), not shipped. The genuinely net-new slice the judge isolates — the same management-table shape applied to the saved-PROFILES registry with per-profile spawn/run counts walked from the ledger — does not exist and is not redundant. It needs no graph/block executor, no server, no OAuth credential store, no marketplace; it is a new table over data Shepherd already holds, reusing existing pure aggregators and the presetPush/presetList CRUD precedent (cc-core.lua:3658-3710). It reinforces the user's explicitly #1-weighted priority (saved agents in a directory). Because the literal feature can't be ADDed but a real transferable slice reinforces queued L1 (with all marketplace slivers — status states, ratings, edit-only-on-PENDING, unpublished list, server serving — correctly DROPPED), ADAPT/reinforces=L1 is the right call. Refutation fails.

#### Provider metadata / capability discovery endpoints  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable pattern is real and hits the user's #1 stated priority: a queryable, read-only catalog that describes each "thing you can spawn/use" plus its capabilities, used to drive the picker/connect UI. That is exactly the shape of the saved agents/skills/MCP directory the user wants — a browsable inventory with human descriptions and capability metadata (which transport an MCP uses, what a skill's shape/command is, what env a profile requires). AutoGPT's ProviderInfo with supports_* flags and ProviderMetadata.get_supported_auth_types is good prior art for "render the picker from declared capabilities, and pre-flight what each option needs before you use it." Shepherd already has the bones (a providers registry via providerById/providerEnv with provider cards and live /model) and L1 already specs the agent/skill/MCP catalog; this feature confirms that a metadata-rich, capability-driven catalog is the right design and that surfacing per-item requirements (an analog of supports_*) belongs on the cards — i.e. it usefully reinforces L1's skills card, MCP inventory badge, requiredEnv flags, and pre-flight validator.
- **Against:** The literal feature is double-mismatched and net-new value is near zero. Its core axes — supported auth types (oauth/api_key/user_password/host_scoped), default OAuth scopes, and which providers have platform "system" credits — exist solely to drive AutoGPT's OAuth credential-storage connect flow and its multi-user billing/marketplace. Both are explicit Shepherd HARD SCOPE violations: NO secrets storage (it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell), no credential store, no server, no multi-user, no marketplace. AutoGPT "providers" are also a different concept (integration connectors for blocks: Google/GitHub) than Shepherd "providers" (model-endpoint env-injection profiles), so the endpoint does not map 1:1. And the genuinely transferable part — a queryable capability catalog driving the picker — is already shipped (provider registry + cards) and already fully specced in L1 (queryable cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json CRUD, skills enumerator with shape/command/description, MCP inventory badge, requiredEnv capability flags, config-by-ref resolver, pre-flight validation). There is no new system to build; only the DONT_ADD plumbing (auth-type enum, OAuth scopes, system credits) is residual.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the feature's distinctive value (auth-type enumeration, default scopes, "system" credits) is OAuth-credential-store + billing/marketplace plumbing that Shepherd structurally cannot host — that part is DONT_ADD. Strip that away and what remains is the operator pattern: a read-only, queryable catalog of spawnable things with human descriptions and capability metadata that drives the picker UI. That need is real and is the user's stated #1 priority, but Shepherd ALREADY HAS the provider half (providerById/providerEnv registry, provider cards, live /model) and has ALREADY QUEUED the rest under L1: cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json with agentList/agentPush CRUD, a skills enumerator surfacing shape//<name>/description, an MCP inventory badge, requiredEnv NAME-presence flags (the local, secret-free analog of supports_*), a config-by-ref resolver, and a pre-flight validator. So the verdict is ADAPT (weak), reinforcing L1, not ADD and not a new Lx. The honest residual is corroboration only: it validates that capability metadata should live on the catalog cards and that the UI should pre-flight each option's requirements before use. Confidence high because both the mismatch (creds/billing) and the existing coverage (registry + L1) are unambiguous.
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds into L1 (cc-core.lua agent/skill/MCP registries) and the existing provider registry — no new system. (1) Capability metadata on cards: extend L1's skills enumerator (FX.listSkills surfacing shape//<name>/description) and the MCP inventory badge so each catalog item shows what it is and needs — the local analog of ProviderInfo.supports_*. (2) The secret-free analog of get_supported_auth_types is L1's requiredEnv: [{name, description, required, default}] with core.missingEnv NAME-presence checks (via loginShellWrap) — declare what an item needs, never store auth values. (3) Make the catalog queryable for the picker via L1's pure CRUD (agentList/mcpList/presetList) feeding the New Session modal pickers and slash-autocomplete; the config-by-ref resolver dereferences names→concrete flags at spawn. (4) The provider half already exists (providerById/providerEnv + provider cards + live /model) — at most ensure provider cards expose a human description field. DROP entirely: auth-type enum (oauth/user_password/host_scoped), default OAuth scopes, platform "system" credits — credential-store and billing/marketplace plumbing out of scope.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted but fails — ADAPT holds (as a WEAK/corroborative reinforcement of L1, not the "high" the prior judge labeled). Double-mismatch split is clean and verified against the code: 1) The feature's DISTINCTIVE value is credential/billing plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot host: enumerate supported auth types (oauth/api_key/user_password/host_scoped), default OAuth scopes, and platform "system" credits to drive a connect UI. context.md sets the hard limit "no secrets" (context.md:13); grep confirms ZERO auth-type/scope/credit machinery in cc-core.lua. The only OAuth touch is the read-only plan-window usage poll (token never logged), not a provider credential store. That whole portion is DONT_ADD, exactly as the judge said. 2) Strip that, and the residual is "a read-only queryable catalog of spawnable things with human descriptions + capability metadata to drive the picker." Shepherd already HAS the provider half (M.providerById cc-core.lua:2632, M.providerEnv:2714, M.envPrefix:2738, provider cards, live /model) and has ALREADY QUEUED the rest in granular detail under L1 (todos.md:247-334): cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json CRUD (agentList/agentPush), the dual-shape skills enumerator surfacing shape//<name>/description (FX.listSkills, todos.md:266-267, 306-310), the MCP inventory badge (270-274), and the secret-free analog of get_supported_auth_types — requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] with core.missingEnv NAME-presence-only checks via loginShellWrap (287-289), plus the config-by-ref resolver (295-298) and pre-flight validator (290-294) feeding the New-Session pickers + slash-autocomplete (311-314). Why it does not drop to DONT_ADD: the residual is real but non-zero — it independently corroborates that capability metadata should live on the catalog cards and that the picker should pre-flight each option's requirements (requiredEnv) before spawn. That corroboration is exactly what separates ADAPT from DONT_ADD. So the verdict bucket is correct. My only correction to the prior ruling: it should be ADAPT (weak), not (high). The feature is largely subsumed by the existing provider registry + the already-specced L1 — it adds confirmation, not new surface. Net: HOLDS at ADAPT, reinforcing L1, with the credential/auth/billing portion dropped entirely.

#### Per-session storage scoping + working folder + layered config  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** One genuine, non-redundant crumb survives the mismatch: AutoGPT's per-agent config layering (.env -> workspace yaml -> per-agent yaml) exposing introspectable model/decoding fields (SMART_LLM, FAST_LLM, TEMPERATURE, REASONING_EFFORT, THINKING_BUDGET_TOKENS, PROMPT_STRATEGY, DISABLED_COMMANDS) is exactly the operator pattern Shepherd's saved-agent registry (L1) wants. Shepherd already has live /model and /effort and provider profiles that bundle a model id + non-secret env, but those are transient session actions, not SAVED per-profile presets. Letting a saved agent profile carry {model, effort, thinkingBudget, disabledCommands, per-profile env overrides} that resolve at spawn-time (fleet default -> provider default -> per-profile override) is a real, useful extension directly aligned with the user's stated priority of pre-configured agents saved in a directory. DISABLED_COMMANDS even maps cleanly to Shepherd's policy/gate bundles (L2). And the 'working folder on the profile + per-session directory scoping' half is precisely Shepherd's model: presetPush/presetList already require an absolute folder per preset and the spawn scopes to it.
- **Against:** The feature's center of gravity is pure AutoGPT double mismatch and Shepherd structurally cannot host it. restrict_to_workspace sandbox enforcement is an EXECUTOR concern: it constrains what the running agent loop may touch on disk WHILE it executes block code -- Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no blocks, so it has nothing to sandbox (the Claude Code GUI session is driven by Claude Code itself, not Shepherd). The pluggable FileStorage backend with local/S3/GCS via clone_with_subroot is hosted-platform/multi-user serving plumbing. Worst of all, the layered config's .env/secret writing directly violates Shepherd's HARD SCOPE LIMIT (NO secrets storage; it deliberately stores only env-var NAMES via authTokenEnv and lets the spawned login shell expand them, so cc-config.json never holds a key -- cc-core.lua:2619-2622). And the surviving crumb is already largely present/queued: profile-carries-folder and per-directory scoping are SHIPPED (presets require an absolute folder), and saved model/effort presets are simply L1's existing profile schema. There is no new system to build, just optional fields.
- **Ruling:** Two-part candidate with a sharp split. DONT_ADD half (the bulk): restrict_to_workspace sandbox enforcement = executor concern (Shepherd runs no agent loop / executes no blocks, nothing to sandbox); pluggable local/S3/GCS FileStorage + clone_with_subroot = multi-user/server plumbing; .env/secret writing = direct violation of Shepherd's NO-secrets hard limit (provider profiles already store env-var NAMES only, never keys). ADAPT half (the only real, non-redundant need): a saved agent profile carrying model/effort/thinking/disabled-commands presets + per-profile env overrides with spawn-time layered resolution. That is squarely L1 (Agent Profiles registry), whose schema already lists folder, provider, model, requiredEnv -- this just adds the decoding-param fields and a resolver. The 'profile records a working folder + session scoped to a directory' crumb is ALREADY SHIPPED (presetPush/presetList require an absolute folder; cc-core.lua:3657-3694) so it's not new work. Net: not a new system, a thin extension of queued L1. Category Library & Organization (the saved-agents/profiles registry). Effort S: a few optional fields on the existing preset schema + the spawn-time resolver, surfaced in L1's planned authoring front-end.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1's agent-profile schema (cc-agents.json) with optional decoding/runtime preset fields: model, effort, thinkingBudgetTokens, promptStrategy, disabledCommands[], plus a per-profile envOverrides map of NON-SECRET names (never values -- keep cc-core.lua:2619-2622's authTokenEnv pattern). Add a spawn-time resolver layering fleet-default -> provider-default -> per-profile override (mirror the existing resolveGateTools precedence at cc-core.lua:2537). Reuse the already-shipped preset 'folder' field (presetPush/presetList, cc-core.lua:3657-3694) for the working-folder-on-profile crumb -- no new work there. disabledCommands routes into the L2 policy/gate bundle. Explicitly DROP: restrict_to_workspace sandbox enforcement, the local/S3/GCS FileStorage backend + clone_with_subroot, and any .env/secret writing.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. I verified every cited line against the source. The candidate is a self-described "MOSTLY DONT_ADD" two-part item and the judge split it correctly. DONT_ADD bulk (correctly dropped): (1) restrict_to_workspace sandbox enforcement is a pure executor concern — AutoGPT enforces it because its agent loop runs block code that reads/writes files; Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no blocks, so there is nothing to sandbox (mismatch a). (2) Pluggable local/S3/GCS FileStorage + clone_with_subroot is storage/server plumbing, and the S3/GCS side is multi-user/server surface (mismatch b/d). (3) .env/secret writing directly violates Shepherd's NO-secrets hard limit — verified cc-core.lua:2619-2622: provider profiles store only env-var NAMES (authTokenEnv) that the spawned LOGIN SHELL expands; Shepherd's process never sees a key. ADAPT half (the only live question): a saved profile carrying model/effort/thinking/promptStrategy/disabledCommands presets + per-profile NON-secret env-name overrides resolved at spawn with layered precedence. I tested the "fully redundant" refutation and it fails. The folder/directory-scoping crumb IS already shipped (presetPush/presetList require an absolute folder, cc-core.lua:3657-3694 — verified), and /model + /effort + permMode already exist as live controls (cc-core.lua:157-168,226-237) AND in the preset schema (line 3664).
