BUT the verified L1 schema (todos.md:256-257) lists only {name,folder,provider,model,permMode,seedPrompt,policyBundle,skills[],mcpServers[],plugins[],versions[]} — it does NOT yet carry effort, thinkingBudgetTokens, promptStrategy, or disabledCommands as saved per-profile fields. So this is a genuine, thin increment, not a new system. The spawn-time layered resolver is also not new: resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537, verified) already implements override > providerDefault > fleetDefault > built-in precedence with providerDefault explicitly "reserved for a future per-provider layer," and L1 already plans a "config-by-reference resolver" (todos.md:295-298). disabledCommands routes into L2's already-planned attachable gate bundle. Net: cannot push ADAPT→DONT_ADD (the ADAPT half is non-redundant and is composed entirely of NON-secret decoding params passed through the spawned shell — no graph execution, no credential store, no server, no marketplace). It is squarely a thin extension of queued L1 (Agent Profiles registry), which is also the user's #1 stated priority. The fit question is satisfied. Ruling holds: ADAPT, reinforces L1.

#### Graph structure validation with per-node/per-field errors (pre-flight)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The validation PATTERN — walk a saved definition, return structured errors keyed by record and field (missing required inputs, unknown fields, unresolvable cross-references), and refuse to launch with the full error list before emitting anything — is directly applicable to Shepherd's saved agent profiles (L1) and parameterized templates (L3). Shepherd already has the seed of this: presetList (cc-core.lua:3658) validates each record (name nonblank, folder absolute) and silently drops the bad ones. The candidate's two-tier strictness (for_run flag: looser structural check before save, stricter before run) maps cleanly onto Shepherd's two moments — a lenient check when persisting a profile to cc-agents.json vs. a strict refuse-spawn check at "Spawn from agent" where cross-refs (policyBundle, mcpServers[], skills[], requiredEnv) must actually resolve. Surfacing field-level errors in the builder before a bad config reaches a spawn is exactly the operator value: catch a dangling MCP ref or missing required env at edit/spawn time, not as a confusing failure inside a launched session. It is pure deterministic logic (zero hs.*), unit-testable, fits the cc-core architecture perfectly, and serves the user's #1 stated priority (a directory of reliable saved agents/skills/MCPs).
- **Against:** A large fraction of the candidate's actual surface is graph/block-executor machinery that Shepherd structurally cannot host and that has no Shepherd analog. validate_graph walks nodes + sub_graphs of an executable DAG, checks broken LINKS between node ports, and enforces static-output-block constraints (is_static_output_block) — these only make sense for a graph that AutoGPT's in-process executor runs. Shepherd runs no graph, has no nodes/links/ports, executes no blocks; there is nothing to "walk" structurally beyond a flat profile/template record. The for_run stricter mode in AutoGPT exists because running the DAG has runtime preconditions (every node's required input must be wired); Shepherd has no such DAG to gate. The frontend useBrokenOutputs.ts is a node-graph builder concern with no counterpart in a tile-grid console. So most of the literal feature is the DONT_ADD half of a classic double mismatch (executor + builder-canvas), and what remains is a generic "validate a record before use" idea that L1 already specifies — risking a parallel validator system if treated as new.
- **Ruling:** Classic AutoGPT double-mismatch split, and the candidate already pre-sorts it correctly. #### Binding a session to a target context (resume on refresh, scope what it may do)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable kernel is genuinely real and already half-native to Shepherd: "a session is bound to a target/context; that binding scopes what it may do and lets you resume the same session." - **Against:** The literal feature is mostly DONT_ADD and the salvageable kernel is largely already shipped, so the net new build is thin. The candidate's concrete value — builder_graph_id locking, BUILDER_BLOCKED_TOOLS blacklist, resolve_session_permissions rejecting other agent_ids, lock-on-create-and-claim for double-TAB races — all presupposes AutoGPT's graph executor and multi-user web platform (per-user LibraryAgent.settings, agent_id ownership, concurrent browser tabs hitting one backend). Shepherd is single-user, runs no graph, has no agent_id namespace to reject, and its "session" is a Claude Code GUI process Claude Code itself drives — there is nothing to "lock to a graph" and no tool-blacklist to enforce (Shepherd's gate is an escalation list, not an allow/deny binding). The "resume the same session on refresh" property is already free: Shepherd reads status off ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json every tick, so the panel inherently re-attaches; projectKey already gives folder-stable identity across close/reopen. So the only incremental delta is a small pointer field on the future L1 profile record — not enough to be its own feature.

(a) Executor mismatch: the feature exists to lock a CHAT to a running GRAPH and reject foreign agent_ids — Shepherd runs no graph and has no agent_id space, so the lock target is meaningless. (b) Platform mismatch: get-or-create-keyed-on-(user, graph), the LibraryAgent.settings pointer, and lock-on-create-and-claim for double-tab races all assume a multi-user FastAPI/Next.js backend with concurrent browser sessions; single-user local Hammerspoon has no such race and no per-user library. So the graph-id locking and BUILDER_BLOCKED_TOOLS blacklist are correctly DONT_ADD. What survives is only the abstract operator pattern — "context-bound session that scopes itself and resumes" — and Shepherd already realizes most of it: projectKey-stable persistence, status-file re-attach every tick, and routing v1 binding a queue to a folder. The remaining honest delta is reshaping that kernel into L1 (a saved profile carrying its folder/policy scope plus an optional live-session pointer so re-spawn re-attaches instead of duplicating). That is an extension of an already-queued item, not a new system — hence ADAPT, reinforces L1. - **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry, cc-agents.json). (2) Resume-on-refresh = add an optional "bound session" pointer field on the L1 profile record (sibling to how relabels/groups persist by projectKey): on "Spawn from agent", get-or-create — if a live session for that (profile, folder) already exists in cc-status, re-attach the panel/queue to it instead of minting a duplicate; clear the pointer when that session goes stale (mirror core.staleDuplicateKeys / projectKey resolution at cc-core.lua ~290). Shepherd has NO graph, NO agent loop, and NO agent_id namespace — `grep -n 'agent_id|agentId|LibraryAgent|builder|graph' cc-core.lua` returns ZERO conceptual hits (only coincidental substrings like 'paragraph'). The lock target is meaningless here, so builder_graph_id, BUILDER_BLOCKED_TOOLS, and agent_id rejection are rightly dropped. - Platform mismatch: get-or-create-keyed-on-(user, graph), the LibraryAgent.settings pointer, and the lock-on-create double-tab CLAIM all presume a multi-user FastAPI/Next.js backend with concurrent browser sessions. Single-user local Hammerspoon has no per-user library and no double-tab race — these are server plumbing, correctly DONT_ADD. WHAT GENUINELY SURVIVES (why it is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD): the abstract operator pattern "a session is bound to a target/context; the binding scopes what it may do and lets you resume the same session." #### Versioning with immutable numbered versions, active-version pointer + changelog  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd's saved-config primitives currently destroy history on edit. templatePush at cc-core.lua:3613 overwrites in place (out[#out+1] = {name=name, text=text}; replaced=true), and presetPush replaces by name the same way. Edit a task template or spawn preset and the prior text is gone — no undo, no "what did this used to say," no record of what changed. The candidate's LOCAL core is exactly the cure and needs none of AutoGPT's plumbing: monotonic integer versions (max+1), all prior versions kept immutably, a movable active-version pointer (spawn from v3 even though v5 exists), a per-version changes_summary that renders as a human changelog, fallback-to-most-recent-prior on revert, and pin-vs-auto-track-latest. Every one is pure JSON revision management — no graph executor, no blocks, no server, no credentials. It maps onto a real operator need (iterating a profile/template over weeks while keeping the version that demonstrably works), and the roadmap already anticipated it: L1's profile schema carries an optional local-only versions[]? field ("reuse the session-lineage mindset; no remote object"), and L3 already specifies templatePushVersioned ("duplicate-on-edit / v(n+1), don't overwrite + versions/revert view"). The candidate enriches those stubs with concrete semantics — the active pointer, changes_summary, and revert-fallback — that the queued items hadn't fully fleshed out. Pin-vs-auto-track-latest is a genuinely useful refinement: a session spawned from a profile can pin to the exact version it was built against (reproducibility) or auto-resolve to latest (always-current), which fits Shepherd's spawn-from-saved-config model cleanly.
- **Against:** Most of AutoGPT's actual code here is marketplace machinery Shepherd structurally cannot host, and stripping it leaves a much smaller feature than the evidence implies. The store path (store/db.py next-version + supersede-pending + ChangelogEntry, ActiveVersion connect/disconnect, "rejecting the active one falls back to the most recent OTHER APPROVED version") is welded to an approval state machine and per-listing public VISIBILITY — there is no approval workflow, no listing, and no multi-user audience in a single-user local console, so the "approved/active/supersede" half is dead weight (the prompt itself flags it DONT_ADD). The @@id([id,version]) composite key and ActiveVersion FK are Prisma/SQL constructs; Shepherd has no DB — it is flat JSON files (cc-templates.json, cc-presets.json), so the storage design doesn't transfer, only the idea does. There's also a real cost-vs-payoff worry: immutable accumulation needs a cap/GC policy or the JSON files grow unbounded, and a single user editing their own presets often just wants overwrite — full version trees plus an active-pointer UI plus changelog rendering plus pin-vs-track toggles is a lot of surface for a one-person tool, risking the kind of complexity the "OFF BY DEFAULT / keep it pure cc-core" ethos guards against. And it partially overlaps existing capabilities: git already versions cc-config.json (todos notes this re L2), and session lineage already captures spawn ancestry, so naive versioning would be a parallel system if not carefully folded into L1/L3.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch: AutoGPT's versioning is delivered through (b) the multi-user marketplace — approval state machine, ActiveVersion visibility in store listings, supersede-pending, ChangelogEntry, @@id composite keys in Prisma — none of which Shepherd can host (no server, no DB, no users, no listings). That coupled half is correctly DONT_ADD. But the OPERATOR PATTERN underneath — immutable numbered revisions of a saved config with a movable active pointer, a per-version change note, revert-with-fallback, and pin-vs-auto-track — is pure local JSON revision management that needs no executor/credentials/server. It is therefore ADAPT, not ADD (building it as-is would drag in marketplace coupling) and not DONT_ADD (the local need is real). It directly reinforces two already-queued items rather than proposing a parallel system: L1's versions[]? profile field and L3's templatePushVersioned/versions-revert view. Concretely it fixes the in-place overwrite at cc-core.lua:3613 (and the mirror in presetPush). I set reinforces=L1 because the agent-profile registry is the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents saved into a directory") and is where pin-vs-auto-track-latest and the active-pointer add the most operator value (reproducible spawns); L3 is the secondary home for the template side. Confidence high: the mapping is explicit in todos.md and the overwrite bug is concrete in source. Effort M because the pure cc-core logic (versioned push/list/setActive/revert-fallback + cap/GC) is modest and sits beside existing CRUD, but it needs a versions/revert/changelog UI and a pin-vs-track resolver threaded through the spawn path across both registries.
- **How (Shepherd):** Replace overwrite-in-place with append-versioned in both cc-core CRUD families. L3 (templates): add templatePushVersioned(state,name,text,changesSummary) that, instead of cc-core.lua:3613's in-place replace, appends {version=max+1, text, changesSummary, ts} to the named template's versions[] and sets activeVersion (default = new max); add templateSetActive(name,version) to move the pointer to any prior version, and templateRevert(name) that drops/deactivates the active version and falls back to the most-recent-other version (the local, approval-free analog of AutoGPT's supersede-fallback). L1 (profiles): give cc-agents.json profiles the already-specified versions[]? field with the same shape; spawnSpec/the config-by-ref resolver gains a per-profile pin flag — pinned spawns resolve the recorded version, auto-track spawns resolve activeVersion/latest (the is_latest_version analog). UI: a versions/revert + changelog view (changes_summary list) in the profile/template authoring front-end (L1's authoring side); show active vs pinned on the card. Storage stays flat JSON with a per-name cap + oldest-version GC (reuse the TEMPLATE_CAP idiom and the after()/GC discipline). DROP: approval/ActiveVersion-visibility coupling, supersede-pending, store ChangelogEntry table, Prisma @@id composite key and FKs.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not. The double-mismatch only cuts off HALF of this feature. (a) No executor: immutable numbered revisions + a movable active pointer + per-version change note + revert-with-fallback + pin-vs-auto-track is pure JSON data management over inert config records — it runs no graph, executes no block code, holds no execution state. (b) The marketplace half is real and correctly DROPPED — AutoGPT delivers versioning through the store (approval state machine, ActiveVersion visibility in listings, supersede-pending, ChangelogEntry table, Prisma @@id composite keys/FKs), and Shepherd has no server/users/listings to host it. But the feature's value does NOT depend on that surface: strip the store and the operator pattern (numbered immutable revisions, active pointer movable to any prior version, changes_summary, reject-active→fall-back-to-most-recent-other, pin-vs-auto-track-latest = is_latest_version) survives intact and is genuinely useful locally. (c) Not redundant: session lineage tracks ancestry and the ledger is an append-only event log; neither gives immutable revisions of a NAMED config with a movable active pointer + pin/track resolution at spawn. It IS already queued, which is precisely why ADAPT (not ADD): L1 already specifies the versions[]? profile field (todos.md:257-258) and L3 already specifies templatePushVersioned 'duplicate-on-edit/v(n+1), don't overwrite + versions/revert view' (todos.md:399). (d) The Prisma/composite-key part is plumbing and is dropped, but the residue (movable active pointer + pin-vs-auto-track + revert-fallback) is real operator value — it controls which version a 'spawn from agent' reproduces (reproducible spawns). The overwrite-in-place bug it fixes is source-confirmed: cc-core.lua:3613 (templatePush) with the mirror in presetPush at cc-core.lua:3688, both flagged in todos.md:391. Cannot be ADD (building it as-is drags in marketplace coupling); cannot be DONT_ADD (the local need is real and already roadmapped). reinforces=L1 is correct: pin-vs-auto-track-latest + the active pointer add the most value to the agent-profile registry (the user's #1 stated priority — reproducible spawns), with L3 the secondary home for the template side. #### Saved presets / re-run with captured inputs (run a configured agent with one click)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator need is real and directly serves Shepherd's spawn console: a human wants to save "an agent plus a configuration" (folder + provider/model + permMode + a seed prompt with named inputs already filled in) and re-launch it with one click, optionally pre-filled from a prior run. This is the user's #1 stated priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory" — and it sits on primitives Shepherd ALREADY ships: presetPush/presetList/presetRemove (cc-core.lua:3658-3717) are saved spawn configs, presetMarkUsed/presetForProject (3709-3722) already recall a project's last-used editor/mode/provider, and respawnSpec (cc-core.lua:2688) already faithfully rebuilds a finished session's spawn config from its captured fields. So "create preset from a past execution" = generalize respawnSpec's rebuild + presetPush save, and "run-again with captured inputs" = spawn a new session pre-filled from the prior session's spec — both are extensions of working code, not new systems. The named-inputs/merge-with-overrides half lands cleanly on L3's {{var}} render-before-spawn. High operator value, low conceptual novelty, off-by-default-friendly.
- **Against:** AutoGPT's AgentPreset is built around exactly the three things Shepherd structurally cannot host: its InputPresets are per-input rows TAGGED credentials-vs-data (OAuth/secret storage Shepherd forbids), it carries a webhookId for trigger-driven setup, and execute_preset's whole point is to feed inputs into the in-process graph executor (v1.py:1844 execute_graph runs the DAG of blocks). Strip credentials, the webhook, and the graph run and you are not "porting AutoGPT's preset" — you are just describing the saved-spawn-preset Shepherd already has plus L1/L3. There is a redundancy risk: building a parallel "AgentPreset" concept would duplicate cc-presets.json / cc-agents.json. The merge-inputs-with-overrides execution semantics only matter when something consumes the inputs at runtime, which in Shepherd is the Claude Code session itself (via a seeded prompt the human submits), not Shepherd.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: AutoGPT's value here is partly an EXECUTOR feature (execute_preset → execute_graph runs the DAG) and partly a CREDENTIAL-STORE feature (input rows tagged credentials, webhookId trigger). Both are DONT_ADD plumbing. But the residual operator PATTERN — a saved {config + named inputs} you launch with one click, plus deriving that saved config from a prior run — is exactly the management/library pattern that DOES transfer, and Shepherd already has its skeleton (presets + respawnSpec + last-used recall). The candidate's own SHEPHERD note reaches the same conclusion (STRONG ADAPT; drop credentials + graph-execution; keep saved-named-inputs + one-click-launch + derive-from-prior-run). It is not ADD because it must be reshaped (no AgentPreset table, no webhook, no executor) and folded into the existing preset→profile primitive rather than copied as-is. It is not DONT_ADD because, unlike pure executor/credential features, the surviving core is useful AND partly already implemented. So: ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (Agent Profiles registry = preset++ with "spawn from a saved agent" + seedPrompt) with the named-inputs half riding L3. Verdict confidence is high because the mapping is concrete and the drops are unambiguous.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry, cc-agents.json as preset++) rather than a new AgentPreset concept. (1) A Shepherd "preset/profile" = {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, + L3 rendered vars}; saved-named-inputs = L3's {{var}} values stored on the profile, merged-with-overrides at the New Session modal before render. (2) "Create preset from a past execution" = generalize respawnSpec (cc-core.lua:2688) — which already rebuilds editor/cwd/permMode/provider from a finished session — into agentPush/presetPush (cc-core.lua:3673), adding the session's seed/first-prompt; a "Save as agent/preset" action on a done/dead tile. (3) "Run-again with captured inputs" = a tile action that calls respawnSpec → spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061) and pre-fills the modal from the prior session's spec + last-used recall (presetForProject, 3720), human confirms (no auto-execute). All CRUD stays pure in cc-core mirroring presetList/presetPush/presetRemove; effects via fx; gated by spawn.live. DROP: AutoGPT's AgentPreset Prisma table, the credentials-vs-data input tagging + any secret storage, webhookId/setup-trigger, and execute_preset's graph execution (v1.py execute_graph) — Shepherd seeds a prompt the human submits, it does not run a graph.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempts all fail; the ADAPT (high, reinforces L1) ruling holds. (1) "Pure executor" refutation: AutoGPT's execute_preset→execute_graph runs the DAG, but that is only half the feature. The residual operator pattern — a saved {config + named inputs} launched with one click, plus deriving that config from a prior run — needs no execution. Shepherd seeds a prompt the human submits; no graph runs. (2) "Credential-store" refutation: AutoGPT preset rows tag credentials + carry webhookId/trigger, but the named-DATA-inputs + one-click-launch survive without secrets; Shepherd resolves $ENV via the spawned shell. That is reshape+extend = ADAPT, not a no-op DONT_ADD. (4) "Should be ADD" refutation: fails — it can't be copied as-is (no AgentPreset Prisma table, no webhook, no executor); it must fold into cc-presets.json / L1. The judge's L1 placement is corroborated directly by todos.md L1 (profile = preset++ with seedPrompt; "spawn from agent" seeds the session queue with seedPrompt — todos.md:256,259-261) and the named-inputs half by L3 (parameterized templates {{var}} + render-before-spawn + input-schema — todos.md:387-426). #### Folders / nested folder tree for organizing saved agents (+ move/bulk-move/drag-drop)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the cleanest possible AutoGPT->Shepherd transfer: a pure local-data organizing layer with ZERO double-mismatch exposure. It runs no graph, executes no blocks, stores no OAuth creds, serves no users, and touches no marketplace — it's a self-referential table plus a circular-move guard, soft-delete cascade, and a filter on the list query. The user's #1 stated priority is literally "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory" — a directory is exactly a folder. Shepherd already has the structural analogs proving the pattern lands: session GROUPS (a flat cohorting layer over runtime tiles) and the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry (cc-agents.json, the static saved-profile store). Once L1 ships a registry of saved agents, an organizing taxonomy over it is the obvious next need — a flat list of 30+ profiles gets unmanageable, and AutoGPT's LibraryFolder is mature prior art for exactly that (icon+color, nesting, per-folder counts, reassign-to-folder bulk + drag). The AutoGPT impl maps almost 1:1 onto Shepherd's pure-core + JSON-data idiom: the LibraryFolder self-referential table becomes a cc-agent-folders.json tree, `_is_descendant_of` becomes a pure cc-core circular-move guard (trivially unit-testable, like the existing pure logic), and the folder-filter-on-query becomes a pure filter sibling to filterTiles/applyGroups. It's pure local data, no server/executor — it fits Shepherd's architecture without bending a single scope limit.
- **Against:** The feature has no standalone value — it organizes a registry that does not exist yet. L1 (Agent Profiles) is still QUEUED, not built, so this is an enhancement to a future feature, and shipping the organizing tree before the thing it organizes is backwards. Worse, Shepherd's saved-data model is deliberately FLAT and string-keyed: presets, labels, groups, templates are all single-level JSON maps keyed by name or projectKey, with no hierarchy anywhere in the codebase. Introducing a self-referential parent/child TREE — with a circular-move guard, soft-delete cascade, unique-name-per-parent constraints, and counts that must roll up subfolders — is a real complexity jump (it's essentially porting a relational tree onto JSON files) that cuts against the app's "flat operator-data JSON like cc-presets.json" grain stated explicitly in context.md. There is also a naming/concept-collision hazard: the L1 profile schema ALREADY has a `folder` field meaning the launch directory (an absolute fs path), and Shepherd already has session GROUPS; adding a third "folder" concept (an organizing taxonomy) risks operator confusion. For a single user managing a personal handful of saved agents, a flat list plus the existing tile/profile search may be entirely sufficient — nesting + icon + color + drag-drop is marketplace-scale library polish (AutoGPT serves many users' large libraries) that a single-operator console likely doesn't need. The drag-drop / bulk-move sub-features especially are UI-weight with thin payoff at single-user scale.
- **Ruling:** Double-mismatch check: this passes cleanly — it's the management/library pattern with none of the executor/credential/marketplace plumbing. So it is NOT DONT_ADD. But it is also not ADD, because its value depends entirely on the saved-agents registry (L1), which is queued but unbuilt; shipping a nesting tree with no flat registry to nest would be premature, and it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives (pure cc-core logic + a JSON data file, not a Prisma table + FastAPI routes). That is the definition of ADAPT, reinforcing L1. The user's stated priority ("saved into a directory") weighs strongly in favor of the underlying NEED being real. The right move is to fold a lightweight organizing layer into L1's build: start FLAT (the profile schema could carry an organizing-category string, mirroring the existing `group` field on sessions) and only escalate to AutoGPT's full nested tree (icon+color+parent/child+counts+circular-guard+cascade) if the operator's library actually grows enough to need it. AutoGPT's LibraryFolder is the right reference design to copy WHEN that nesting is warranted — _is_descendant_of becomes a pure unit-tested guard, the filter-on-query becomes a pure filter — but as a phase-2 widening of L1, not a parallel system and not a standalone build. Effort is M: the pure tree logic + guard + cascade + counts is modest and testable, but the authoring UI (tree view, recolor, drag-drop, bulk-move dialog) is non-trivial webview work, and it's strictly gated on L1 landing first.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry) as its organizing layer. Phase 1 (ship with L1): add an organizing-category string to the cc-agents.json profile schema — mirror the existing session `group` field (core.setGroup/groupNames/applyGroups at cc-core.lua:341-385) and reuse the filter-chip + filterTiles UX, giving flat folders for free. Phase 2 (only if libraries grow): port AutoGPT's LibraryFolder as a cc-agent-folders.json tree — a pure cc-core module (agentFolderList/Push/Move/Remove) siblings to presetList/agentList; AutoGPT's db.py _is_descendant_of becomes a pure circular-move guard (zero hs.*, unit-tested like the rest of cc-core); soft-delete cascade + per-folder agent/subfolder counts are pure derivations; the folder filter on the agent list is a pure filter beside filterTiles/applyGroups. Reassign-to-folder (bulk + drag) maps to the existing bulk-action pattern (core.selectActionable / BULK_RULES) and the New-Session/profile authoring front-end. CRITICAL: name it distinctly from the profile's existing `folder` field (launch directory) and from session GROUPS to avoid the three-way concept collision. Effects (JSON read/write) go through the fx table; everything organizing is pure + off-the-critical-path.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. I tried to break it both ways and couldn't. Cannot push to DONT_ADD: This passes the AutoGPT double-mismatch test cleanly. (a) No executor: organizing folders for saved profiles run no graph, execute no block code, hold no execution state — it's pure list/tree data. (b) No server/credential/marketplace: AutoGPT's LibraryFolder ships as a Prisma table + FastAPI routes + per-user libraries, but the *pattern* (a tree with parent/child, counts, circular-move guard, soft-delete cascade) is pure data logic that reshapes onto Shepherd's primitives. Not redundant either: I verified Shepherd has session GROUPS (cc-core.lua:341-385: applyGroups/setGroup/groupNames keyed by projectKey, with filter chips) — but those organize RUNTIME sessions, not a saved-profile library. There is no agent-library-folder primitive today. And the user's #1 stated priority is literally "agents... saved into a directory," so the underlying NEED is real, not invented. Cannot push to ADD: Its entire value is parasitic on L1 (the Agent Profiles registry). I confirmed L1 does NOT exist in the code — grep for agentList/agentPush/cc-agents.json/agentFolder returns ZERO hits in cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua; only presetList/presetPush (spawn presets, cc-core.lua:3658+) and session groups exist. Shipping a nested folder tree for a registry that has no entries to nest is premature by definition. It also must be reshaped (pure cc-core CRUD mirroring presetList + a JSON file via the fx table, AutoGPT's _is_descendant_of as a unit-tested pure circular guard), not copied as the Prisma+FastAPI design. Reshape-onto-primitives + gated-on-a-queued-item = ADAPT, not ADD. Two corroborating points for the judge's mapping: (1) The judge's CRITICAL naming warning is genuinely necessary — presetList already carries a `folder` field meaning the LAUNCH DIRECTORY (cc-core.lua:3662), so an organizing 'folder' would be a real three-way collision with that and with session GROUPS; must be named distinctly. (2) The flat phase-1 ('group'-string on the profile schema, reusing filterTiles/applyGroups) is nearly free off the existing group primitive, with the full icon+color+nested-tree+counts+cascade as a phase-2 escalation only if the library grows — which is exactly the lightweight-fold-into-L1 the ruling prescribes. Effort M is fair: pure tree/guard/cascade/counts logic is modest and testable, but the tree-view + recolor + drag-drop + bulk-move webview authoring UI is non-trivial. Strictly gated on L1 landing first. Verdict unchanged: ADAPT (high), reinforces L1.

#### Save / publish a running thing as a reusable definition (authoring wizard) + LLM-drafted profile  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The CREATE side of the saved-agents registry is the user's literal #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory") and is the one gap L1 still half-leaves open. Today a Shepherd operator who tunes a session live (provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills) has no way to capture that working config back into cc-agents.json — they'd hand-edit JSON. AutoGPT's publish wizard is genuine prior art for the exact operator flow Shepherd needs: a 3-step select-the-source -> fill-the-info-form -> review-before-commit progression, ending in a self-confirmation screen. That pattern maps 1:1 onto a "Save this running session as an Agent Profile" wizard in the New Session / detail panel, and the review step is the natural home for L1's already-specced pre-flight validator (validate-keep-valid-drop-bad). Separately, classic AutoGPT's generate_agent_profile_for_task() is a clean, self-contained idea: one function-calling LLM round (create_agent JSONSchema) turns a freeform task string into a drafted role/description/best-practices/constraints. This is genuinely feasible on Shepherd's primitives with ZERO new infrastructure — the local claude CLI (v2.1.175, confirmed at ~/.local/bin/claude) makes exactly one LLM call, no executor, no server, no credential store. It directly populates L1's structured {role, goal, backstory} identity subfields and slashes the friction of authoring a profile from scratch ("describe the job, get a draft you edit"). It fits Shepherd's existing prompt-builder lineage (auditReviewPrompt, improvePrompt) and the "session does the authoring, Shepherd renders + confirms" stance already written into L1's enrichments.
- **Against:** The feature as shipped in AutoGPT is fundamentally a MARKETPLACE feature, and that half hits the double mismatch squarely: PublishToMarketplace.tsx collects a StoreSubmissionRequest, requires a graph-ownership precondition, does next-version numbering against a server-side store, and feeds a publish->review->approve/reject pipeline where OTHER USERS review and consume the submission. None of that can or should exist in Shepherd — no server, no multi-user, no store to publish INTO, no graph to own. If you took the feature literally you'd build a marketplace, which is structurally impossible. Even the LLM-draft half carries risk of scope creep: it adds an LLM round-trip whose output is non-deterministic, which sits awkwardly against Shepherd's "pure deterministic cc-core, zero hs.*" discipline (the call must live entirely in the fx/CLI effects layer, never in cc-core), and it could be seen as redundant with L1's existing "session runs the interview and writes the profile" enrichment — i.e. we may already have a CREATE story and this just adds a second, fancier one. There's also a real failure-mode surface: the claude CLI may be slow/absent/rate-limited, so the draft must degrade gracefully to manual entry or it becomes a flaky dependency on a hot path.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch and the feature cleaves into three parts. (1) The marketplace SERVING plumbing — StoreSubmissionRequest, graph-ownership precondition, remote version numbering, review-by-others, the /store/submissions endpoint — is pure (a)-executor + (b)-multi-user-platform and is correctly DONT_ADD per the candidate's own note. (2) The WIZARD PATTERN — select source -> info form -> review/self-confirm — is operator UX, not graph plumbing; it transfers cleanly. A "graph+version to publish" becomes "a running session (or existing profile) to save", and "review before submitting to the store" becomes "self-confirmation + pre-flight validation before writing cc-agents.json locally." (3) The LLM-DRAFT (generate_agent_profile_for_task) is the strongest transferable nugget: a single function-calling LLM round to synthesize a profile is exactly the kind of thing the local claude CLI can do with no executor/server/creds. Both transferable parts land squarely on L1, whose "Authoring front-end" enrichment (todos.md:327-333) ALREADY names the missing CREATE side ("Save as agent pre-seeds defaults... runs the pre-flight validator") and ALREADY assumes "authoring done by the session, not Shepherd; off-by-default, dry-run behind spawn.live." So this is not a new system — it sharpens L1's CREATE side with two concrete imports: the staged-review wizard shape and the one-shot LLM draft. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence. It is not ADD (the as-is feature is a marketplace, which Shepherd structurally cannot host) and not DONT_ADD (the underlying NEED — capture a working session into a saved, reusable agent profile — is the user's #1 priority and already queued).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's "Authoring front-end" enrichment (todos.md:327-333), adding two concrete pieces. (A) WIZARD: a "Save as Agent Profile" flow in the New Session modal / detail panel mirroring AutoGPT's select->info->review. Step 1 select source = the current running session's resolved config (provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle) OR an existing profile to clone/version; pure cc-core captureProfileFromSession(target) builds a draft record. Step 2 info form = name/folder/{role,goal,backstory}/skills/mcp pickers (reuse L1 pickers). Step 3 review = render the draft + run L1's pre-flight validator (validate-keep-valid-drop-bad, todos.md:323-326) and show a NUMBERED self-confirmation before agentPush writes cc-agents.json (off by default / dry-run behind spawn.live, like other write side-effects). Local-only versions[] reuses session-lineage versioning, NOT remote next-version numbering. (B) LLM-DRAFT: a new prompt-builder draftAgentProfilePrompt in cc-core (sibling to auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt, pure, tested) that renders a freeform task string into a create_agent-style instruction; the actual LLM round runs in the fx/CLI layer via the local claude CLI (one call, --append-system-prompt + structured-output ask) and returns name/description/{role,goal,backstory}/best-practices->backstory-constraints, prefilling the Step-2 form. Degrade gracefully to manual entry if the CLI is absent/slow. DONT_ADD: StoreSubmissionRequest, graph-ownership precondition, remote version numbering, /store/submissions, and the review-by-others publish/approve/reject workflow.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT ruling holds and reinforces L1. The feature cleaves cleanly and the problematic half is already self-DONT_ADD'd by the candidate. Skepticism gates: (a) executor — the marketplace's graph-ownership/version-running is DONT_ADD; the two surviving parts (the select->info->review wizard and generate_agent_profile_for_task) run no graph/blocks/DAG. The LLM-draft is one local `claude` CLI call (v2.1.175 present at ~/.local/bin/claude, supports --append-system-prompt; cc-core.lua:2993 already builds CLI flags) yielding a STATIC profile record — no agent loop. (b) server/creds — surviving parts write a local cc-agents.json; the /store/submissions + review-by-others + remote version numbering are DONT_ADD. (c) redundant/queued — NOT redundant: the existing capture (presetPush/presetList, cc-core.lua:3641) saves only a thin {name,folder,editor,permMode,provider} spawn preset, whereas L1's Agent Profile is far richer ({...skills[],mcpServers[],knowledge[],plugins[],{role,goal,backstory},versions[]}); and L1's 'Authoring front-end' enrichment (todos.md:327-333) explicitly names the missing CREATE side — 'Save as agent pre-seeds defaults + runs the pre-flight validator,' authoring done by the session, off-by-default behind spawn.live. The rubric's own rule ('if an AutoGPT feature matches an already-queued Lx, verdict ADAPT and set reinforces to the Lx') endorses ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. (d) plumbing — the marketplace IS the DB/serving plumbing and is excluded; the wizard + LLM-draft are real operator-console UX. The two concrete imports are genuine: a draftAgentProfilePrompt sibling to the verified pure string-renderers auditReviewPrompt (cc-core.lua:715) / improvePrompt (cc-core.lua:481), and a staged self-confirm-before-write wizard reusing the existing spawn.live dry-run pattern. Not ADD (as-is feature is a marketplace Shepherd cannot host); not DONT_ADD (capturing a working session into a saved reusable profile is the user's #1 stated priority and is queued).

#### Agent detail page / rich definition card (metadata, versions, recommended cron, changelog)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The presentation pattern is pure metadata UI — no graph executor, no block code, no credential store, no server. It maps cleanly onto two things Shepherd already has on deck: L1's saved agent-profile registry (cc-agents.json, whose schema ALREADY carries versions[]) and L5's planned detail-panel tabs. A "profile detail card" (name, folder, provider/model, seedPrompt, skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[], requiredEnv, versions[]) is exactly the authoring/inspection front-end L1 already calls for ("an authoring front-end" + "skills card"), and it directly serves the user's #1 stated priority: pre-configured agents saved in a directory, browsable with their config visible. Shepherd even has a native run-history analog — lineageByProject/lineageSummary already render "session churn made legible" per project, so a per-profile "spawned N times / last used" stat and a session-detail card are derivable from data on hand, not new infrastructure. recommended_schedule_cron lands as a per-profile suggested cadence feeding the emerging scheduled-spawn idea. A per-profile changelog ("what I changed about this preset") is just a local human-edited notes field on versions[], echoing the lineage mindset (no remote object).
- **Against:** Much of AutoGPT's detail page is marketplace chrome that Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host: hero media/agent_video, agent_output_demo, public creator attribution, store run-count as social proof, and the StoreListingVersion join all presuppose the multi-user STORE — the (b) half of the double mismatch. recommended_schedule_cron is only meaningful in a platform that actually RUNS the agent on a schedule; Shepherd doesn't run an agent loop, so the cron is at best a suggestion attached to a not-yet-built scheduled-spawn feature. A "changelog" of graph_versions reflects edits to an executable graph DAG — Shepherd has no graph, so the analog (notes on a spawn preset) is thin and risks gold-plating an L1 card the user may want kept lean. There's also redundancy risk: tile search, groups, relabel, the detail panel, and lineage already give the operator most of the "find and inspect" value, so a heavyweight card could duplicate existing surfaces.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch test: the FEATURE'S worth does NOT depend on running graphs/blocks, executing block code, storing OAuth, or serving a marketplace — strip the store chrome (video, output-demo, public visibility, creator social proof, store run-count, the StoreListingVersion join) and what remains is a read-mostly definition card + a richer session detail view, both of which are management/library/observability patterns the mismatch rubric explicitly says CAN transfer. The transferable core overlaps almost exactly with already-queued work: L1's profile registry (the card IS the missing inspection/authoring face of cc-agents.json, whose schema already declares versions[]) and L5's detail-panel tabs reshape. So this is a textbook ADAPT/reinforce, not a new system and not ADD-as-is. I set reinforces=L1 because the dominant pull is the saved-agent profile card (the user's stated #1 want); the session-detail half folds into L5, and the cron suggestion nudges the emerging "scheduling" idea — but the schema/registry home is L1. Not DONT_ADD: it does not require any of the four structural blockers. Not ADD: the as-is page is steeped in marketplace serving that Shepherd cannot host, and the genuinely useful parts must be reshaped onto L1/L5 primitives. Effort is M (not L): the profile schema, validator family, lineage rollups, and detail-panel scaffolding already exist; this is largely a panel-JS card view + a per-profile notes/versions field + wiring lineage stats, riding L1/L5's pure-core derivations rather than standing up new subsystems.
- **How (Shepherd):** L1 (cc-agents.json): add a per-profile detail/definition card to the authoring front-end — render name, folder, provider/model, permMode, seedPrompt (or role/goal/backstory), skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]/knowledge[]/requiredEnv[] as badges/rows, plus versions[] (already in schema) with an expandable per-version changelog notes field ("what I changed about this preset") edited by the human; categories→Shepherd session GROUPS as badges. recommended_schedule_cron = an optional per-profile string surfaced on the card and offered as a default when wiring the emerging scheduled-spawn. Run-count/last-updated come from a pure derivation over lineageByProject/the ledger keyed to the profile (spawn-from-agent already stamps by:; reuse lineageSummary/cc-core.lua:1098). L5 (detail-panel tabs, cc-core.lua ~#detail reshape): a session "Definition" view showing the resolved profile the session was spawned from (reusing L1's config-by-ref resolver), with the honest "(human-started; not visible to Shepherd)" note for un-spawned sessions — same surface as the planned skills-loaded inspector. DONT_ADD: agent_video/hero media, agent_output_demo, public creator/visibility, store-wide run-count-as-social-proof, the StoreListingVersion/marketplace join, A2A/public agent cards.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT/reinforces=L1 ruling holds. I checked the four downgrade levers and none let it fall to DONT_ADD or rise to ADD. (a) Graph/block executor: the transferable core (a read-mostly card rendering a saved profile's name/folder/provider/model/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[] + a versions[] changelog + a cadence string + a run-count rollup) executes nothing. No DAG, no block code, no execution state. Blocker does NOT apply. (b) Server/multi-user/marketplace/OAuth: the judge correctly amputates exactly the parts that need it — hero video, output-demo, store run-count-as-social-proof, public/creator visibility, A2A agent cards, the StoreListingVersion/marketplace join — all marked DONT_ADD. What remains stores no secrets and serves no one. Blocker does NOT apply. (c) Redundant with shipped / already-queued: this is the strongest lever and it is real but it does NOT yield DONT_ADD. The feature is almost entirely already L1: the profile schema {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], versions[]} is in todos.md L1 verbatim (cc-core.lua presetPush/presetList ~3658-3722 are the preset++ base it extends); 'show role/goal on the profile card' and the 'skills card' are already specced; an 'authoring front-end' (create/inspect face) is already an L1 OpenHands enrichment; the per-session Definition view is L5's 'skills loaded inspector'. BUT the rubric is explicit: when an AutoGPT feature MATCHES already-queued work, the verdict is ADAPT with reinforces=Lx, NOT DONT_ADD. DONT_ADD is reserved for the structural blockers or re-adding a SHIPPED feature. The agent card is queued, not shipped. So redundancy routes to ADAPT, exactly as ruled. (d) Platform/DB plumbing mistaken for a console feature: no — strip the Prisma/StoreListing chrome (the judge does) and the residue is an operator-facing inspection card, a management/library pattern the mismatch rubric explicitly allows to transfer. Net-new over L1 is genuine but modest, which is why it reinforces rather than duplicates: (1) a per-profile human-edited changelog string ('what I changed about this preset') hung off versions[]; (2) recommended_schedule_cron as a per-profile suggested cadence — correctly nudged at the emerging 'scheduling' idea (cline mining), not a new Lx; (3) run-count/last-updated as a PURE derivation that already has its machinery — lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1061) and lineageSummary (cc-core.lua:1098) exist and roll up by projectKey, so this is wiring, not a subsystem. One small correction to the judge's mapping, not enough to overturn: the reasoning says 'spawn-from-agent already stamps by:' — spawn-from-agent does NOT exist yet (L1 is unbuilt; only presets ship, and presets are not lineage-stamped). The run-count rollup would need spawn-from-agent to stamp the profile name first. This makes the effort honest at M (the judge already says M, not L), but does not change the verdict. ADD is wrong (the as-is page is marketplace-serving Shepherd cannot host; the useful parts must be reshaped onto L1/L5). DONT_ADD is wrong (no structural blocker; queued-match routes to ADAPT). ADAPT/reinforces=L1, session-detail half folding into L5, media+public visibility DONT_ADD — the ruling stands.

#### Agent Skills registry (SKILL.md, directory-discovered, progressive disclosure)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is a bullseye for the user's #1 stated priority — "skills saved into a directory" — and it is literally the Anthropic Agent Skills spec (`~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` + YAML frontmatter) that the local `claude` CLI already consumes via `--append-system-prompt`/`--plugin-dir`/`--agents`. The directory-discovery + frontmatter-parse + per-profile attach pattern transfers cleanly to a watch/steer/spawn console: Shepherd can scan the skills dir, parse the frontmatter index (name/description/triggers/tags), render a skills card, let the operator attach skills[] to a saved agent profile, and surface those at spawn time. No executor needed — pure directory read + flag injection, which is exactly Shepherd's primitive. AutoGPT (CoPilot's user-distilled skills + Classic Forge's list_skills/load_skill discovery) is genuine prior art for the saved-skills-as-files registry.
- **Against:** Half the feature is pure executor plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot host: CoPilot's StoreSkillTool/ReadSkillTool/DeleteSkillTool are agent-callable tools inside a running loop; the per-turn `<available_skills>` injection into the first user message, the Level1/Level2/Level3 progressive disclosure, and the `max_loaded_skills` runtime cap all require an in-process agent that constructs the prompt each turn and decides when to load a skill body mid-conversation. Shepherd runs no agent loop and makes no API calls — the native `claude` CLI handles skill auto-loading and progressive disclosure itself, so re-implementing any of it would be both impossible and redundant. The genuinely-new surface beyond what L1 already specifies is near zero: L1 already has the enumerator, frontmatter parse, card, per-profile skills[] toggle, dual-shape globbing, the per-session "skills loaded" inspector expandable to SKILL.md frontmatter, and slash autocomplete over skill names.
- **Ruling:** DOUBLE MISMATCH split is clean. (a) The executor half — StoreSkillTool/ReadSkillTool as in-loop tools, per-turn `<available_skills>` context injection, Level2/Level3 progressive disclosure, max_loaded_skills runtime capping — depends on running the agent loop and constructing prompts each turn. Shepherd does neither and the local `claude` CLI already does skill auto-loading natively, so that half is DONT_ADD/redundant. (b) The management/library half — a directory of SKILL.md folders, frontmatter as a metadata index, list/read, and attaching skills to a saved agent profile — is exactly Shepherd's read-dir-and-inject-at-spawn primitive and exactly the user's stated #1 interest. That half is already fully specified inside L1 (todos.md:266-267 skills enumerator + FX.listSkills; 307-310 dual-shape glob + /<name> command + shape on card; 315-319 per-session skills-loaded inspector expandable to SKILL.md frontmatter; 320-322 per-profile skill toggle rows; 311-314 slash autocomplete). It is queued, not shipped (no listSkills/SKILL.md references exist in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua yet). So the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L1, NOT a new parallel registry — the candidate corroborates and slightly sharpens L1 (the frontmatter field set name/description/triggers/license/allowed-tools/author/version/tags can extend the card's parsed fields, and the Level1-metadata-always/Level2-body-on-demand framing maps to "show frontmatter on the card, expand to full body on click" in the inspector — a UI affordance, not an executor feature).
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds entirely into L1's skills card. Concrete plug-in points: (1) `FX.listSkills` globs both directory-shape `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and flat `skills/*.md`, parsing YAML frontmatter (name, description, triggers, tags, version, author, license, allowed-tools) into a Level1-style metadata index for the card (todos.md:266-267, 307-310). (2) Per-profile `skills[]` toggle rows on the card add/remove skills from the editing agent profile (todos.md:320-322). (3) At "Spawn from agent", the config-by-reference resolver dereferences skills[] against `~/.claude/skills` and `spawnFlags` emits `--append-system-prompt`/`--plugin-dir`/`--agents` (todos.md:259-262, 295-298), with safe-path name validation `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` rejecting traversal (todos.md:302-303). (4) The per-session "skills loaded" inspector renders each attached skill expandable from frontmatter (Level1) to full SKILL.md body (Level2) on demand — the progressive-disclosure UI affordance, honestly labeled "what Shepherd attached at spawn" (todos.md:315-319). EXPLICITLY DROP the executor half: StoreSkillTool/ReadSkillTool/DeleteSkillTool, per-turn `<available_skills>` injection, max_loaded_skills runtime cap, and mid-loop Level2/Level3 loading — the native claude CLI owns those.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and FAILED — the ADAPT ruling holds. I checked the codebase: cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua contain ZERO skill/listSkills/SKILL.md references, so the skills card is queued in L1, not shipped (no redundancy-with-shipped basis for DONT_ADD). And todos.md L1 (lines 247-334) already specifies the candidate near-verbatim: FX.listSkills globbing ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md and parsing frontmatter into a card (266-267), dual-shape enumerator with /<name> command (307-310), per-session "skills loaded" inspector expandable from frontmatter to full SKILL.md body (315-319), per-profile skills[] toggle rows (320-322), slash autocomplete (311-314), and config-by-ref resolve + --append-system-prompt/--plugin-dir/--agents at spawn (259-262, 295-298) with safe-path validation [A-Za-z0-9._-] (302-303). Per the harness rule "if an AutoGPT feature matches a queued Lx, verdict ADAPT, reinforces Lx" — that is exactly this case, so it cannot be downgraded to DONT_ADD. The three usual refutation levers all fail: (a) the executor half (StoreSkillTool/ReadSkillTool/DeleteSkillTool, per-turn <available_skills> injection, max_loaded_skills runtime cap, mid-loop Level2/Level3 loading) needs the agent loop + per-turn prompt construction, which Shepherd does NOT do — but the judge ALREADY explicitly drops it, and todos.md:234-235 confirms the native claude CLI does skill auto-loading itself, so that half is correctly DONT_ADD/redundant, not smuggled in; (b) the marketplace/CoPilot-store/delete/server half maps to L1's explicit DROP list (todos.md:275-279: /v1/skills proxy, zip/Bytes storage, SkillVersion, marketplace endpoints) — no server/OAuth/multi-user survives; (c) not platform plumbing mistaken for a console feature — the surviving half is genuinely a local read-dir-and-inject-at-spawn affordance for the user's #1 stated priority ("skills saved into a directory"). The candidate adds essentially nothing new beyond L1 (even the extra frontmatter fields license/allowed-tools/author/version/tags are a trivial card-field extension atop the already-parsed name/display_title/description at todos.md:235), which is precisely the definition of ADAPT reinforcing L1 — corroborate and slightly sharpen, not a new parallel registry. ADAPT (high) reinforces L1 stands.

#### Fan-out matrix runs (same task across N configs in parallel) + result ledger with resume  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The fan-out half is a real, high-value operator need that Shepherd has ALREADY committed to: L1's "Batch fan-out spawn" bullet (todos.md:299-301) is verbatim the same pattern — "fan out one session per row, queue seeded, gated by spawn.live, by:'batch' ledger event, aggregate per-session usage into insights." AutoGPT's "run the same task across N config/model/effort/profile presets in parallel and compare" is the same shape on a different axis (a config matrix rather than input rows), and MODEL_PRESETS/STRATEGIES is direct prior art for L1's saved agent-profile presets and providers — which lands squarely on the user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory). The CompletedRun record {config, task, success, cost, steps, runtime, error, completed_at} is almost exactly Shepherd's audit-ledger / shift-report field set, so the result-ledger half drops onto an existing primitive with no new system. And "resume by skipping completed keys" reinforces Shepherd's already-shipped drain/respawn/auto-continue idempotency ("don't re-spawn finished work"). This sharpens L1 from "fan out" toward "fan out across a preset MATRIX and compare outcomes side-by-side on the board," which is a genuinely useful extension a watch/steer/spawn console wants.
- **Against:** The feature's center of gravity is the BENCHMARK HARNESS, which is pure double-mismatch (a): a challenges library with task + ground-truth should_contain/should_not_contain, file/python/llm evaluators, and maintain/improve/explore classification by historical pass record. All of that requires an in-process EXECUTOR to run each challenge to completion AND score its output — Shepherd runs no agent loop and produces no output artifact it could grade; it only reads status files and steers GUI sessions a human/Claude already drives. The asyncio-semaphore parallel matrix executor is likewise an engine Shepherd structurally is not (spawning is gated dry-run, sessions are GUI processes, not awaited coroutines with results). "Crash-resilient resume" presumes a long-lived run with persisted intermediate state to resume INTO — Shepherd has no such run; a closed session is gone. So a literal port adds an executor + scoring layer Shepherd cannot host, and the genuinely-transferable parts are already queued under L1 + the existing ledger — risking a redundant parallel system rather than net-new value.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch by splitting the feature. (1) Fan-out matrix execution: the OPERATOR pattern (spawn the same task into several sessions with different presets, compare) transfers and is already L1's "batch fan-out spawn" — extend that bullet's row-axis to also accept a preset/provider/effort MATRIX axis (Cartesian of selected agent profiles x input rows), still pure row/matrix expansion in cc-core, effects via fx, gated by spawn.live, by:"batch" ledger event. MODEL_PRESETS = L1 agent profiles; STRATEGIES = seed-prompt/policy variants. (2) Result ledger: maps onto the existing JSONL audit ledger + shift report + insights — record {config/profile, task, outcome, cost, steps, runtime, error, completed_at} per spawned session; aggregate into the shift report's "by config" breakdown (a small enrichment of fleetStats). (3) Resume/skip-completed: reinforces drain/respawn idempotency, not a new resume engine — at most a "skip rows already spawned in this batch" guard so a re-run of an interrupted batch doesn't double-spawn. (4) DONT_ADD the scoring harness: challenges + ground-truth eval + python/llm evaluators + pass-record classification all need the executor to run and grade outputs — Shepherd compares outcomes HUMAN-judged on the board/insights, never auto-scored. Net: ADAPT, reinforcing L1, with the matrix-axis and the per-config shift-report breakdown as the concrete new increments; explicitly drop the benchmark/scoring engine.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1's "Batch fan-out spawn" (todos.md:299-301) to add a MATRIX axis: select K agent profiles/presets (MODEL_PRESETS analog: provider x model x effort x permMode x seedPrompt/policy variant) and optionally N input rows (L3 render) → Cartesian expand to K*N spawn specs → fan out one GUI session each with its queue seeded, gated by spawn.live (dry-run default), each tagged with the {profile,row} cell. Pure expansion in cc-core (sibling to row-split), effects via fx. Ledger: emit by:"batch" events carrying the cell config; enrich the shift report / fleetStats with a per-config (per-profile) outcome breakdown reusing the existing JSONL ledger fields {success, cost via token tracking, steps, runtime, error-reason (L5 taxonomy), completed_at}. Resume: a "skip cells already spawned/finished in this batch" idempotency guard layered on the existing drain/respawn discipline (context.md:86-89) — do not build a resume engine. The tile grid is already the side-by-side comparison board; insights/sparklines are the compare view. DROP: the challenge library, ground-truth should_contain/should_not_contain eval, file/python/llm evaluators, and maintain/improve/explore pass-record classification (all need the executor — DONT_ADD).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails — the ADAPT(high)/reinforces-L1 ruling holds. The feature is a clean double-mismatch split, and the transferable half survives every disqualifier I tested. (1) Fan-out MATRIX is an OPERATOR pattern, not executor work. Shepherd's spawn pipeline is already pure: spawnSpec/spawnFlags/presetList/templateList/respawnSpec (cc-core.lua:2688,3001,3061,3590,3658) are zero-hs.* functions. A K-profile x N-row Cartesian expansion is the exact same shape as the existing row-split — a sibling in cc-core, effects via fx, gated by spawn.live (dry-run default), tagged by:"batch". It does NOT need the graph/block executor: each cell spawns a GUI Claude Code session that Claude Code drives; Shepherd only generates specs and watches status files. No server/multi-user/OAuth surface is touched. MODEL_PRESETS = L1 agent profiles; STRATEGIES = seed-prompt/policy variants. (2) Not fully redundant. L1's "Batch fan-out spawn" (todos.md:299-301) today is ROW-axis only (N input rows, one profile). The preset/provider/effort MATRIX axis is a real unbuilt increment, correctly framed as extending that L1 bullet rather than a parallel system. (3) Result ledger maps onto existing JSONL ledger + fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) + shift report. fleetStats aggregates bySession, NOT by config/profile, so a "per-config breakdown" is a small genuine enrichment reusing the provenance/by: machinery already present (cc-core.lua:996). The per-run record {config,task,outcome,cost,steps,runtime,error,completed_at} is exactly Shepherd's audit-ledger / shift-report field set. (4) Resume/skip-completed reinforces existing drain/respawn idempotency (context.md:86-89), at most a "skip cells already spawned in this batch" guard — not a new resume engine. (5) The scoring half is correctly DONT_ADD: challenges + should_contain/should_not_contain ground-truth + python/llm evaluators + maintain/improve/explore pass-record classification all require the executor to RUN and GRADE outputs. cc-core.lua:1128 explicitly states Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth and judges HUMAN-on-the-board, never auto-scores. Net: ADAPT, reinforcing L1, with the matrix axis + per-config shift-report breakdown as the concrete increments and the benchmark/scoring engine explicitly dropped. I could not refute it.

#### Saved-agent profiles / portable definition files (the canonical 'saved agents in a directory')  `[H/L]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the single strongest operator-pattern match in the whole AutoGPT corpus and a direct hit on the user's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." The transferable core is pure local data + management, not graph execution. Classic AutoGPT's AIProfile (ai_name, ai_role, ai_goals) + AIDirectives (constraints, resources, best_practices) is the cleanest prior art for Shepherd's saved-profile shape: it is persisted as plain state.json under .autogpt/agents/{id}/, reloadable, CLI-overridable, and — crucially — AIDirectives.__add__ implements the exact append-vs-replace directive-merge that L1 needs for "override seedPrompt/policy at spawn." LibraryAgent contributes a battle-tested flag set (isFavorite/isCreatedByUser/isHidden/isArchived/isDeleted, folderId) and the "list both created and saved" surface that directly enriches Shepherd's "My Agents" card and folder grouping. Graph templates prove the portable, versioned, self-contained definition-file pattern (and secrets-stripped-on-export, which aligns with Shepherd's no-secrets scope). AgentManager's enumerate/list/load-by-id maps to a pure agentList over cc-agents.json. Mechanically the fit is exact: cc-core already has presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (3658-3720), spawnSpec (3061), spawnFlags (3001), providerById (2632) — the profile is a superset of the existing preset, CRUD'd the same way, with zero hs.* in the pure layer.
- **Against:** Most of AutoGPT's concrete evidence is the half Shepherd structurally cannot host and L1 already explicitly drops: graph_templates instantiate into nodes/links/sub_graphs run by the block executor; LibraryAgent is a Prisma row joining a user to a graph+version inside a multi-user FastAPI/Next.js platform with a store/marketplace; from_db, list_library_agents routes, and the import/instantiate flow all assume server-side persistence and serving. None of that transfers — Shepherd has no graph, no executor, no DB, no users, no marketplace. So if one took the candidate "close to as-is" (graph templates + LibraryAgent registry + instantiation pipeline), it would be DONT_ADD plumbing. There is also a redundancy risk: Shepherd already has spawn presets + providers + live /model + permission-mode, so a naive read could call the registry redundant. And this is already queued as L1 with three independent corroborations (LiteLLM/crewAI/OpenHands) — adopting AutoGPT as if it were a new feature would spawn a parallel system instead of reinforcing the existing design.
- **Ruling:** Double-mismatch applied: AutoGPT's value here splits cleanly. The MANAGEMENT/LIBRARY half (a directory of saved, persisted, reloadable, overridable agent definitions, with flags and folders) transfers directly and is exactly what the user wants. The EXECUTOR/MARKETPLACE half (graph templates that instantiate into a runnable DAG, multi-user LibraryAgent rows, store import) is structurally impossible in Shepherd and is already on L1's explicit DROP list. Verdict is ADAPT, not ADD, because Shepherd does not yet have a saved-agent registry but HAS queued this exact thing as L1 — so the right move is to fold AutoGPT in as a fourth corroborating source, not propose a parallel feature. It is not DONT_ADD because the local-data core (cc-agents.json + cc-mcp.json, spawn-from-agent, skills/MCP attach) sits entirely within Shepherd's primitives and needs no graph/executor/credential-store/server. AutoGPT's distinctive net-new contributions over the existing L1 spec are: (1) the append-vs-replace directive merge (AIDirectives.__add__) as the canonical override semantic for profile-at-spawn; (2) the LibraryAgent flag set (favorite/hidden/archived/created-by-user) + folderId to enrich the card/organization layer; (3) secrets-stripped-on-export as the explicit serialization rule for any future profile export — all consistent with no-secrets scope.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry) in todos.md. Concretely: (a) cc-core CRUD agentList/agentPush/agentRemove over cc-agents.json mirroring presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (3658-3720), profile = superset of preset {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt/{role,goal,backstory}, policyBundle, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], requiredEnv[], versions[]}. (b) Adopt AIDirectives.__add__ as the spawn-time override semantic: profile carries base directives; New-Session modal can append-or-replace before spawnSpec/spawnFlags emit --append-system-prompt + --mcp-config + --agent. (c) Add LibraryAgent-derived flags (isFavorite/isHidden/isArchived/isCreatedByUser) + folderId to the profile schema and the "My Agents" card for filtering/grouping; "list both created and saved" = enumerate cc-agents.json entries plus skills enumerated from ~/.claude/skills. (d) Any export action strips $ENV refs (Shepherd already resolves $ENV via shell, never stores values), matching graph templates' stripped_for_export. DROP from AutoGPT: all Prisma tables, the graph-template instantiation/executor path, multi-user routes, marketplace import — already on L1's DROP list.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT ruling in both directions and could not. The refutation paths fail: DOWN to DONT_ADD (redundant-with-queued) fails — but only barely. todos.md L1 (lines 247-334) is already EXACTLY this feature: cc-agents.json + cc-mcp.json, pure agentList/agentPush/agentRemove mirroring the live presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (verified at cc-core.lua:3658-3717), the profile schema {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt/{role,goal,backstory}, policyBundle, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], requiredEnv[], versions[]}, spawn-from-agent threading --mcp-config/--append-system-prompt/--agent, the skills card, and it ALREADY absorbs three independent corroborating sources (LiteLLM/crewAI/OpenHands). So this isn't redundant in the "we already shipped it" sense — it's the still-unbuilt #1-priority queue item. The right verdict for an exact match to a queued Lx is ADAPT (fold in as a corroborating source), not DONT_ADD. AutoGPT does contribute genuine, scope-clean net-new refinements over the current L1 text: (1) the append-vs-replace directive-merge semantic (AIDirectives.__add__) as the canonical profile-at-spawn override — L1 renders {role,goal,backstory} into --append-system-prompt (line 282) but never spells out append-vs-replace; (2) the LibraryAgent flag set (isFavorite/isHidden/isArchived/isCreatedByUser) + folderId organization layer on the My-Agents card — L1 only has a per-skill "hide from picker" toggle (line 321), not a favorite/archive/folder layer; (3) secrets-stripped-on-export as the explicit serialization rule (consistent with Shepherd already resolving $ENV via shell and never storing values — context.md:13). These are real but small. UP to ADD fails — it is not a parallel net-new feature; it is structurally the queued L1, so it must reinforce L1, not stand alone. ADD->ADAPT is correct. The AutoGPT double-mismatch splits cleanly and the DROP half is already on L1's explicit DROP list (lines 275-279): Prisma tables, graph-template instantiate-into-runnable-DAG, multi-user LibraryAgent rows, marketplace/store import, OAuth credential storage. The keeper half (a local directory of saved, reloadable, spawn-time-overridable JSON profile definitions) sits entirely inside Shepherd's primitives — no graph/block executor, no server, no credential store, no marketplace. Classic AutoGPT's AIProfile (ai_name/ai_role/ai_goals) + AIDirectives persisted as state.json, reloadable and CLI-overridable, is genuine prior art for the persona+directives shape and matches the user's stated #1 priority verbatim. Verdict: ADAPT (high), reinforces L1. Concrete mapping: (a) add agentList/agentPush/agentRemove over cc-agents.json mirroring cc-core.lua:3658-3717; (b) adopt append-vs-replace as the spawn-time directive override in the New-Session modal before spawnSpec/spawnFlags emit --append-system-prompt/--mcp-config/--agent; (c) add the favorite/hidden/archived/created-by-user flags + folderId to the profile schema and My-Agents card for filter/grouping; (d) strip $ENV refs on any export action. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1, lines 247-334), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (preset CRUD, 3658-3722), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/context.md (no-secrets scope, line 13).

#### Destructive-action confirmation gate (dependent-count warning + force)  `[M/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The transferable kernel is a real operator-safety pattern Shepherd does NOT yet fully have: "this delete affects N still-active dependents — confirm or pass force." Shepherd already confirms Close/Clear/Compact (native submenu) and bulk-queue ("Queue N tasks?"), but none of those count or surface live dependents before the destructive act. The cleanest home is the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry: `core.agentRemove` over `cc-agents.json`. Deleting a saved agent profile that running sessions were spawned from, or that an L2 policy bundle / L3 template / L1 MCP record references, is a footgun — a pre-remove pure scan ("3 active sessions reference this profile; remove anyway?") plus a `force` flag, and a "protected" guard for the last/default profile or one bound to a live drain, is exactly the AutoGPT shape minus the plumbing. It also generalizes to closing a session with a non-empty queue (warn "N queued tasks will be lost"), clearing a group, or removing a cwd-keyed label active sessions inherit. It is pure, deterministic, cc-core-testable (a `core.deleteImpact(item, fleet) -> {dependents, protected}` sibling to the existing validators), and off-by-default friendly — a small, well-fitting UX hardening of an already-queued feature.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's value lives in the half Shepherd structurally cannot host: the `force` path REVOKES the OAuth provider token and the "system/managed credential" protection guards a credential store. Shepherd has NO secrets storage (hard scope limit — it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell) and no providers-as-stored-credentials, so the credential-deletion semantics, token revocation, and managed/system protection are DONT_ADD outright. What remains — "warn with a dependent count, require force" — is a thin, generic UX convention Shepherd ALREADY embodies for its real destructive ops (Close/Clear/Compact behind native-submenu confirms; the bulk-queue confirm dialog; the drain mechanism that lets a session finish its turn). The dependent-COUNT refinement is nice-to-have, not a missing capability, and risks over-engineering: Shepherd profiles are local JSON the operator can re-create in seconds, so a deleted profile is far lower-stakes than a revoked OAuth token — the original feature's gravity does not survive the port. The double mismatch fires on the load-bearing half (credential store), leaving only a minor confirm-UX note.
- **Ruling:** DOUBLE-MISMATCH CHECK: the candidate straddles both AutoGPT halves. The token-revocation + system/managed-credential protection is part (b)'s OAuth credential store — DONT_ADD (Shepherd: NO secrets storage, NO stored providers-as-credentials). The remaining operator pattern (warn-with-dependent-count, require explicit force on a destructive delete) is a pure MANAGEMENT/LIBRARY convention with no graph/block/credential/multi-user dependency, so it CAN transfer. Net: half DONT_ADD, half ADAPT — the prompt's own "ADAPT (weak)" read is correct. It is NOT a fresh primitive: Shepherd already does destructive confirms (Close/Clear/Compact submenus, the confirmbar at claude-dashboard.lua ~L3030, the "Queue N tasks?" confirm at ~L3400) and graceful drain (core.shouldDrainClose). What's genuinely new and worth adding is the DEPENDENT-COUNT step before the confirm, and its natural attach point is the queued L1 registry's delete path (core.agentRemove over cc-agents.json, todos.md L254) rather than a parallel system — hence reinforces=L1 (with a minor spillover applicability to L6 rules and to session-close/group-clear). Confidence medium: the need is real and the fit is clean, but the stakes are low (local re-creatable JSON, not revocable secrets), so it's an enhancement of L1's CRUD, not standalone.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 as a delete-safety step, NOT a new feature. Add a pure cc-core function `core.deleteImpact(target, fleet, registries) -> { dependents = {kind, key, label}[], protected = bool, reason }` sibling to the existing agent/preset/template validators (cc-core.lua ~3590-3722). On "Remove agent" in the registry UI: if dependents > 0 (running sessions spawned from this profile, or L2 bundles / L3 templates / cc-mcp.json records that reference it), show the existing confirmbar (claude-dashboard.lua ~L3030) reading "N active dependents reference this — remove anyway?" requiring an explicit confirm = the `force` analog; refuse outright if `protected` (last/default profile, or one bound to a live drain). Reuse the same primitive for two spillovers: (1) closing a session with a non-empty queue → "N queued tasks will be lost"; (2) clearing a group / removing a cwd-keyed label that active sessions inherit. DROP entirely: OAuth token revocation, system/managed-credential protection, anything touching a credential store (no secrets in Shepherd). Keep deterministic + unit-tested in cc-core; effects only render the count.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and FAILS — the ADAPT verdict survives (though it's weak, not medium). The feature splits in two. The credential half (revoke the provider OAuth token on delete + protect system/managed credentials) is squarely AutoGPT mismatch (b): Shepherd has NO secrets/credential store (context.md hard scope limit), so that half is correctly DONT_ADD and the judge already drops it. The surviving operator half — "warn that this delete affects N still-active dependents; require explicit force" — carries NONE of the double-mismatch disqualifiers: no graph/block executor, no credential store, no server/multi-user/marketplace. It is a pure management/library convention that transfers onto Shepherd primitives, so I cannot honestly knock it down to DONT_ADD. I checked the strongest refutation angle (c, redundancy) and it only partially lands: Shepherd ALREADY has destructive-confirm UX — the confirmbar with startClose/commitClose (claude-dashboard.lua L3571-3577, bar markup L3030), the "Queue N tasks (one per line)?" confirm (L3400), and graceful drain via core.shouldDrainClose (cc-core.lua L2092). So the confirm-before-destroy + force-analog primitive is not new. What IS genuinely new is the DEPENDENT-COUNT step shown before that confirm, and it has a real local use: removing an L1 saved profile that running sessions were spawned from, or closing a session whose queue would lose N tasks. I verified its natural home is unbuilt — agentRemove/agentList/agentPush return 0 hits in cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua; the L1 cc-agents.json registry is entirely QUEUED (todos.md L254), and today's presetRemove (cc-core.lua L3697) is a plain no-op-on-miss delete with no dependent gate. Net: this is an enhancement of queued L1's CRUD delete path (reinforces=L1, with minor spillover to closing a session with a non-empty queue and to group/label clear), not a fresh primitive and not a parallel system — exactly the "ADAPT, set reinforces to Lx" shape. The credential/marketplace/server plumbing is correctly dropped. The verdict tier (ADAPT) is right; I'd downgrade the judge's strength from medium to weak (most of the original AutoGPT feature's substance is the credential half = DONT_ADD; the surviving sliver is small, low-stakes — local re-creatable JSON, not revocable secrets — and partly overlaps existing confirms), but that is not a verdict change. Refutation to DONT_ADD does not hold.

### Reinforces L6  (8)

#### Event-trigger / webhook blocks + per-event-type subscription filter + trigger-payload contract  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The OPERATOR pattern underneath is real and Shepherd already has the substrate for it. AutoGPT's event_filter_input (a boolean map of event toggles) + is_triggered_by_event_type() (match an incoming event against the selected filter at fire time) is, stripped of HTTP, exactly a "pick WHICH event kinds fire this automation, then re-check the match each tick" selector. That is the missing UI for L6's rule registry, whose trigger.kind (done|error|hung|approval|starved) is today just a bare enum with no front-end. Shepherd's local event vocabulary already exists as session statuses (idle/working/approval/done/error in cc-core.lua, plus hung/starved clocks), and the level-triggered dispatcher already re-evaluates predicates (shouldFeed ~1975, shouldAutoContinue ~2200, isHung ~2375) every ~1s refresh — that IS the "match at fire time" loop.
