The second strong half — "the validated payload becomes the trigger node's input" — maps cleanly onto L3 render-before-spawn: a fired rule seeds an L3 template with the local event's fields ({{event.title}} approximates {{prev_output}}/{{repo}}: sessionKey/projectKey/error-reason/last_prompt), giving the L6 to L3 to L4 chain (rule fires, render template, inject/route). It also lands squarely on the user's stated priority (saved agents + templated handoff). Net: a small multi-select-of-local-event-kinds widget on a rule is a genuinely cleaner UI than L6's current bare enum.
- **Against:** The literal feature is mostly the half of AutoGPT Shepherd structurally cannot host — the double mismatch hits hard. BlockType.WEBHOOK presumes (a) the graph/block EXECUTOR: BlockWebhookConfig, the mandatory payload INPUT on a node, typed block OUTPUTS unpacking PR/issue/sender, and router nodes_input_masks={node.id:{payload}} are all node/graph plumbing — Shepherd runs no graph and executes no blocks. And (b) the multi-user web platform: setup_webhook_for_block REGISTERS a subscription with a provider over OAuth, an inbound FastAPI HTTP RECEIVER accepts the event, and signatures get verified — Shepherd has NO network server, NO credential store, and makes NO API calls (hard scope limits in context.md). So the thing that gives this feature its name and most of its code (receive an external HTTP event, validate a provider payload, hold OAuth subscriptions) is precisely DONT_ADD. There is also a redundancy risk: the GitHub-triggers mining is ALREADY captured (todos.md:436-439 "DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth ... the spawned session owns that"), so adopting this wholesale would re-propose a parallel system L6 already owns.
- **Ruling:** Clean double mismatch with a clear seam. The inbound HTTP receiver, OAuth subscription registration/setup_webhook_for_block, signature verification, BlockWebhookConfig/block-schema, mandatory payload-input node, typed block outputs, and nodes_input_masks router injection are all graph-executor + multi-user-server + credential-store plumbing — Shepherd is structurally none of those, so all DONT_ADD. What transfers is the management/trigger PATTERN: a per-rule multi-select of LOCAL event kinds (the EventsFilter boolean-toggle shape) plus match-at-fire-time, which is the missing selector UI for L6's existing trigger.kind enum and re-uses the per-tick predicate loop verbatim. The payload-as-input contract reshapes onto L3 (event fields seed a render-before-spawn template), giving L6 to L3 to L4. This is the AutoGPT corroboration of GitHub-triggers mining already recorded at todos.md:436-439, so the honest verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L6 (not ADD, since the server/blocks are excised; not DONT_ADD, since the filter-selector UI genuinely improves L6 and matches the user's saved-handoff priority).
- **How (Shepherd):** Add to L6 (cc-rules.json): change trigger.kind from a single enum to a kinds[] multi-select (the EventsFilter boolean-toggle shape) over Shepherd's LOCAL event vocabulary {done, error, hung, approval, starved} — sourced from session statuses in cc-core.lua and the existing hung/starved clocks; a pure matchesTrigger(rule, event) predicate evaluated each ~1s refresh alongside shouldFeed (~1975)/shouldAutoContinue (~2200)/isHung (~2375) — that is is_triggered_by_event_type at fire time. Render the multi-select chip group in the rules editor of the webview JS twin. Map the payload-as-input contract onto L3: when a rule fires, expose the local event's fields (sessionKey, projectKey, error-reason, last_prompt) as render vars so a feed/spawn processor seeds an L3 template ({{event.kind}}/{{event.reason}} approximates {{prev_output}}), wiring L6 processor.feed to routeTask to L4. Lifecycle/outcome already covered by L6's by:"rule" ledger event. Excised (DONT_ADD): the HTTP receiver, OAuth subscribe/setup_webhook_for_block, signature verify, BlockWebhookConfig, the payload-input node and typed block outputs, nodes_input_masks.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on all four downgrade paths; ADAPT (reinforces L6) holds. (a) Executor: the judge already excised the inbound HTTP receiver, BlockWebhookConfig, payload-input node, typed block outputs, is_triggered_by_event_type's schema validation, and nodes_input_masks router injection — all graph/block-executor plumbing. The kept part (a multi-select of event kinds + match-at-fire-time predicate) needs none of it; Shepherd runs no graph. (b) Server/OAuth/marketplace: OAuth subscribe/setup_webhook_for_block, signature verify, and the HTTP receiver are explicitly DONT_ADD; the retained selector runs purely over Shepherd's LOCAL filesystem event vocabulary — no server, no creds. (c) Redundant/queued was the strongest angle, but it FAILS as a DONT_ADD: I verified L6 is still queued (no cc-rules file, no rules editor, no by:"rule" ledger event in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua) and its spec at todos.md:550 declares trigger.kind as a SINGLE enum {done|error|hung|approval|starved}. The EventsFilter contributes a genuinely-absent refinement: single-enum -> kinds[] multi-select chip group, mapping is_triggered_by_event_type onto a pure matchesTrigger(rule,event) predicate evaluated alongside the existing per-tick predicates shouldFeed (cc-core.lua ~1975), shouldAutoContinue (~2200), isHung (~2375). That is reinforcement, not redundancy — which is exactly ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. (d) The selector half is real operator-console surface (a chip group in the rules editor); only the plumbing half is platform machinery, and it was correctly cut. The payload-as-input contract maps cleanly onto L3: L3 already specs renderTemplate/{{var}}/{{prev_output}} (todos.md ~394/~411), and the local event fields exist (last_prompt from cc-status.sh ~509, error .reason via planned classifyError ~512, projectKey/sessionKey as core item fields) — so 'event payload becomes a render var' needs no typed-output schema. The ADD->ADAPT downgrade is the right call (value is real but folds into queued L6), consistent with the GitHub-triggers mining already recorded at todos.md:436-439. Verdict: hold ADAPT, reinforces L6.

#### Inbound webhook ingress + auto/manual webhook registration + payload validation/HMAC + ping/test-fire  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** Triggers are exactly the operator pattern Shepherd is moving toward: "when an external event happens, spawn/act on a session." Shepherd already has the SPAWN half (presets, providers, routing v1) and a queued rule engine (L6) whose processor catalog (feed/continue/nudge/relabel/log) is literally a trigger's action side. The candidate also ships a genuinely transferable, NOT-yet-captured kernel that the rubric tells me to weigh favorably: the TEST-FIRE / dry-run button — simulate a trigger with a synthetic event and show what WOULD spawn/act, fully locally, no Redis/provider. That rides Shepherd's existing dry-run primitive (spawn.live / ORCH_DRY_RUN, cc-core.lua:3141) and L6's rule→spawn mapping, and it makes the otherwise-invisible automation rules legible and safe to enable — a real operator win on a watch/steer/spawn console. HMAC-verify-before-acting is even philosophically echoed by Shepherd's nonce-bound gate, so the "validate the event before you act on it" instinct already has a home.
- **Against:** The feature's CORE VALUE is the network-ingress server, and that is a clean triple violation of Shepherd's hard scope. (1) It needs a public HTTP server with a reachable PLATFORM_BASE_URL — Shepherd has NO network server and reads status off the local filesystem. (2) Auto-setup hits provider REST APIs (POST /repos/{repo}/hooks, setWebhook) using STORED OAuth credentials plus a GENERATED HMAC SECRET it must persist — Shepherd has NO secrets storage (it only resolves $ENV via the spawned shell) and stores no creds. (3) The ping endpoint blocks on a Redis pub/sub event bus — Shepherd runs no message bus and no agent loop. This is the AutoGPT platform-half double mismatch in its purest form. The repo has ALREADY adjudicated this exact surface: todos.md:438 says "DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth, and the branch/commit/push executor — the spawned session owns that." Building the ingress/registration/HMAC/ping plumbing would mean standing up server + secret store + bus that the architecture explicitly forbids, with no local source of these external events anyway.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature. The bulk — POST ingress endpoint, auto/manual provider registration, HMAC/secret-token validation, Redis-backed ping — is DONT_ADD: it requires a network server, a public PLATFORM_BASE_URL, OAuth-credential storage, a generated HMAC secret to persist, and a Redis event bus, each of which directly violates Shepherd's stated hard limits (no server, no multi-user, no secrets). The repo has already explicitly dropped this surface (todos.md:437-438) and recorded "Shepherd has no firing engine; the level-triggered dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine" (todos.md:448). So I do not propose a parallel webhook subsystem. But the candidate names a real, transferable kernel the rubric directs me to weigh favorably and that is NOT yet captured by name: the TEST-FIRE / dry-run UX — synthetic-event simulation showing what a rule WOULD spawn/act, locally. The rule→spawn mapping itself is already L6 (the event-callback rule engine), so the right verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L6: fold a "test-fire this rule" affordance into L6 using the existing spawn.live/ORCH_DRY_RUN dry-run path and the dispatchSerialized delivered-callback, with synthetic trigger events of the L6 trigger kinds (done/error/hung/approval/starved). Net: the network/credential/HMAC/registration/ping plumbing is dropped; the small dry-run-test-fire affordance becomes a feature of L6.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a "Test-fire" / dry-run affordance to the queued L6 event-callback rule engine (todos.md:544). When a rule (cc-rules.json) is selected in ⚙ Settings, a button injects a SYNTHETIC trigger event of the rule's trigger.kind (done|error|hung|approval|starved) for a chosen scope (projectKey|sessionKey) into the SAME pure predicate path (shouldFeed ~1975 / shouldAutoContinue ~2200 / isHung ~2375 and the L6 predicates) but routed through spawn.live=false / ORCH_DRY_RUN (cc-core.lua:3141) so the processor (feed/continue/nudge/relabel/log) prints what it WOULD do — which session(s) it would spawn/act on — without firing. Surface the simulated outcome in the 📜 Audit overlay as a by:"rule" dry-run row. No HTTP ingress, no provider registration, no HMAC secret, no Redis ping — the "validate before acting" instinct is already covered by the nonce-bound gate. Pure simulator lives in cc-core beside the L6 predicates; webview button + injection in claude-dashboard.lua.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT(high)-reinforces-L6 ruling holds. The bulk of the feature (POST /ingress endpoint, auto/manual provider webhook registration via GitHub/Telegram APIs, X-Hub-Signature-256/secret-token HMAC validation, generated-secret persistence, Redis-pub/sub ping) is correctly DONT_ADD and the judge already drops it: each requires a network server, a public PLATFORM_BASE_URL, secrets storage, and an event bus — direct violations of Shepherd's hard limits (context.md:13 "no network, no multi-user, no secrets ... reads session status off the local filesystem"). The repo already dropped this exact surface (todos.md:438-439 "DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth") and recorded "Shepherd has no firing engine; the level-triggered dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine" (todos.md:448). Grepping cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua for webhook/HMAC/Redis/PLATFORM_BASE_URL returns nothing — and the judge proposes building none of it. What survives is a genuine, not-yet-named transferable kernel the rubric directs me to weigh favorably: the TEST-FIRE / dry-run UX (simulate a trigger, show what WOULD spawn/act, locally, with a synthetic event — no Redis/provider). The judge maps it onto primitives that ALL verifiably exist: L6 the event-callback rule engine (todos.md:544) with its trigger.kind enum done|error|hung|approval|starved and cc-rules.json; the dry-run chokepoint ORCH_DRY_RUN (claude-dashboard.lua:115) + spawn.live (line 1553) which already prints 'would spawn ... without launching' and ledgers a dryRun flag (lines 1557-1562); the pure predicates shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975), shouldAutoContinue (2200), isHung (2375); and the 📜 Audit overlay which already renders a '(dry-run)' suffix (claude-dashboard.lua:4644) and supports by:"router"/by:"rule" tagging. This is an extension/reinforcement of already-queued L6, not a new parallel system, and it genuinely improves the local single-user console (preview what a rule would do before arming). It does not depend on graph/block execution, credential storage, a server, or a marketplace. No grounds to downgrade to DONT_ADD (the test-fire kernel is new and not redundant with anything shipped) nor to upgrade toward ADD (it is plainly a reinforcement of L6). The HMAC verify-before-acting instinct is already echoed by the nonce-bound gate. Ruling stands at ADAPT.

#### Declarative trigger-source/provider registration (pluggable trigger sources)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The architectural pattern beneath the webhook plumbing is real and directly improves L6. AutoGPT's value here is NOT the WebhooksManager or the provider/credential wiring — it is the *shape*: a declarative registry that maps a trigger-SOURCE name to a small handler, consulted at evaluation time, so adding a new event source is a registered block instead of a core edit. Shepherd today hard-codes one branch per local event source: shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975), shouldAutoContinue (:2200), isHung (:2375), queueStarved (:2075), transcriptError (:1588). L6 as currently specced already inherits this rigidity — its rule trigger.kind is a CLOSED enum (done|error|hung|approval|starved). The candidate pushes one level deeper: make the SET of trigger kinds itself extensible. Each Shepherd event source is filesystem-derived and reduces to a pure detector (prev, cur, ctx) -> event[]; registering those by name (status-file change, hook event, git-state change, timer/clock) and having L6's rule resolver dereference trigger.kind against that registry — exactly like AutoGPT's get_webhook_manager consults the provider->manager map — turns L6 from a fixed-enum rule engine into an extensible one. This reuses L1/L3's name->resolver and the validator family L6 already plans to share, so it is nearly free design guidance that future-proofs the highest-leverage queued item.
- **Against:** The literal feature is deep in DONT_ADD territory and the candidate's own assessment concedes the provider/credential machinery is DONT_ADD. A WebhooksManager exists to receive HTTP POSTs at an ingress endpoint, verify signatures, and resolve per-provider OAuth credentials — Shepherd has NO network server, NO ingress, NO credential store (all three are HARD SCOPE LIMITS in context.md), so 90% of what ProviderBuilder().with_webhook_manager().build() does cannot exist here. What remains — "a registry that maps a name to a handler" — is a generic software pattern, not a transferable AutoGPT feature; Shepherd already has registry-of-named-things three times over (providers, presets, the planned cc-rules.json). The risk is over-engineering: L6's five-kind enum covers every event source Shepherd can actually observe today, and a pluggable-source abstraction with no second real consumer is speculative generality that violates the off-by-default, thin-layer ethos. There is also no new local event source on the roadmap that the closed enum blocks. So this is at most a one-line design note on L6, not a feature, and a purist could call it DONT_ADD as redundant with L6 + the existing registry pattern.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the webhook/WebhooksManager/OAuth-credential/HTTP-ingress half is structurally impossible in Shepherd (no server, no creds, no multi-user) — DONT_ADD. But judge the OPERATOR pattern, not the plumbing: the transferable kernel is "declarative, registered trigger SOURCES, looked up at evaluation time." That is precisely L6's weak spot — L6 currently bakes trigger.kind as a closed enum over Shepherd's hard-coded detectors. The candidate's contribution is to make the set of trigger kinds a name->detector registry (status-file change, hook event, git-state change, timer), so L6 becomes extensible rather than enum-bound, reusing the same name->resolver + validator family L6 already shares with L1/L3. This is light, architectural design guidance for an already-queued item, not a standalone build and not a new system — the textbook ADAPT/reinforces case. It is NOT scheduling (the timer source is one possible registered source, but the candidate's substance is the registry mechanism, not a scheduled spawn), so it reinforces L6, not the emerging scheduling idea. Confidence is high because the candidate, the verdict guidance, and L6's own text all converge.
- **How (Shepherd):** Refine L6 (cc-rules.json rule engine) so the rule's trigger.kind is resolved against a small registered SOURCE table rather than a closed enum. Concretely: define a pure cc-core registry of event-source detectors, each (prev, cur, ctx) -> event[], registered by name and evaluated on the 1s refresh tick alongside the existing shouldFeed (:1975) / shouldAutoContinue (:2200) / isHung (:2375) / queueStarved (:2075) / transcriptError (:1588). Seed it by wrapping those existing predicates as the first registered sources (done/error/hung/approval/starved), then allow new local sources to be added as small handler blocks: status-file change (cc-status/<id>.json diff), hook event (a Claude Code hook writing a marker file), git-state change (reuse FX.gitRoot), timer/clock tick. L6 rules dereference trigger.kind via this registry; reuse L1/L3's name->resolver + the validator family L6 already plans to share, and emit a by:"rule" ledger event on fire. Effects stay behind fx; everything off by default. Explicitly DROP the WebhooksManager/ProviderBuilder/HTTP-ingress/OAuth-credential machinery (no server, no creds — context.md scope limits).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT holds, reinforcing L6. Walked the double mismatch and the four common-error grounds against the actual code and backlog: (b) server/creds half — the strongest knock, but only a HALF-kill the judge already applied. The literal ProviderBuilder().with_webhook_manager()/load_webhook_managers/HTTP-ingress/OAuth-credential machinery is structurally impossible in Shepherd (context.md:13 "No network, no multi-user, no secrets") and the mapping explicitly DROPS it. The harness's own rule is to judge the operator pattern, not the plumbing — so this DONT_ADDs the plumbing, it cannot force the whole verdict to DONT_ADD. (a) executor — fails. The transferable kernel is a registry of pure (prev,cur,ctx)->event[] detectors run on the existing 1s tick. That is exactly Shepherd's current model: shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975), shouldAutoContinue (:2200), isHung (:2375), queueStarved (:2075), transcriptError (:1588) are already pure predicates evaluated each refresh. No graph, no block code, no execution state. (c) redundant/already-queued — the decisive ground, and it lands as a REFINEMENT of L6, not redundancy. L6 (todos.md:550) bakes trigger as kind:done|error|hung|approval|starved — a literal closed enum. The candidate's substance is to make that set a name->detector registry so new LOCAL sources (cc-status diff, hook-event marker file, git-state change via FX.gitRoot, timer) become small registered handlers instead of enum branches. That is L6's actual weak spot, and it reuses the L1/L3 name->resolver + validator family L6 already says it shares (todos.md:555-556). So ADD->reinforce, not a parallel system. (d) plumbing-as-UI — fails. The webhook/provider half is plumbing and is correctly stripped; what remains is pure cc-core trigger logic, exactly where L6 lives. Not scheduling: the timer is merely one possible registered source; the candidate's substance is the registry mechanism, so it reinforces L6's extensibility, not the emerging scheduled-spawn idea. Off by default, by:"rule" ledger event, effects behind fx — consistent with Shepherd's invariants. Verdict: ADAPT, reinforces L6.

#### Edge-triggered threshold alerts with once-until-reset de-dupe  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The two design patterns the candidate isolates are exactly the right discipline for an alerting console, and Shepherd has alert surfaces that are NOT yet edge-triggered on every metric. The candidate's own insight is correct: (1) fire-only-on-crossing avoids spamming the operator every 1s tick when a condition persists, and (2) once-until-reset de-dupe means a stuck condition pings once, not continuously. Shepherd's ~1s refresh loop plus ntfy push make naive level-triggered alerts a real foot-gun. The cleanest concrete win is the usage substrate: Shepherd already tracks per-session context fill (usageState.lastContext, the context-fullness mini-bar) and the official plan-usage window (5h/7d utilization %), but there is NO "notify when context first crosses 90%" or "plan window first crosses 80%" alert. Adding a threshold-crossing rule there reuses code Shepherd already has (the escalated/alerted carry-forward dedupe at claude-dashboard.lua:5530-5568) and gives the operator a genuinely new heads-up. The pattern also belongs in the queued L6 event-callback rule engine as a first-class condition type ("when context% crosses N -> notify/compact").
- **Against:** Shepherd has already independently implemented BOTH patterns the candidate claims are transferable, so most of it is redundant. The approval-escalation path (claude-dashboard.lua:5530-5547) fires once on the crossing via `if not nowEsc then ... nowEsc=true` and resets when the condition clears (`if it.status~="approval" then nowEsc=false`) — that IS edge-trigger + once-until-reset. The stuck-session watchdog (5552-5568) does the same with the `alerted` flag, re-armed on transcript progress via applyProgress (cc-core.lua:2401) and cleared by watchdogShouldReset (cc-core.lua:2415) — cc-core literally documents this as "once per stall, not once per working stint", precisely the Redis-NX-until-reset semantic in pure Lua with no Redis. The auto-respawn path is even labeled "edge fired" (cc-core.lua:2146). So the abstract lesson is already learned and shipped. And the entire money/credits/zero-balance/Redis substrate (InsufficientBalanceError, ZERO_BALANCE, INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS_NOTIFIED_PREFIX/TTL) is a clean double-mismatch DONT_ADD: Shepherd has no billing, no credits, no per-user balance, and no Redis (no server, single-user, in-memory state). There is no balance to cross.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the candidate ships inside AutoGPT's multi-user billing engine (credits, per-user balance, Redis SET NX dedupe across processes). Shepherd has none of that substrate — no money, no Redis, no users, no server — so the literal feature is DONT_ADD. The candidate itself concedes this and pivots to two pure design patterns. The verdict turns on whether those patterns are net-new to Shepherd: they are NOT. Shepherd already implements edge-trigger + once-until-reset twice (approval escalation at dashboard.lua:5530; stuck watchdog at dashboard.lua:5552 + cc-core.applyProgress/watchdogShouldReset), with the in-memory nowEsc/alerted carry-forward serving as the exact functional analog of the Redis NX-key-until-reset. The only sliver of new value is extending the same crossing+dedupe to the usage metrics Shepherd tracks but does not yet alert on (context-% per session, official plan window %). That is a small extension onto existing primitives, not a new system, and it slots naturally into the queued L6 event-callback rule engine as a threshold-crossing condition type rather than spawning a parallel alert framework. Hence ADAPT reinforcing L6, not ADD (would duplicate shipped logic) and not DONT_ADD (the usage-threshold heads-up is a real, fitting gap). Confidence high: the existing dedupe/edge code is explicit in the source and the billing substrate is unambiguously absent.
- **How (Shepherd):** Do NOT rebuild the dedupe/edge engine — reuse the existing carry-forward dedupe pattern (the nowEsc / watchdog.alerted flags, re-armed/reset on condition clear) already at claude-dashboard.lua:5530-5568 and cc-core.applyProgress/watchdogShouldReset. Net-new piece: add threshold-crossing alert(s) on the usage subsystem that already exists — per-session context fill (usageState.lastContext + the context-fullness bar) and the official plan-usage window (5h/7d %). Add config-driven thresholds (e.g. context.alertFraction, plan.alertPct) with an in-memory once-until-reset flag per session keyed like the watchdog (fire when the value first crosses up, clear when it drops back below / on /clear / on respawn). Route the fire through the SAME path the escalation/watchdog alerts use: a ledgerFor event (feeds the 🔔 notification history), optional FX.playSound, optional FX.push(ntfy). Expose it as a first-class condition in the queued L6 event-callback rule engine ("when context% crosses N -> notify | compact | nudge") so threshold rules are declarative rather than hard-coded. Drop entirely: InsufficientBalanceError, ZERO_BALANCE/LOW_BALANCE, credits/shortfall, Redis SET NX + TTL prefix.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute, but the ADAPT ruling holds — the source confirms every load-bearing claim. (1) The literal feature is correctly DONT_ADD: no billing substrate exists in Shepherd — grep for redis/credit/balance/shortfall/InsufficientBalance/SET NX finds nothing (the lone "credits the gap" hit at cc-core.lua:1254 is a time-bucket accounting verb, not money). No Redis, no users, no server. (2) The two transferable patterns (edge-trigger + once-until-reset) are already shipped TWICE, exactly as the judge cites: approval escalation at claude-dashboard.lua:5530-5547 uses a carried `nowEsc` flag set-once-on-cross and reset-on-non-approval; the stuck watchdog at claude-dashboard.lua:5552-5568 plus cc-core.lua `applyProgress` (2401) and `watchdogShouldReset` (2415) sets `w.alerted` once per stall, re-arms on transcript progress, clears on reset — a precise in-memory analog of an NX-key-until-reset. Both fire through the same ledgerFor + FX.playSound + FX.push path. Because these patterns are NOT net-new, the verdict cannot be ADD (that would duplicate shipped logic). (3) But it cannot be DONT_ADD either, because there IS a real, fitting gap: usageState (claude-dashboard.lua:319-386) tracks per-session context fill (lastContext) and the official plan-usage window, yet nothing alerts when those values first cross a threshold. L6's queued trigger taxonomy (todos.md:550) is done|error|hung|approval|starved — it has no context%/plan% crossing condition, so a threshold-crossing usage alert is genuinely net-new value that slots into L6 as a new declarative condition type rather than a parallel alert framework. That is the definition of ADAPT reinforcing L6. I could not find a basis to downgrade.

#### Triggered preset (saved agent + event/cron binding + pause/resume toggle)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The operator pattern at the core — "bind a saved agent config to a trigger, store its inputs so each event re-runs the same setup, and flip it on/off with a pause/resume toggle, with a list of what's configured" — maps cleanly and almost 1:1 onto primitives Shepherd already has or has queued. A LibraryAgentPreset is Shepherd's L1 agent profile (cc-agents.json, the user's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory"). The event/cron binding + is_active flag + "inactive presets skipped at ingress" is exactly L6's already-designed rule registry, whose records carry status(ACTIVE|DISABLED) (= the pause/resume toggle) and a trigger taxonomy {done|error|hung|approval|starved} evaluated each ~1s refresh tick — DISABLED rules simply aren't evaluated, the local analog of skipping inactive at ingress. "Stored trigger-node inputs re-merged per event" is L3's parameterized templates (render-before-spawn, stored vars). The cron/recommended_schedule_cron/next_scheduled_run piece reinforces the emerging "scheduling" idea (auto-spawn-on-starvation already exists as the only time-ish trigger; a scheduled spawn is a natural sibling). The routes (setup/update/list/delete) are CRUD over a JSON registry — the same shape as presetPush/presetList/agentPush. So the union {saved profile + trigger + active flag + stored inputs + on/off + a configured-triggers list} is genuinely in-scope and high-value for a watch/steer/spawn console: it turns ad-hoc spawning into "this saved Claude Code agent fires on this local event, and I can pause it."
- **Against:** The feature's distinctive value as shipped in AutoGPT depends on exactly the two things Shepherd structurally cannot host. (1) The webhook half — webhookId FK, setup_trigger creating/linking a real webhook, ingress receiving an external payload at router.py:681, and delete pruning a dangling webhook — requires a network server receiving inbound HTTP, which Shepherd's hard scope limit forbids (NO network server). todos.md already pre-emptively dropped this exact thing ("DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth"). (2) The ingress merge-and-execute ("merge preset.inputs with the live payload and EXECUTE") is the in-process graph executor running a graph version — Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no graph; it watches status files and steers a GUI session Claude Code itself drives. Graph-version pinning and credential/OAuth plumbing the candidate's DONT_ADD list itself flags are likewise unhostable. If you strip those, what remains (a saved profile + an on/off flag + an event rule) is already fully covered by L1 + L6, so a purist could call the residue redundant rather than a new feature — i.e., there's no net-new primitive here, only a recombination, and the recombination is mostly L6 with an L1 reference.
- **Ruling:** Classic AutoGPT double-mismatch split. The candidate is a composite object whose transferable kernel — a saved agent config bound to a trigger with a pause/resume toggle and stored inputs — is real, in-scope, and aligned with the user's stated priority, but whose load-bearing AutoGPT plumbing (inbound webhook server, ingress-merge-and-EXECUTE the graph, graph-version pinning, OAuth/credential store) is precisely what Shepherd's hard scope limits forbid (no server, no graph/block executor, no secrets). Verdict is ADAPT, not ADD, because none of it can be built close to as-is, and not DONT_ADD because the underlying NEED maps onto existing/queued primitives rather than requiring an unhostable subsystem. It reinforces L6 most directly (the rule registry already carries status ACTIVE|DISABLED = the pause/resume toggle, a trigger kind, and per-tick evaluation = the ingress-skip-inactive behavior), with L1 supplying the saved-profile target, L3 the stored/re-merged inputs, and the cron piece reinforcing the emerging "scheduling" idea (set reinforces=L6 since that's the primary anchor and the schema asks for one). The one genuinely new surface vs. today's L6 is making the trigger target a saved L1 profile + stored inputs (rather than only inline processors like nudge/feed/relabel) and adding a time/cron trigger kind alongside the event kinds — a modest extension of L6's processor catalog and trigger taxonomy, not a parallel system. Drop entirely: webhookId/setup_trigger/webhook-prune, ingress payload execution, graph-version pinning, OAuth/credential storage.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L6's rule registry (cc-rules.json) to let a rule's processor target a saved L1 agent profile and spawn-from-agent (processor:{kind:"spawn", agent:<L1 name>, inputs:<stored L3 vars>}), reusing the L1 config-by-ref resolver + spawnSpec/spawnFlags and L3's render-before-spawn for the stored inputs. The is_active/pause-resume toggle = L6's existing status(ACTIVE|DISABLED) self-mutating field (persisted via fx like routePending/alerted markers); DISABLED rules are not evaluated each refresh tick = the local analog of "skip inactive at ingress." Add a cron/time trigger kind to L6's trigger taxonomy {done|error|hung|approval|starved}+{cron} (the "scheduling" idea), evaluated by a pure cc-core predicate beside shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/isHung against the wall clock; lifecycle/firing emits a by:"rule" ledger event. CRUD routes -> pure agentPush/agentList-style CRUD over cc-rules.json with a "configured triggers" list view in the panel. EXPLICITLY DROP: webhookId FK / setup_trigger / inbound webhook server / dangling-webhook prune (no network server), ingress merge-and-execute the graph (no executor), graph-version pinning, recommended_schedule_cron stored remotely / OAuth / credential storage.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT ruling and could not; it holds, reinforcing L6. Verified against the actual repo (cc-core.lua, todos.md, context.md), the four common-error tests all confirm ADAPT rather than break it: (a) Executor? No. The candidate's "each event re-runs the same config" maps to spawn-from-a-saved-profile, which is steering an external Claude Code GUI session — NOT running a graph or executing block code. The judge correctly DROPS "ingress merge-and-execute the graph." Shepherd already spawns sessions (core.spawnSpec at cc-core.lua:3061, spawnFlags at :3001); a rule firing a spawn reuses that, no engine. (b) Server/multi-user/OAuth/marketplace? The load-bearing AutoGPT plumbing IS exactly this: inbound webhookId server, dangling-webhook prune, OAuth/credential store, graph-version pinning. The judge explicitly drops every one of these. Confirmed Shepherd has no network server / no secrets (context.md hard limits). The transferable kernel survives without any of it. (c) Redundant / already-queued? This is the real test of skepticism, and it is where ADAPT (not ADD, not DONT_ADD) is exactly right. The kernel decomposes cleanly onto existing/queued primitives: saved profile = L1 Agent Profiles + the already-SHIPPED spawn presets (cc-presets.json); event rule + active flag = L6's queued rule registry (cc-rules.json), whose spec ALREADY carries status(ACTIVE|DISABLED) = the pause/resume toggle and a trigger taxonomy + per-tick evaluation = the ingress-skip-inactive behavior; stored inputs = L3 render-before-spawn. So it can't be ADD (nothing builds close to as-is) and the judge's reinforces=L6 with L1/L3 supporting is accurate. But it is NOT pure redundancy / DONT_ADD either, because there is a genuine, modest delta beyond today's L6 spec: (1) a NEW processor kind that targets a saved L1 profile and spawns (L6's current processor catalog is only feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log), and (2) a NEW cron/time trigger kind — verified ABSENT from the codebase: L6's trigger taxonomy is {done|error|hung|approval|starved} with no time/wall-clock trigger, and grep found no cron/schedule/webhook primitive in cc-core (only an unrelated step-scheduler and os.date formatting). That cron delta is the one piece that prevents collapse to DONT_ADD and is precisely the "scheduling" idea the schema note says to fold into L6 rather than spin up as a parallel system. (d) Platform/DB plumbing mistaken for a console feature? The DB/FK/network parts (webhookId FK, setup_trigger route, webhook prune) are correctly stripped; what remains (CRUD over a local JSON rule file + a "configured triggers" list view in the panel) is genuine operator-console surface, matching Shepherd's agentPush/agentList CRUD style. The fit question resolves YES: binding a saved local Claude-Code spawn profile to a local event-or-time trigger with an on/off toggle and stored inputs directly improves the local single-user console's watch/steer/spawn loop, and aligns with the user's stated priority (pre-configured saved agents we hand work off to). Net: the AutoGPT plumbing is unhostable and dropped, the kernel maps onto L1+L3+L6 with two small real extensions to L6, so ADAPT (reinforces L6) is correct and survives refutation. Files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L6 at line 544, L1 at 247, L3 at 387), cc-core.lua (spawnSpec/spawnFlags, shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/isHung predicates the cron predicate would sit beside), context.md (hard scope limits).

#### Operator watchdog: stuck/late-run detection + error-grouping/masking  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The OPERATOR pattern here is exactly Shepherd's job: a background monitor that notices sessions wedged too long and tells the human, then summarizes the fleet. Shepherd already has every primitive this needs — the stuck-session watchdog (cc-core.lua:2375 isHung + :2362 approvalStale), the ntfy notification history, and pure fleet aggregation (insights at cc-core.lua:896, shift report at :1122). Three concrete upgrades transfer cleanly and add real value: (1) DISTINCT thresholds per state — today isHung covers "working too long" and approvalStale covers "waiting on you too long," but there's no "queued/idle too long" alarm; AutoGPT's QUEUED-threshold-vs-RUNNING>24h split is the right shape to add a third, idle-rot threshold. (2) A DIGEST instead of N separate pings — "N sessions stuck, here are the worst (counts by status, sample IDs)" rolls up beautifully on the existing insights aggregation and prevents notification spam on a busy fleet. (3) The standout: error-sample MASKING + similar-error GROUPING ("these 5 sessions failed for the same reason") is genuinely additive — it's the missing analytic layer on top of the queued L5 error-reason taxonomy, clustering recurring failure reasons across the fleet so the human sees one root cause instead of five red tiles. All of it is local, read-only over status files + ledger, off by default, ntfy not Discord.
- **Against:** Most of the AutoGPT IMPLEMENTATION is the wrong half of the double mismatch. LateExecutionMonitor polls a shared multi-user execution DB by status/window and fires Sentry + Discord_SYSTEM_alert (operator-of-a-SaaS framing); block_error_monitor runs per-block SQL aggregation across all users' runs; accuracy_monitor is explicitly marketplace quality (week-over-week correctness drop of published agents). None of that DB/SQL/per-block/marketplace plumbing exists or can exist in Shepherd — no server, no execution table, no blocks, no multi-user, no store. There is also a real redundancy risk: Shepherd ALREADY watches for stuck sessions and ALREADY has notification history, so the danger is bolting on a parallel monitor instead of extending what exists. The per-block-failure-rate and marketplace-accuracy-drop monitors specifically must be dropped outright — they presuppose blocks and a marketplace Shepherd structurally cannot host. So this is not an ADD: taken literally it's mostly DONT_ADD, and only the reshaped operator patterns survive.
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch verdict: the value is in the OPERATOR/run-history pattern (watch runs, notice the stuck ones, alert + digest, cluster failures), not in the graph/block executor, the multi-user DB, or the marketplace — so it's ADAPT, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. Shepherd maps "execution stuck" -> "session stuck," and it already has the watchdog + ntfy + ledger aggregation to host all three transferable pieces. The cleanest home is L6 (the queued event-callback rule engine: a declarative when-session-stuck->notify rule), with the grouping/masking analytic riding on the queued L5 error-reason taxonomy. I picked L6 as 'reinforces' because the candidate's own SHEPHERD note maps it to L6 and the core deliverable is the when-stuck->alert automation; L5 is the dependency the grouping sits on, noted in the mapping. Explicitly excluded: per-block failure-rate SQL and marketplace accuracy-drop — both require blocks/marketplace Shepherd cannot have (DONT_ADD parts). Category is Executions & Monitoring: this is fleet run-monitoring + alerting. Effort M, not S, because the idle/queued third threshold + a roll-up digest formatter are modest extensions of existing pure aggregation, but the similar-error clustering + PII masking is real new logic (and depends on L5 landing first, which is why it's not L).
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing watchdog, do not add a parallel monitor. (1) Add a third, state-specific threshold alongside cc-core.lua isHung (working-stall) and approvalStale (waiting-on-you): an "idle/queued too long" timer for sessions sitting unworked past a bound — three distinct thresholds {queuedIdle, working-stall, approval-stale}, all off by default. (2) Add a fleet DIGEST formatter on top of the existing insights aggregation (cc-core.lua:896) / shift report (:1122): "N sessions stuck — counts by status, worst offenders, sample IDs," delivered once via the existing ntfy notification-history path instead of one ping per tile. (3) Land the masking + similar-error grouping as the analytic layer ON the queued L5 error-reason taxonomy: cluster sessions in error state by normalized/masked reason ("these 5 failed for the same reason"), surfaced in insights and in the stuck digest. Wire the trigger declaratively as an L6 rule (when-session-stuck -> notify) with a result entry in the automation ledger. Keep it pure in cc-core (new threshold predicate + digest/cluster builders), effects via the existing fx ntfy path. EXCLUDE the per-block failure-rate SQL and the marketplace accuracy-drop monitor entirely.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute; the ADAPT verdict holds. Checked all four refutation traps against the code: (a) Executor? No. The transferable pieces are pure descriptive analytics over the status files + the opt-in JSONL ledger — exactly Shepherd's existing model. isHung (working-stall, cc-core.lua:2375), approvalStale (:2362), queueStarved (:2075), fleetStats (:954), fleetStandup/mkShiftReport (:1137/:1204) are all pure, zero hs.*, zero model cost. The masking/clustering is string normalization, not graph/block execution. The genuinely-executor/DB parts (per-block failure-rate SQL, marketplace accuracy-drop) were ALREADY carved out by the judge as DONT_ADD. (b) Server/multi-user/marketplace? Stripped correctly. "Affected users" collapses to nothing in single-user Shepherd; the marketplace accuracy-drop monitor is excluded. Delivery is via the existing ntfy notification-history path (notificationEvents, cc-core.lua:787), not Discord/server. (c) Redundant / already-queued — the strongest angle, and it nearly downgrades two of three sub-parts but not the whole: - "Distinct thresholds per state": LARGELY REDUNDANT. Shepherd already has three state-specific thresholds — working-stall (isHung), waiting-on-you (approvalStale), AND queued-work-waiting (queueStarved, with its own `minutes` threshold + sinceTs clock at :2075). The feature's "queued too long" maps almost exactly onto the existing queueStarved. So this is reinforce/relabel, not new. - "Digest": THIN. mkShiftReport already emits Escalations/Stalls(hung)/queue_starved counts + by-project worst-offenders. A stuck-specific roll-up is a small extension of existing pure aggregation. - "Error-sample masking + similar-error grouping": the ONE genuinely-new piece, and it explicitly rides on the L5 error-reason taxonomy (core.classifyError) which I confirmed does NOT exist yet — only queued (todos.md:512-516). This is a real reinforcing extension of a queued item, with real local value ("these 5 sessions failed for the same reason"), so it is NOT pure DONT_ADD. (d) Platform/DB plumbing mistaken for a console feature? The per-block SQL + marketplace are exactly that — and already excluded. Net: I could not flip this to DONT_ADD (the clustering/masking is real, absent, and locally useful) nor to ADD (it's clearly an extension of the watchdog + L5/L6, not net-new). One honest quibble with the judge's mapping: the substance lands more on L5 (the taxonomy the clustering depends on) and on the existing watchdog/queueStarved/shift-report than on L6 — L6 only supplies the optional declarative when-stuck->notify wiring. But that's a reinforce-target nuance the judge already acknowledged (they name L5 as the dependency and chose L6 because the candidate's own note maps to L6 and the core deliverable is the alert automation). A mapping nuance is not a verdict flip. ADAPT (reinforces the existing watchdog + ntfy history + queued L5 taxonomy, with L6 as the trigger layer) is correct.

#### Browser/OS push completion alert with away-detection + click-to-jump + badge  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** This is almost surgically on-target for Shepherd's core job: tell the human a session is DONE, especially when they're not looking, and let them click straight to it. The operator pattern survives the double mismatch cleanly because none of its value depends on running the graph, executing block code, OAuth creds, or multi-user serving — it's pure "notify-on-state-change + navigate." Shepherd already owns every primitive it needs: FX.push (ntfy), FX.playSound, a setNotifyBadge unseen-count badge on the ☰ button, fx.focusWindow + the per-event Jump button + ⌘⌥J/⌘⌥N jump hotkeys, and focus.popOnComplete that already fires on a fresh `done` transition. The candidate adds three genuinely-missing refinements: (1) AWAY-DETECTION as a suppression gate — today escalation/pop fire regardless of whether you're already staring at that exact session, so a completion alert for the tile you're actively watching is noise; suppressing it is a real UX win; (2) completion is NOT currently a notification type (NOTIFY_TYPES = escalation/hung/auto_respawn/auto_continue only) so "response is ready" alerts and a completed-while-away count are net-new and clearly useful for an unattended fleet; (3) click-to-navigate-to-the-session is already proven valuable (the drawer Jump + jump hotkeys exist) and extending it to the alert itself is consistent.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate's literal mechanism is either already present or structurally inapplicable, which risks it being a redundant re-skin. Shepherd already has ntfy push, sound, a notification badge with an unseen count, popOnComplete, and click-to-jump — so the "browser notification + title badge + audio + click-to-navigate" headline is largely DONE, just keyed off different events. The AutoGPT-specific plumbing (Web Notification API, WebSocket copilot_completion stream, Notification.permission gating, cross-tab dedup via completedSessionIDs) is all DONT_ADD: Shepherd is single-process, single-webview, no server, no tabs — there is nothing to dedup across and no Web Push to request permission for; on macOS this is hs.notify/ntfy. The only truly novel atom is away-suppression, and even that is a small refinement to existing escalation/pop logic, not a feature. There's also a real noise risk: `done` is the highest-frequency transition in a busy fleet, so a per-completion OS notification (vs the current quiet badge) could become spammy fast and must be off-by-default and ideally batched.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch is passed: the value is in watch/steer (notify-the-human-on-completion + jump), not in graph/block execution, credentials, or multi-user serving — so it's not DONT_ADD. But it's not a clean ADD either, because the bulk of its mechanism already exists in Shepherd (ntfy push, sound, unseen-count badge, popOnComplete, click-to-jump, jump hotkeys); building it "as-is" would duplicate existing systems. The right disposition is ADAPT: reshape the genuine NEW need — (a) add `completion`/`done` to NOTIFY_TYPES so completion feeds the existing badge + (optionally) the ntfy/sound channel, and (b) add an away-suppression gate so completion/escalation alerts don't fire for the session/tile the operator is currently focused on, and (c) optionally a 'completed while away' count — onto the primitives already there. This is most naturally expressed as a rule in the queued L6 event-callback engine (when event=done AND user-not-viewing-this-session → notify/push/sound), and it extends the existing notification/escalation/badge subsystem rather than standing up a parallel one. The Web Push/WebSocket/cross-tab-dedup layer is correctly dropped. Must be off-by-default and consider de-bouncing given done is high-frequency.
- **How (Shepherd):** 1) Add a `completed`/`done` type to core.NOTIFY_TYPES (cc-core.lua:786) and emit one ledger event on a fresh done transition (reuse the shouldFeed-style prev!=done guard, e.g. near M.routePendingDone), so it feeds the existing setNotifyBadge unseen-count (claude-dashboard.lua ~5686) and the Notifications drawer with its existing per-event Jump button. 2) Add away-detection: track the currently-focused session (from the webview focusChange hook already wired for ⌘V + the selected tile) as a pure predicate in cc-core, and gate completion/escalation notify+pop+push+sound on 'not actively viewing this session' — extends focus.popOnComplete and the escalation channel rather than adding a new system. 3) Completion alert delivery reuses FX.push (ntfy) + FX.playSound + hs.notify for the OS toast; the toast/drawer Jump reuses fx.focusWindow (the existing click-to-jump). 4) Express the whole thing as an L6 rule (when done & user-away → notify/sound/push/jump-link), with a 'completed-while-away' count derived from the same ledger events. New config keys under notifications.* (off by default), with optional debounce/batch since done is high-frequency. DROP: Web Notification permission flow, WebSocket completion stream, cross-tab completedSessionIDs dedup (no server/tabs in Shepherd).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not: it survives both refute directions. The double mismatch is genuinely passed — the value is notify-the-human-on-completion + jump-to-it, not graph/block execution, OAuth/credential storage, or multi-user/marketplace serving. The judge correctly drops the only AutoGPT-platform pieces (Web Notification permission flow, WebSocket completion stream, cross-tab completedSessionIDs dedup): Shepherd has no server and no tabs. So it is NOT DONT_ADD on a double-mismatch basis. Refute-toward-DONT_ADD (pure redundancy) ALSO fails, but only narrowly. I verified the bulk of the mechanism is already built: NOTIFY_TYPES (cc-core.lua:786) {escalation, hung, auto_respawn, auto_continue}; the unseen-count badge pipeline (notificationEvents/unseenNotificationCount at ~801, setNotifyBadge at claude-dashboard.lua:4842, driven at ~5690-5704); ntfy push (FX.push, :973), sound (FX.playSound, :969), and hs.notify available; click-to-jump (FX.focusWindow :1177 → focusProject :207 with title-matching via bestWindowFor/focusCandidates); the once-per-episode edge-fire pattern of ledger+sound+push for escalation (:5538-5544) and hung (:5559-5562); the fresh-done-transition guard shouldFeed (cur=="done" and prev~="done", :1975); and a real, off-by-default completion alert in popOnComplete (cc-popup.sh pops the editor on Stop). That is ~90% overlap — building it as-is would stand up a parallel notification system, which is exactly why it is NOT a clean ADD either. What is genuinely NEW (so it can't be pure DONT_ADD): (1) `done`/`completed` is NOT in NOTIFY_TYPES, so a fresh-done transition doesn't currently feed the badge/drawer/push/sound channel — adding it is a small, real extension. (2) Away-detection does not exist: the ONLY focus signal Shepherd tracks is panelHasFocus (the Shepherd panel's own key focus, used for ⌘V, set in the webview focusChange hook :5124-5126). Nothing continuously maps the frontmost OS window back to a specific session/tile, so 'suppress the alert if the operator is actively viewing that very session' is genuinely new logic (a small predicate over hs.window.focusedWindow():title() reusing the existing focusCandidates/bestWindowFor matchers). (3) a 'completed-while-away' count derived from those ledger events. Reshaping that thin new need onto the existing notification/escalation/badge/pop primitives — most naturally as an L6 event-callback rule (when done AND user-not-viewing-this-session → notify/push/sound, with the jump reusing FX.focusWindow) — is the textbook ADAPT disposition, reinforcing the queued L6, off-by-default, with debounce since done is high-frequency. The judge's Shepherd mapping is accurate down to the cited line numbers. Disposition unchanged: ADAPT, reinforces L6.

#### Per-notification-type preferences + mute/sound toggles + urgency taxonomy  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The transferable kernel is a genuine operator pattern that Shepherd half-has and would benefit from. Shepherd already raises distinct alert types (escalation, hung/stall, auto_respawn, auto_continue, plus non-human gate decisions in NOTIFY_TYPES) and already has the delivery mechanism (FX.playSound Submarine/Ping + FX.push ntfy). But today the sound/push prefs are a SINGLE global pair bolted to escalation only (escalation.sound/push/pushTopic at cc-core.lua:5311-5313, fired at dashboard 5540-5561) — you cannot say "ping me on approval-needed but stay silent on auto-continue." A per-event-type toggle grid ("notify on done / error / approval-needed / hung independently") is the obviously-correct refinement for a many-session panel, and AutoGPT's NotificationsCard NOTIFICATION_GROUPS is good prior art for the layout. A global mute (one header-bell click silences the whole fleet during a meeting) and a sound on/off toggle are both sensible and cheap given playSound already exists. Best of all, the urgency TAXONOMY (critical->notify-now, noisy->coalesce, routine->roll-into-digest) is a per-event-type 'urgency' field that maps cleanly onto L6's processor catalog (immediate->nudge/push, backoff->coalesce, summary->log into the ledger/shift-report) — it gives the queued rule engine a principled severity dimension instead of every rule firing at the same loudness.
- **Against:** Most of the feature's substance is the AutoGPT broker plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host: NotificationTypeOverride.strategy -> RabbitMQ routing key, the QueueType enum + get_routing_key + BATCHING_RULES, IMMEDIATE/BATCH/BACKOFF/SUMMARY/ADMIN as queue exchanges — all of that is the (b) multi-user-platform half of the double mismatch (message broker delivering to N users' channels). The persistence model (a migration adding notifyOn* columns to a users table, backend/data/user.py get/update_user_notification_preference) is per-USER server state; Shepherd is single-user local JSON. The ADMIN/operator-only strategy and the daily/weekly/monthly billing-style summaries presuppose a serving platform. The one-time OS 'enable notifications' permission dialog is browser/web-push ceremony that a Hammerspoon webview doesn't need. And there's a redundancy risk: Shepherd already has the notification-history badge + Alerts tab + ntfy push + shift report, so a heavy new "notifications subsystem" would overlap rather than extend. If built literally as-is it would be DONT_ADD.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch cleanly separates a transferable operator pattern from non-portable plumbing. PORTABLE: per-event-type preferences, global mute, sound on/off, and an urgency classification — all pure-config + UI that ride on primitives Shepherd already ships (NOTIFY_TYPES, FX.playSound, FX.push, the escalation.sound/push config family, the bell badge). NOT PORTABLE and correctly dropped: RabbitMQ routing keys / QueueType / topic exchanges / BATCHING_RULES (the broker), the per-user notifyOn* DB columns (multi-user server state), and the OS web-push permission flow. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note already prescribes exactly this split ("ADAPT the grid + mute/sound + taxonomy, drop the broker + OS-permission flow"), and it explicitly invokes L6 and the off-by-default philosophy — so this is not a parallel system, it is the severity/preference dimension of the already-queued L6 event-callback rule engine. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L6. Not L5 (that's per-tile observability, not alert routing) and not the emerging 'scheduling' idea. Keeping everything off/quiet by default is a hard scope rule, so the global mute defaults to unmuted only because alerts themselves are already opt-in.
- **How (Shepherd):** Generalize the single global escalation.sound/push pair into a per-event-type matrix under L6. Concretely: (1) extend cc-core's M.NOTIFY_TYPES into a config-backed table notifications.types.<kind> = {sound, push, urgency} where kind in {approval, done, error, hung, auto_respawn, auto_continue, starved} — the same kinds L6's trigger.kind already enumerates (todos.md:550). (2) Add notifications.mute (global) and notifications.sound (master on/off) checked inside the existing fire sites (dashboard ~5540/5560) before FX.playSound/FX.push, so mute short-circuits all channels. (3) Add an 'urgency' enum per type (immediate|coalesce|summary) that maps onto L6's processor catalog: immediate->push/nudge now, coalesce->batch within an episode window (reuse the once-per-episode 'alerted' marker), summary->only ledger it for the shift report / Alerts tab. (4) UI: a small tabbed toggle-grid settings card (cols: Sound | Push | Urgency; rows: the event kinds) replacing the lone s-e-snd/s-e-push checkboxes, plus a mute+sound glyph on the existing menu/notify-badge bell. All rows default OFF; keeps the off-by-default scope. No broker, no DB, no OS-permission dialog.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not. The judge's claims check out against the actual code. The portable core rides entirely on shipped Shepherd primitives: M.NOTIFY_TYPES = {escalation, hung, auto_respawn, auto_continue} (cc-core.lua:786), FX.playSound (claude-dashboard.lua:969), FX.push (973), the global escalation.sound/escalation.push/escalation.pushTopic config family (5311-5313; UI checkboxes s-e-snd/s-e-push at 3143-3144, loaded at 3743-3745), the single fire sites that gate sound/push (5540/5560), and the existing 🔔 bell badge + Notifications menu (3000) + Alerts tab (3307) + notification history (cc-core notificationEvents). So per-event toggles, a global mute, a master sound toggle, and an urgency field are pure config + UI, not new infrastructure. The non-portable plumbing is correctly dropped and maps to the AutoGPT double mismatch (b) server/multi-user: RabbitMQ topic exchanges / QueueType / BATCHING_RULES (the broker), per-user notifyOn* DB columns (multi-user server state), and the OS web-push permission dialog. Mismatch (a) graph/block executor does not even apply — notifications are not graph execution. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note prescribes exactly this split. Not redundant and not a parallel system: this is precisely the missing severity/preference dimension of already-queued L6. L6's spec enumerates trigger:{kind: done|error|hung|approval|starved} (todos.md:550) — the same event kinds — but L6 today only routes ACTIONS (feed/continue/nudge/relabel/log) and the current notification config is one global escalation.sound/push pair shared across escalation+hung with no per-type matrix and no urgency classification. So it genuinely extends L6 rather than re-adding shipped function (full DONT_ADD overreaches) and isn't a standalone subsystem (ADD overreaches). The L5 (per-tile observability) and scheduling rejections are also correct. Refutation attempts all fail: (1) DONT_ADD-as-redundant fails because the per-type granularity, global mute, and master-sound toggles are net-new; (2) ADD-as-new-system fails because it plugs into L6's existing kind enum and the escalation config family; (3) the report/digest types (daily/weekly/monthly, BATCH/SUMMARY) that smell like broker machinery are handled by the judge's summary→ledger-only mapping onto Shepherd's existing shift report/insights, no broker. The global mute defaulting unmuted is fine because alerts are already opt-in, preserving off-by-default. ADAPT (reinforces L6) holds.

### Reinforces L5  (6)

#### Session-as-first-class-object with status lifecycle + persisted history  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The operator pattern underneath is exactly Shepherd's job: treat each session as a first-class object with a validated status lifecycle, terminal-vs-active distinction, and a recorded ended-reason. Shepherd already half-implements this (cc-status/<id>.json carries status + a `since` timestamp; cc-core has a fixed enum idle/working/done/approval/error, RANK ordering approvals-first, and `transcriptError` that derives a frozen-on-API-error state). AutoGPT's contributions REINFORCE concrete queued work: (1) the richer enum + terminal/active split + ended-reason maps straight onto L5's "Error-reason taxonomy" (classifyError -> budget_exceeded|timeout|... with a cause badge and an errors-by-cause shift-report breakdown) and would give Shepherd's existing `since` field a symmetric `ended_at`/`ended-reason`; (2) the explicit valid-transition table is a tiny pure helper that fits cc-core.lua's zero-hs.* unit-tested discipline and would harden the ad-hoc status overrides (e.g. transcriptError forcing working->error) against illegal jumps; (3) "park in REVIEW until ALL pending reviews clear" is literally Shepherd's approval-gate invariant -- don't clear approval / don't let the level-triggered dispatcher advance the queue until every queued prompt is answered; (4) the persisted ordered transcript feeds L5's "Export session archive" (copy .jsonl + meta.json). Every piece lands as pure cc-core derivations over the transcript Shepherd already tails plus the status file it already writes -- no new subsystem.
- **Against:** The feature's headline value in AutoGPT is the turn-loop / state-machine that DRIVES an in-process agent: QUEUED->RUNNING->COMPLETED is the executor advancing a graph run, and executor/manager.py:849's REVIEW->RUNNING "resume" literally re-enters the agent loop after review clears. Shepherd runs no agent loop -- Claude Code itself drives the GUI session, and Shepherd only WATCHES the status file and STEERS via keystrokes. So the literal lifecycle engine (level-triggered resume that re-runs the task, started_at/ended_at set BY the executor, stats.error written BY the run) is DONT_ADD: Shepherd cannot author those transitions because it never owns the run. The Pydantic-session-over-Prisma-rows-plus-Redis persistence layer is also pure platform plumbing (a DB + cache for a multi-user server) with no analogue in a single-user filesystem-backed console. Adopting the full state machine wholesale risks Shepherd asserting authoritative run outcomes (COMPLETED/FAILED) it cannot actually observe -- Shepherd only sees status edges from hooks, not ground-truth run results -- which violates its "honest stand-in, never claim outcomes" posture (see the `continues` = error-recovery stand-in comment).
- **Ruling:** Double mismatch applies and is decisive on the split. The graph-execution half (turn-loop, executor-authored transitions, REVIEW->RUNNING resume, Prisma+Redis persistence) is exactly the in-process EXECUTOR Shepherd structurally lacks -- DONT_ADD on its own. But the candidate's own SHEPHERD note is correct that the MANAGEMENT/RUN-HISTORY/HUMAN-REVIEW pattern transfers, and it transfers onto things Shepherd already has or has queued rather than a new system. Mapping: session-as-object-with-status = already shipped (cc-status JSON + cc-core enum + `since`); richer enum + terminal/active + ended-reason = L5 error-reason taxonomy; REVIEW + "all reviews clear" = the shipped approval gate / queue-hold invariant; persisted transcript = L5 export. The single best backlog match is L5 (it absorbs the enum-richening, ended-reason, taxonomy, and export). The only genuinely new artifact is the validated transition table -- a small pure cc-core helper, not a subsystem -- so the net is ADAPT/REINFORCES, not ADD. Confidence high: the feature is explicitly self-classified ADAPT and the Shepherd primitives it touches are all present in the code I read. Category is Executions & Monitoring (run status lifecycle + history is the observability surface). Effort S: enrich the existing status model + add classifyError/transition-table helpers; the export piece is already scoped S under L5.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L5. (1) Extend the existing per-session status object (cc-status.sh JSON + cc-core enum) with a symmetric `ended_at` + `ended_reason` alongside today's `since`, and a terminal/active flag derived from the enum. (2) Implement the AutoGPT-style ended-reason as L5's `core.classifyError(message)` (budget_exceeded|model_error|runtime_error|timeout|user_cancelled|unknown), extending the result of `transcriptError` (cc-core.lua:1588) with a `.reason`; surface as a cause badge + search facet + errors-by-cause line in fleetStats/shift report. (3) Add a small pure `core.validTransition(from,to)` table-guard in cc-core (unit-tested, zero hs.*) to gate the status overrides (e.g. working->error from transcriptError, working->approval) so illegal jumps are rejected -- modeled on AutoGPT's VALID_STATUS_TRANSITIONS but applied to Shepherd's 5-state enum. (4) Map REVIEW onto the shipped approval gate: keep the existing invariant that the level-triggered dispatcher / autopilot does not advance the queue until every pending prompt is answered (AutoGPT's "resume only when pending-review count is zero"). (5) Persisted history -> L5 "Export session archive" (copy the .jsonl transcript + meta.json). EXCLUDE: the turn-loop/resume engine, executor-authored transitions, and the Prisma+Redis persistence layer (those require the in-process executor Shepherd does not run).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT ruling holds. I checked the code and the backlog and the judge's split is precise on both halves. EXECUTOR HALF = DONT_ADD (double mismatch a). The turn-loop, REVIEW->RUNNING resume, executor-authored transitions, and the Prisma+Redis persistence layer are exactly the in-process graph/block EXECUTOR Shepherd structurally lacks. Shepherd runs no agent loop — Claude Code drives the session; Shepherd only WATCHES status files. The candidate self-concedes "the literal resume requires the executor (DONT_ADD)." Tried to push the whole feature to DONT_ADD on this basis, but that overreaches because the management/run-history half genuinely transfers. MANAGEMENT HALF = already shipped or already queued — so it REINFORCES, it does not ADD a new system: - Session-as-object with serialized status + timestamp is ALREADY SHIPPED. cc-status.sh writes per-session {session_id,name,cwd,status,updated,since} with a fixed 5-state enum (idle/working/approval/done/error; cc-core.lua:28-33,50). `since` is the started-at-status stamp. The dashboard derives `error` via core.transcriptError and attaches error_message (claude-dashboard.lua:5426-5427). The "at most one task in flight per session" + REVIEW maps onto the shipped approval gate and the level-triggered single-dispatcher-per-tick queue-hold invariant (cc-core.lua:1975 shouldFeed / routing dispatcher). - The richer enum + ended-reason + error taxonomy + persisted-transcript export are ALREADY QUEUED in L5, not new ideas: todos.md:512-516 specifies core.classifyError(message) with the IDENTICAL enum the candidate proposes (budget_exceeded|model_error|runtime_error|timeout|user_cancelled|unknown), extending transcriptError's result with .reason plus an errors-by-cause shift-report line; todos.md:522-525 is "Export session archive" (copy .jsonl + meta.json); auto-title is also L5. ONE GENUINELY NEW ARTIFACT confirms ADAPT (extension/reinforcement, not pure redundancy and not pure executor): I grepped cc-core.lua and there is NO validTransition / VALID_STATUS table today. A small pure core.validTransition(from,to) table-guard to gate the existing status overrides (working->error from transcriptError, working->approval) is a real, transferable hardening — a unit-tested cc-core helper, not a subsystem. That single new pure helper is why this lands at ADAPT/REINFORCES rather than DONT_ADD. Net: matches the judge exactly. Reinforces L5 (enum-richening + ended-reason taxonomy + export), maps REVIEW onto the shipped approval/queue-hold invariant, and adds one small transition-table guard; the turn-loop/resume/Prisma+Redis executor stays excluded. Effort S. Could not refute. Key files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-status.sh (status JSON + 5-state enum, lines 76-82,177-195); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (status enum/RANK 28-50, transcriptError 1588-1609, shouldFeed/dispatcher 1975+, no validTransition present); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua:5426-5427 (error-status override); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md:499-525 (L5 classifyError enum + export + auto-title).

#### Windowed fleet aggregate dashboard with top-N (cost/usage summary)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The underlying operator pattern — a windowed aggregate across the whole fleet plus a ranked top-N of the heaviest sessions — is exactly the read a single-operator console wants: "over this window, what did the fleet consume and which sessions dominated?" Shepherd already has all the raw material to make this excellent: per-session token parsing from local transcripts (sumUsage, usageInWindow, formatTokens, all zero-cost and no network), rolling 5h/7d plan-window tracking, and a fleetStandup/shift report that ALREADY takes {sinceTs, untilTs, topN} and emits byProject + mostActive. The candidate's two genuinely additive slices are cheap and useful: (1) a default calendar-month preset alongside the existing since-opened/8h/24h presets, and (2) ranking top-N by token volume or wall-clock duration rather than only by prompt count. Both plug straight into existing pure functions and the existing shift-report UI, giving the operator a "heaviest sessions this month" view that the current activity-only ranking misses.
- **Against:** The candidate's headline value in AutoGPT is DOLLAR COST (stats.cost, get_user_cost_summary, UserExecutionCostSummary), and that half is structurally impossible and undesirable in Shepherd: there is no billing data, no per-token pricing, no API proxy that sees spend — Shepherd never makes API calls, it watches a GUI session that Claude Code itself drives. The cost summary is also inherently a MULTI-USER, per-account billing artifact (get_user_cost_summary returns one row per user), which collides head-on with Shepherd's single-user, no-server, no-accounts scope. Strip the cost/credits/per-user dimension and what remains is not a new feature at all — it is the shift report Shepherd already ships, which already does windowed fleet aggregation with a top-N. So there is a real risk of building a redundant parallel "dashboard" rather than a tiny extension.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the cost engine (a) reads execution/billing state from the in-process executor and (b) serves per-user billing rows on a multi-user platform — both halves are DONT_ADD for Shepherd (no executor, no server, no accounts, no API spend it can meter). What transfers is the management/run-history pattern: windowed aggregate + top-N over run history, where a "run" maps to a session. Crucially, Shepherd ALREADY HAS this pattern shipped — fleetStandup(events,{sinceTs,untilTs,topN}) -> {totals, byProject, mostActive} rendered as the 📋 Shift report with window presets and Copy, plus fleetStats sorting perSession descending into mostActive[1..topN]. So this is not ADD (it would duplicate the shift report) and not DONT_ADD (the token-volume/duration ranking and a calendar-month preset are real, in-scope enrichments using existing zero-cost token primitives). That is the definition of ADAPT: reshape onto existing primitives, drop the credits, keep windowed-aggregate-with-top-N on tokens/duration. It reinforces L5 (richer session observability) — adding a token/duration cost-proxy ranking to the existing shift report — rather than standing up a new system. Calendar-month default also lightly touches the emerging window-preset idea but the core home is L5.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing shift report rather than add a new view. In cc-core.lua: (1) add a fourth window preset "month" computing sinceTs = start of current calendar month (the only impure bit, a clock read, stays in the dashboard which already passes sinceTs into fleetStandup at claude-dashboard.lua ~L1999); (2) enrich fleetStats.perSession with a token figure per session (join the existing per-session transcript usage already parsed for the tiles via sumUsage/lastUsage, plus duration = lastTs - firstTs computable from agg.events) and add a second ranking, e.g. report.heaviest = top-N by real tokens and/or duration, beside the existing mostActive (by prompts); (3) surface those in standupMarkdown as a "Heaviest sessions (this month): name — 1.30M tokens · 2h 5m" block, reusing formatTokens and fmtDuration. UI: add a "Month" preset button to the shift report's window-preset bar (open-shift handler ~L1991) — no new overlay, no new tab. Explicitly NO dollar cost, NO credits, NO per-user rows; tokens/duration only, all zero-cost and local.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; ADAPT holds. I verified every primitive the judge cited and the double mismatch on the dollar-cost half. The credits/billing engine is correctly DONT_ADD: it (a) reads execution/billing state and (b) serves per-user rows on a multi-user platform — Shepherd has no executor, no server, no accounts, no API spend it can meter. What transfers (windowed aggregate + top-N over run history, run=session) is genuinely in-scope AND already partly shipped, which is exactly why this is ADAPT not ADD. Confirmed in code: fleetStandup(events,{sinceTs,untilTs,topN}) at cc-core.lua:1137 reuses fleetStats and returns {totals, byProject, mostActive}; fleetStats at cc-core.lua:954 builds perSession (prompts/toolRequests/decisions/blockedSeconds/lastTs), sorts DESC by prompts, slices topN into mostActive[1..N]; standupMarkdown at cc-core.lua:1201 renders the 📋 Shift report (also feeds Copy). The open-shift handler at claude-dashboard.lua:1991-1999 has window presets (8h/24h/open) and passes sinceTs/untilTs into fleetStandup — precisely as the judge described. Zero-cost local token/duration primitives all exist: sumUsage (1684), lastUsage (1749), usageInWindow (1709), formatTokens (1721), parseUsageLine (1647), fmtDuration (902). Why it does not collapse to DONT_ADD (pure redundancy): the existing mostActive ranks by PROMPTS only and byProject is a project rollup — there is NO existing top-N ranking of heaviest sessions by token volume or duration. That ranking is novel, and a calendar-month preset is a new (clock-read) window. So it enriches, not duplicates. One caveat that does NOT change the verdict but corrects the judge's mapping: the judge says to "join the existing per-session transcript usage already parsed for the tiles" — but that live usage (usageState/computeUsage, claude-dashboard.lua:319/331) is a 60s LIVE snapshot keyed by the live session key, and it DROPS ended sessions (usageState[p]=nil at L386). The ledger events that fleetStandup consumes carry NO token fields (ledgerFor at claude-dashboard.lua:555 writes only session_id/key/name/projectKey/cwd). So token-heaviest-over-a-past-month can't be a trivial join with the live usage payload — you'd need either per-event token counts added to the ledger or a windowed re-parse of historical transcripts. Duration (lastTs−firstTs) IS already computable from agg.events in perSession with zero new data. This is an implementation seam, not a scope breach: everything stays local, single-user, zero-cost, no executor/server/credentials/marketplace. So the adapted feature (windowed aggregate + top-N heaviest by duration now, by tokens with the token source sourced correctly) legitimately reinforces L5 (richer session observability) by extending the shipped shift report. ADAPT (high) stands; could not be refuted to DONT_ADD.

#### Auto-generated session titles (never clobber a manual rename) + rename/search  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** Naming and finding sessions is a real operator need on a fleet board, and AutoGPT ships the single most useful design detail Shepherd should lock in: the auto-title is generated only-if-empty via an atomic guard (UPDATE WHERE title IS NULL), so a machine-written title can NEVER overwrite a human's deliberate rename. Shepherd's L5 "auto-title tiles from content" item declares the same precedence (manual relabel > auto-title > folder basename) and "recompute only while empty," and AutoGPT is concrete prior art that the only-if-empty invariant must be atomic at the write, not just checked at render — a worthwhile hardening to copy into L5. The rename + substring-find half maps 1:1 onto things Shepherd already has (setLabel / persistent relabels, filterTiles), so the title piece slots in with near-zero new surface and improves at-a-glance scanning of a tile grid, which is exactly Shepherd's core UX. The user's stated priority (a directory of saved, well-labeled agents) is also better served by sessions that carry meaningful names.
- **Against:** Almost everything here already exists in Shepherd, so the marginal value is small. Rename = M.setLabel + persistent relabels (cc-core.lua:307); case-insensitive substring find = M.filterTiles over label/name/cwd/projectKey/status/group (cc-core.lua:322); auto-title is already the queued L5 item with the correct precedence and "recompute only while empty" guard. The semantic embedding search (/search/global, chat_session_embeddings.py) is a hard DONT_ADD — it needs an embedding service plus a server to host vectors and query them, which violates Shepherd's no-network-server / no-API-call scope. AutoGPT's auto-title also leans on an aux LLM call; Shepherd already captures last_prompt in cc-status.sh, so the title should be derived deterministically (core.deriveAutoTitle) with no API call rather than a fire-and-forget LLM round-trip. There is no new capability to add — only a one-line invariant to remember when L5 is built.
- **Ruling:** Double-mismatch check: the only platform-specific part (semantic embedding global search) depends on a server + embedding service and is correctly out of scope (DONT_ADD), while rename and substring search are already shipped (filterTiles, setLabel). The genuinely transferable kernel is the auto-title-only-if-empty / never-clobber-a-manual-rename invariant, which is not a new feature but a refinement of the already-queued L5 "auto-title tiles from content" item (precedence manual relabel > auto-title > folder basename; recompute only while empty). AutoGPT's atomic UPDATE WHERE title IS NULL is good prior art that the guard belongs at the write, not just at render. Shepherd should still derive the title deterministically from the captured last_prompt rather than firing an aux LLM call, to stay within the no-API-call posture. Net: minor reinforcement of L5, not a parallel system — ADAPT/reinforces L5.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into the existing L5 "Auto-title tiles from content" item: when implementing core.deriveAutoTitle(tail|last_prompt, maxLen), enforce the only-if-empty invariant atomically at the cc-autotitles.json write (write the cached auto-title ONLY when there is no manual relabel for that projectKey AND no existing auto-title), making the documented precedence (manual relabel via setLabel > auto-title > folder basename) a write-time guarantee, not just a render ordering. Rename already = setLabel + persistent relabels; substring find already = filterTiles. Derive the title from the already-captured last_prompt with no LLM/API call.
