Drop the semantic embedding /search/global path entirely (no server, no embedding service). Settings toggle default OFF, esc() at the render sink.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on every axis; ADAPT/reinforces-L5 holds. (1) Double-mismatch is real but already excised by the ruling: the LLM-auto-generated title needs an aux LLM call (breaks Shepherd's no-API-call posture) and semantic embedding search needs a server + embedding service — both correctly sent to DONT_ADD. What remains is a deterministic title derived from already-captured data, no executor/server needed. (2) Not redundant-enough to be DONT_ADD: rename and substring search ARE already shipped — core.setLabel (cc-core.lua:307) and core.filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322, with a JS twin at claude-dashboard.lua:3611), wired at claude-dashboard.lua:2367 — but the auto-title-only-if-empty kernel is NOT shipped; it's a queued, unimplemented L5 item (no deriveAutoTitle / cc-autotitles.json exists in code). (3) Not a parallel system to be ADD: todos.md:508-511 already holds the identical L5 'Auto-title tiles from content' item with the same seed (cc-status.sh:199 captures last_prompt), same cache (cc-autotitles.json by projectKey), same precedence (manual relabel > auto-title > folder basename), recompute only while empty, esc() at the render sink, toggle default OFF. The ONLY transferable refinement is AutoGPT's atomic UPDATE WHERE title IS NULL pattern: enforce the never-clobber-a-manual-rename guard at the cc-autotitles.json WRITE (write the cached auto-title only when there is no manual relabel for that projectKey AND no existing auto-title), making the documented precedence a write-time guarantee, not just render ordering. That is a one-line tightening of an existing queued item — reinforcement, not a new feature. ADAPT, reinforces L5.

#### Run history list (paginated) + run detail timeline (inputs/outputs/per-step)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** Shepherd's observability is live-biased: the tile grid shows only sessions that currently exist, and the audit overlay surfaces events filtered by type/session, not a browsable index of completed runs. Once a session ends and its tile drops off the board, there is no persistent "show me every run I've done, newest first, with status + start/end + stats" view, and no single drill-down that puts a finished session's seed prompt, collected outputs, and a sorted per-turn timeline on one screen. That is a real operator gap for a fleet console — the "what happened across all my past sessions" question. The data already exists locally: cc-status.sh logs session_start/end + prompt + tool_request, cc-approve.sh logs decisions, claude-dashboard.lua logs operator actions into the JSONL ledger, and core.sessionTimeline already reconstructs a per-session timeline from it. AutoGPT's Task->Steps->Artifacts schema (task~session, step~tool/turn event, artifact~file/PR) maps cleanly, and its account-wide-list + per-agent-page split maps onto Shepherd's session GROUPS. L5's todos.md already names "detail-panel tabs" with an inline Timeline tab and "Export session archive"; a paginated past-run list is the missing index that makes those drill-downs reachable for sessions no longer on the board.
- **Against:** The bulk of AutoGPT's implementation is the DOUBLE MISMATCH made concrete: a Prisma/SQLite store, a Paginated DTO over AgentGraphExecution with compound indexes, GraphExecutionWithNodes/NodeExecutionResult assembly, the get_graph_execution HTTP endpoint, the FastAPI Agent Protocol server persisting Task/Step/Artifact, and a Next.js SidebarRunsList/SelectedRunView/RunDetailCard frontend. All of that is graph-execution + server + DB plumbing Shepherd structurally rejects (no server, no DB beyond local JSONL, no node-graph executor). The "node execution" framing — per-node status/timing/data, the DAG timeline — has no analogue: a Claude Code session is not a graph of blocks, so "per-step node execution" must be reinterpreted as turn/tool events, losing the feature's literal shape. There is also overlap risk: the audit overlay + sessionTimeline + the queued L5 Timeline tab + Export archive already cover much of "everything this session did," so the net-new is narrow (a persistent paginated INDEX of past sessions), and adding a SQLite-style pagination layer would duplicate the JSONL ledger Shepherd deliberately standardized on.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED — browse past runs + open one to see its full story — is real and a genuine gap (verified: no pagination primitive and no past-run index exist in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua; the grid is live-only and the audit overlay is event-filtered). But its worth in AutoGPT rides on the parts Shepherd cannot host (Prisma/SQLite, Paginated DTO, FastAPI Agent Protocol server, Next.js run views, and the node-graph DAG framing). Strip those and what transfers is a read-only run-history list built from the existing JSONL ledger, with a detail view assembled by the existing sessionTimeline/renderNarrative — precisely L5's territory (detail-panel tabs + Timeline + Export). So ADAPT, reinforcing L5, not ADD. Drop pagination-as-DB (reuse the ledger's date-file scan + the overlay's cap, paging in pure cc-core), drop the node-graph/Artifact-server framing, and reframe task->steps->artifacts as session->turn/tool events->files/PRs. Not DONT_ADD because the value survives once the executor/DB/server are removed; not ADD because the literal feature is the disallowed plumbing.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure core.listRuns(events, {page, pageSize, group?}) that folds the JSONL ledger (cc-ledger/*.jsonl, already parsed by parseLedger) into per-session run rows {sessionId, projectKey, label, provider/model, status from session_start/end + transcriptError, startedTs/endedTs, turn/decision/tool counts} sorted newest-first, with pure in-memory paging (no SQL — scan recent date files, cap like the overlay). Surface it as a new "Runs" overlay or an Audit-overlay tab listing past+active runs (active rows deep-link to the live tile/Jump; dead rows open the detail). Clicking a row opens the per-session drill-down that ALREADY exists: core.sessionTimeline + renderNarrative inside the queued L5 detail-panel tab strip (Activity/Timeline/Decisions/Usage), seeded prompt from cc-status last_prompt, collected outputs/files+PR via the L5 PR-status/Export assembly. Per-agent page_size ~ filter by session GROUPS. Reuse the existing audit export side-effect path for "export this run." All read-only, off by default, no new writer.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT/L5 ruling holds. I tried to push this to DONT_ADD on redundancy/plumbing grounds and to ADD, and neither survives the code. What the feature actually is in AutoGPT (the disallowed core): the Agent Protocol HTTP server — Task->Steps->Artifacts persisted in SQLite/Prisma, FastAPI endpoints, a Paginated DTO with account-wide top-250 + page/page_size, Next.js run views, framed as a node-graph DAG with per-NODE execution status/timing/data. Server (Shepherd has none), DB/Prisma (none), accounts/account-wide (single-user), and the graph-executor framing (Shepherd runs no graph/blocks) are all out of scope. So the literal feature is disallowed plumbing — ADD is wrong. Why it is NOT DONT_ADD (the value survives stripping all that): the residual need — 'browse past runs, open one for its full story' — partly exists and partly is a verified gap. - Detail timeline: ALREADY SHIPPED. core.sessionTimeline (cc-core.lua:732), renderNarrative (cc-core.lua:697), gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752) exist; the dashboard wires TWO drill-downs — open-session-timeline for live tiles and open-audit-for-session by session_id for DEAD sessions (claude-dashboard.lua:2057, 2081) — plus a 📜 Timeline button (line 3072). session_start/session_end events are actually written to the ledger (cc-status.sh:67,264; NARRATE table cc-core.lua:643-644), so a per-session run row {status, started/ended, counts} folds straight out of parseLedger/filterLedger. - Tabbed sub-views: ALREADY QUEUED as L5 'Detail-panel tabs' (Activity/Timeline/Decisions/Usage, todos.md:517-521) and 'Export session archive' (522-525). - The one GENUINE gap (verified): a persistent, browsable INDEX of past runs as per-session rows. There is no listRuns, no pagination primitive, no past-run index — the tile grid is live-only and the audit overlay is a flat event-filtered stream, not a per-session run-row list. Paging is trivial in-memory slicing (capLedgerSlice cc-core.lua:553 already caps newest-first), NOT SQL — the DB pagination is plumbing, not a feature. So the value clearly survives removing the executor/DB/server (=> not DONT_ADD) but the literal feature is the disallowed server/DB/graph plumbing (=> not ADD): textbook ADAPT, reinforcing L5 (richer session observability — a read-only run-history list off the JSONL ledger, with the detail view assembled by the already-shipped sessionTimeline/renderNarrative and the queued L5 detail tabs/export). One caveat the judge slightly over-credited: the 'detail timeline' it cited as the L5 reinforcement is already shipped, so the only NEW surface is the paginated past-run list — a thinner ADAPT than framed, but still L5 territory. The ruling stands.

#### Per-run cost & resource stats + AI-generated run summary & correctness score  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The operator NEED behind this is real and already half-built in Shepherd: per-run structured stats + a human-readable "what got done" + a quality indicator are exactly what makes a watch console legible after the fact. Shepherd already tracks the resource side at zero cost — token/context usage parsed from the local transcript JSONL (core.sumUsage, core.lastUsage, usageInWindow, the per-tile context bar, sparklines, fleetStats, shift report) plus wall-clock from ledger ts pairs. It already has a per-session RISK score (core.sessionRisk, med/high band) that is the natural home for the "correctness/quality" indicator. And the backlog ALREADY queues the AI summary as L5 "Post-run self-summary callback" (core.summaryPrompt, fires on a fresh done edge via the dispatchSerialized chokepoint, optional summary ledger event) AND L5 "Error-reason taxonomy" (core.classifyError → budget_exceeded|timeout|… as an error-cause badge + an "errors by cause" shift-report breakdown). The candidate's structured-stats record (node-exec count → tool-request count, node-error count → per-session error count, recorded end error → transcriptError text/reason, duration → wall-clock) drops cleanly into the post-run summary and the error tile. This is reinforcing prior art, not net-new architecture, and it advances the user's "give me legible saved-agent runs" direction.
- **Against:** Taken as-is it carries AutoGPT's double mismatch on both halves. The "cost (cents)" / credit / billing field only exists because AutoGPT is a multi-user metered platform with a credit ledger — Shepherd makes no API calls, stores no billing, and meters nothing; a session genuinely has no "cents," so that field is meaningless and must be dropped. The "stats pydantic persisted as JSON on GraphExecutionMeta" assumes an in-process graph/node executor that records node_exec_time/node_exec_count/node_error_count as it runs the DAG — Shepherd runs no graph and owns no execution engine; those counters don't natively exist and can only be approximated from the ledger/transcript. The activity_status_generator path calls a SERVER-SIDE helper model over execution data; Shepherd has no server and must not make API calls — the summary can only come from the session driving the LOCAL claude CLI on its own done edge (which is exactly L5's framing, not the server-batch model). The "admin batch backfill" + feature flag are multi-user-platform machinery (admin endpoint, hide_activity_summaries_if_disabled gating) with no analogue in a single-user local panel. And a 0.0-1.0 "correctness SCORE" risks claiming an authoritative outcome Shepherd cannot verify — it must be framed as an indicator (mapped onto the existing empirical risk score / self-review), never a truth label.
- **Ruling:** Double-mismatch test: the candidate's load-bearing value is NOT running the graph/blocks, storing creds, or serving users — it's run legibility (stats + summary + quality indicator), which is a pure MANAGEMENT/RUN-HISTORY pattern that transfers. But it must be reshaped, so ADAPT not ADD. The mapping is unusually clean because Shepherd already SHIPS the hard parts: (1) resource stats — already covered by the token/context usage + wall-clock + sparklines + fleetStats/shift report; the candidate adds only a per-session error-count and the recorded end-error to the post-run record, both derivable from the ledger/transcriptError. (2) AI run summary — this IS the queued L5 "Post-run self-summary callback," driven by the session via the local claude CLI on a fresh done edge, not by a server. (3) correctness score — maps onto the existing per-session RISK score and L5 error-reason taxonomy, framed as an indicator, not an authoritative outcome. Three things from AutoGPT are explicitly DROPPED as double-mismatch: dollar/credit cost (no metering/billing), the node-level executor stats as ground truth (no graph engine — approximate from ledger), and the server-side helper-model call + admin batch backfill + platform feature-flag (no server/admin/multi-user). Net: the underlying need is real and already queued, so verdict ADAPT, reinforces L5. Confidence high — the backlog text describes this feature almost line-for-line.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L5, no new system. (a) Per-run stats: extend the post-run summary record + ledger summary event with per-session error-count (count of error/api_error edges) and the recorded end-error text+reason; reuse existing token/context totals (core.sumUsage / lastUsage), wall-clock from ledger ts pairs, and tool-request count (already in core.sessionRisk.signals.toolRequests) as the "node-exec" analogue. Surface in the L5 detail-panel Usage/Activity tab + the shift report. (b) AI summary: implement as the queued L5 core.summaryPrompt(item) sibling to auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt — target filter selects sessions on a fresh done edge (modeled on shouldAutoContinue/remoteControlSweepTargets), typed via dispatchSerialized so the session self-summarizes through the LOCAL claude CLI; store the 1-3 sentence result + optional summary ledger event; render in history/detail. Opt-in, OFF by default. (c) correctness_score: do NOT add a new field — map to the existing per-session risk score (core.sessionRisk band) and the L5 error-reason taxonomy (core.classifyError), framed as an indicator. DROP entirely: cents/credit cost, the pydantic-on-GraphExecutionMeta node-executor stats as ground truth, the server-side helper-model call, the admin batch backfill, and the platform feature-flag gating (replace with Shepherd's standard Settings toggle).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation failed; the ADAPT ruling holds, reinforcing L5. The feature's load-bearing value is run legibility (per-run stats + an AI summary + a quality indicator) — a pure run-history/management pattern that transfers onto Shepherd's primitives, NOT graph/block execution, credential storage, or multi-user serving. Verified the mapping against source: (1) The AI run summary IS the already-queued L5 "Post-run self-summary callback" almost line-for-line — todos.md:534-538 specifies core.summaryPrompt(item) as a sibling to auditReviewPrompt/improvePrompt (both exist: cc-core.lua:715/481), a fresh done-edge target filter modeled on remoteControlSweepTargets/shouldAutoContinue, typed via dispatchSerialized so the SESSION self-summarizes through the local claude CLI, plus an optional summary ledger event, opt-in/off-by-default. Session-driven, not server-driven. (2) Resource stats already ship: sumUsage/lastUsage (tokens, cc-core.lua:1684/1749), usageInWindow (wall-window), sessionRisk.signals.toolRequests (cc-core.lua:1438 — the node-exec analogue), transcriptError (recorded end error, cc-core.lua:1588), plus fleetStats/shift report; the only new bits are per-session error-count and the recorded end-error text, both derivable from the existing ledger/transcript. (3) The correctness score maps to the existing 0-100 banded core.sessionRisk (cc-core.lua:1371) + the L5 error-reason taxonomy core.classifyError (todos.md:512), framed as an indicator (consistent with todos.md:353 "rides the existing ledger + risk score as an indicator only"), not a new authoritative field. The three AutoGPT-only parts are correctly DROPPED as double-mismatches: dollar/credit cost (grep finds ZERO cents/dollar/billing/price anywhere — usage tracking is explicitly 'ZERO-COST' from transcript JSONL with no metering; AutoGPT per-agent billing/marketplace is listed as a dropped non-Shepherd surface at todos.md:276); the pydantic node-executor stats as ground truth (no graph engine — approximate from the ledger); and the server-side helper-model call + admin batch backfill + platform feature-flag (no server/admin/multi-user — replaced by Shepherd's standard Settings toggle). Cannot be ADD (must be reshaped: drop cost/executor/server) and cannot be DONT_ADD (the core need is real and already queued as L5, not redundant with a shipped feature).

#### Run-completion + error notifications with backoff/coalescing  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** This is the single most on-pattern AutoGPT feature for a watch/steer console: "tell the human when an agent finishes or errors." Shepherd already does the structurally hard part — it derives done/idle/error from cc-status + transcriptError, fires escalation/hung alerts, and has a real ntfy push channel (FX.push, dashboard.lua:973) feeding a notification-history overlay (🔔). The candidate adds two things Shepherd genuinely lacks and has already queued: (1) a per-run completion SUMMARY (node/output/exec-time analog → a session's turns/duration/done-edge summary), which is exactly L5's "Post-run self-summary callback" + run-summary enrichment (todos.md:534); and (2) an ERROR-REASON taxonomy (block-failed vs continuously-erroring agent), which is exactly L5's `core.classifyError` (todos.md:512). The BACKOFF/coalescing window is a real improvement on Shepherd's current alerting: today escalation/hung dedupe is only "once per episode" via the `escalated`/`alerted` markers (dashboard.lua:5530-5564) — a session that thrashes (errors, recovers, re-errors) over and over would raise a fresh episode each time and spam ntfy. A 60-min collapse window over a session's error stream is the precise fix, and it slots cleanly into the existing per-tile watchdog state + pure cc-core predicates.
- **Against:** Much of the candidate's literal value is AutoGPT-platform plumbing that Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host: RabbitMQ routing keys + a batch consumer, Postmark/email delivery, jinja2 server-rendered templates, an executor hook that builds the event, and "credits" billing — all of which presuppose the in-process graph executor and a multi-user server. None of that transfers. Even the "run finished" framing is a category mismatch: AutoGPT's run is a graph the platform executed end-to-end with authoritative outputs/credits; Shepherd's "done" is just a status-file edge on a GUI session Claude Code itself drove, so any "outputs/credits/node count" report is necessarily a thin local approximation, not the real artifact. And the core notification spine already exists — done detection, error state, ntfy push, once-per-episode dedupe, notification history — so the risk is re-adding what's shipped under a new name rather than extending it.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: strip the executor hook (no graph/blocks), RabbitMQ (no event bus — Shepherd uses the 1s level-triggered refresh loop), Postmark/email + jinja2 (no server-side rendering; the panel webview + ntfy push are the channels), and credits/billing (no API proxy). What remains is a pure operator pattern that maps 1:1 onto Shepherd's primitives, so the NEED is real but must be reshaped → ADAPT, not ADD. It reinforces L5 specifically (post-run self-summary + error-reason taxonomy are both already queued there), so I tie it to L5 rather than proposing a parallel notification system. The one net-new contribution beyond L5 is the BACKOFF/coalescing window for repeated errors — that's a genuine upgrade over the current once-per-episode dedupe and belongs on the watchdog/escalation path. Confidence is high: the existing FX.push/escalation/hung/transcriptError code makes the mapping unambiguous, and the AutoGPT-specific plumbing is cleanly separable.
- **How (Shepherd):** 1) DONE-summary: on a FRESH `done` edge (reuse the shouldAutoContinue/remoteControlSweepTargets edge-selection pattern), enrich the existing done notification + 🔔 history with a per-session run summary built by a pure cc-core fn (sibling to fleetStats/sessionTimeline): session label, provider/model, turns, approvals/denials, blocked-on-you time, wall-clock duration, done-edge ts — i.e. L5's post-run self-summary (todos.md:534) but as a status-derived report rather than a typed prompt. 2) ERROR alerts: extend the existing escalation/hung firing (dashboard.lua:5525-5564) and the transcriptError error state with `core.classifyError` (L5, todos.md:512) → block-failed vs continuous-error reason + retry/attempt count carried from the auto-respawn budget. 3) BACKOFF: add a pure `core.alertBackoff(key, type, now, windowSec=3600)` predicate beside isHung/approvalStale that collapses repeat error/hung episodes per-session over a 60-min window (state stored in the per-tile watchdog table that already carries `alerted`), gating both FX.push and the ledger write so a thrashing session can't spam ntfy. DROP: RabbitMQ, Postmark/email, jinja2, the executor hook, and credits/billing.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — ADAPT holds. I checked each downgrade lever against the code and none lands. (a) No executor needed. A session reaching "done" is read straight off the cc-status file (it.status=="done"; states idle/working/approval/done/error all already detected, plus the watchdog "hung" flag), and the error condition is parsed locally by core.transcriptError (cc-core.lua:1588, decodes the transcript tail's system/api_error line). No graph, no blocks, no execution state. Executor hook correctly stripped. (b) No server/credentials. The channels are FX.push→ntfy (claude-dashboard.lua:973) and the local 🔔 notification history (core.notificationEvents, cc-core.lua:787). RabbitMQ/Postmark/jinja2/email/credits correctly dropped. Local single-user, no secrets. (c) Redundancy/already-queued — strongest angle, but it does not fully collapse: - The DONE-summary maps onto L5 "Post-run self-summary callback" (todos.md:534) and the done-edge plumbing already exists (the "fresh done" transition drives drain-close at dashboard.lua:5457 and queue auto-feed at 5474; shouldAutoContinue/shouldDrainClose are the edge selectors). BUT note a nuance the judge overstated: there is currently NO "done" notification to "enrich" — M.NOTIFY_TYPES is {escalation, hung, auto_respawn, auto_continue} (cc-core.lua:786); "done" fires no push today. So the done-summary-as-push is net-new observability that extends L5's notification lane (still ADAPT, not ADD-a-new-system). - The ERROR-reason taxonomy (block-failed vs continuous-error) maps 1:1 onto L5 "Error-reason taxonomy" core.classifyError (todos.md:512). Already queued. Retry/attempt count is already carried by the auto-respawn per-folder budget (stepAutoRespawn attempts, cc-core.lua:2149) and shouldAutoContinue attempts (2200). - The escalation/hung once-per-episode push ALREADY exists (dashboard.lua:5525-5564) with the `alerted` dedupe. The one genuinely net-new, separable contribution is the BACKOFF/coalescing window. Today's dedupe is once-per-episode and re-arms on any progress/state change (cc-core.lua:2404: `if p.ts ~= w.ts then w.alerted = nil`), so a session flapping done→error→respawn→error would fire a fresh ntfy push every cycle. A pure core.alertBackoff(key,type,now,3600) predicate beside isHung, gating FX.push + the ledger write using the per-tile watchdog table that already holds `alerted`, is a real upgrade on the escalation/watchdog path — not redundant, not server/executor-bound. (d) Not platform plumbing — it's a pure operator-console concern (notify the human when their session finishes/errors) computed by a pure cc-core fn sibling to fleetStats/sessionTimeline. Conclusion: cannot downgrade to DONT_ADD (need is real, local-fit, and the backoff window is genuinely new); cannot upgrade to ADD (the done-summary + error-taxonomy halves are reinforcements of already-queued L5, and the alert/push machinery already exists). Verdict stays ADAPT, reinforcing L5 (post-run self-summary + error-reason taxonomy), with the backoff/coalescing predicate as the one net-new piece on the watchdog/escalation path. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md.

### Reinforces L2  (6)

#### Submission/review state machine + admin review queue (approve/deny with comments)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** The transferable kernel of this feature is a status lifecycle gated by a human reviewer who approves/rejects with a reason and a who/when/why decision record — which is EXACTLY Shepherd's reason for existing. The mapping is uncannily direct: PENDING ~ a session's armed gate (item.gate == "waiting"), APPROVED/REJECTED ~ writeDecision(key,"allow"|"deny") (cc-core.lua:131-134), reviewer_id+reviewed_at+comments ~ the gate decision log already rendering who/when in gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752) and the detail panel (claude-dashboard.lua:2099). The "paginated listings-with-versions, filter by status" admin queue ~ the tile grid, which already sorts approvals-first (RANK={approval=0} at cc-core.lua:50) — a de-facto fleet-wide pending-review board. The "preview a submission before approving" ~ jump-to-window/focus (cc-core.lua:128) + the backwards activity peek (cc-core.lua:1528) that lets the operator inspect what a session is about to do before deciding. The external/internal comment split (creator-visible deny reason + private operator note) and the required deny-reason are concrete, valuable enrichments to the decision log — and the deny-reason half is already queued under L2 (todos.md:368-371: fx.writeDecision(key,"deny",reason) → permissionDecisionReason → ledgered → "⛔ denied — <reason>"). So the human-review pattern lands cleanly on primitives Shepherd already owns and partly queued.
- **Against:** Strip the HITL veneer and what AutoGPT actually ships here is a MARKETPLACE submission pipeline, which hits the double mismatch on both barrels. The feature's real machinery is: StoreListingVersion with an ActiveVersion pointer, supersede-the-prior-pending-version semantics (store/db.py:892), embedding generation on approval, email notification, cache clearing, and an admin router gated by requires_admin_user — i.e. a multi-user web platform serving a public agent store. Shepherd is single-user, has no server, no email, no embeddings, and no marketplace to publish into; none of that plumbing can be hosted. Worse, the draft→published version state machine is something todos.md:365 ALREADY explicitly DROPPED for L2 ("git already versions cc-config.json"), so porting the version-supersede logic would re-introduce a rejected idea. And the genuinely useful core — gated approve/deny with a logged reason — is not new; it is Shepherd's shipped core plus an already-queued L2 enrichment. The risk is double-counting: treating this as a fresh feature would spawn a parallel "submission queue" system when the gate + decision log + approvals-first tile grid already are that queue.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch: the value of the AutoGPT feature splits cleanly. (1) The marketplace/multi-user half — StoreListingVersion+ActiveVersion, supersede-pending, embeddings, email, requires_admin_user, cache clearing — is DONT_ADD: it needs server + multi-user + marketplace serving Shepherd structurally cannot host, and the version state machine was already explicitly dropped (todos.md:365). (2) The operator-pattern half — a human reviewing pending items and approving/rejecting with a logged who/when/why reason — is real and maps directly onto Shepherd's CORE (the gate) plus existing surfaces (gateDecisionSummary, the approvals-first tile grid, focus/peek as "preview before approving"). Because the deny-reason piece is already queued under L2 and the rest is shipped, this is ADAPT-reinforces-L2 rather than a new system. The ONLY net-new, in-scope increment is enriching the gate decision log with an explicit external (creator/session-visible) vs internal (private operator note) comment distinction and a dedicated filterable pending-review panel chip — small, additive, and consistent with the off-by-default ledger. I weighed the user's saved-agents priority but it doesn't bear here (this is a review queue, not an agent registry). Effort is S: it extends the L2 deny-reason work and the existing decision-log rendering rather than building anything structural.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2's already-queued deny-reason work, not a new system. Concretely: (1) Extend FX.writeDecision (cc-core.lua:131-134) and cc-approve.sh emit_deny to carry TWO fields — an external reason (already mapped to permissionDecisionReason, surfaced to the session) and an optional private operator note (ledger-only, never sent to the session). (2) Enrich the ledger_decision event + gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752) to stamp reviewer-intent and render both comment tiers ("⛔ denied — <external> · note: <internal>") in the detail-panel decision log (claude-dashboard.lua:2099). (3) Optional small UI: a status-filter chip turning the approvals-first tile grid (RANK at cc-core.lua:50) into an explicit filterable "pending across the fleet" view, with the existing focus/peek (cc-core.lua:128/:1528) serving as "preview before approving." DROP: StoreListingVersion/ActiveVersion, supersede-pending version state machine (already dropped per todos.md:365), embeddings, email, requires_admin_user/multi-user, cache clearing.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute, but the ruling survives verification. The double mismatch is applied correctly and cleanly. The marketplace/multi-user/DB half — StoreListingVersion+ActiveVersion, supersede-pending version state machine, embeddings, email notification, requires_admin_user, cache clearing — is correctly stripped as DONT_ADD (needs server + multi-user + marketplace Shepherd structurally cannot host). I confirmed the version state machine was already explicitly dropped: todos.md:365 lists "the draft→published→prod version state machine (git already versions cc-config.json)" under DROP. The residual operator pattern — a human reviewing pending items and approving/rejecting with a logged who/when/why — is NOT a new system: it IS Shepherd's core gate. I verified the mappings: fx.writeDecision (cc-core.lua:131/134) and cc-approve.sh emit_deny (line 40-43, permissionDecisionReason) and ledger_decision (line 127), gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752), and the approvals-first RANK tile grid (cc-core.lua:48-50, sortByStatus line 85) all exist as the judge states. The deny-reason piece is genuinely QUEUED not shipped — todos.md:368 lists it under L2 crewAI-corroborated enrichments, and the current decisionContent (cc-core.lua:2882) only carries allow/deny+nonce with no reason field. The single net-new, in-scope increment the judge identifies — an explicit external (session/creator-visible) vs internal (private operator-only) comment distinction on the gate decision log — I confirmed does NOT exist anywhere (grep for internal/external/operator-note across cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, cc-approve.sh, todos.md returns nothing). That increment is small, additive, local/single-user, off-by-default-consistent, and reinforces the gate + L2. The user's saved-agents priority correctly does not bear (this is a review queue, not an agent registry). No common error applies: not the graph/block executor (the residual is the gate, not running anything), not the server/marketplace surface (those parts are dropped), not fully redundant (external/internal tiering is absent), not DB plumbing mistaken for a console feature (the DB/version table is dropped). I cannot push it to DONT_ADD (the comment-tiering is verifiably net-new and in-scope) nor to ADD (it folds into existing L2 work, not a new system). ADAPT reinforcing L2 is correct.

#### Dry-run / preview mode (simulate without real execution)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** "Preview what this would do before it spends compute or touches anything" is a genuinely useful operator gesture, and it fits the user's stated priority of pre-configured agents we hand work off to: before fanning a saved agent out across a fleet, you want a low-risk way to see what it would do. Shepherd has all the raw pieces to satisfy that need cheaply — Claude Code's `plan` permission mode (already in M.MODE_CYCLE and launchable via `--permission-mode plan` from both the New-session modal and detail panel) produces exactly a "propose, don't execute" session, and L2's queued read-only / no-Bash starter bundles (autoDeny the mutating gate set) make a session that can read and reason but cannot hit side effects. Packaging those as a one-click "preview / dry-run agent" spawn preset would be a small, on-model convenience that reuses the dry-run vocabulary Shepherd already speaks elsewhere (spawn.live, the queue's s-q-dry, route DRY-RUN).
- **Against:** The literal AutoGPT mechanism is non-transferable and most of the need is already met. AutoGPT's dry_run is a flag on its in-process executor: run_block/run_agent branch to a SIMULATION code path so the engine declines to actually call LLMs / run block DAG / hit side effects. Shepherd runs no graph and executes no blocks — Claude Code itself drives the session, and Shepherd cannot reach into a running Claude session and make it "simulate its own LLM calls / file writes." So the feature's actual engine has no home here. Worse, the operator-side value is largely SHIPPED or QUEUED: Shepherd's own control actions already dry-run (ORCH_DRY_RUN default-on, spawn.live OFF prints describeSpec, queue s-q-dry, route DRY-RUN), `plan` mode already exists as a launch flag, and L2 already queues the read-only/no-Bash bundles. Adding a separate "dry_run session metadata flag" would be a parallel system duplicating plan-mode + L2 rather than a new capability. It is also distinct from the trigger test-fire UX, so it doesn't even consolidate that.
- **Ruling:** This is the AutoGPT double mismatch in clean form. AutoGPT's dry_run is worth something only because there IS an in-process executor to short-circuit (run_block/run_agent → simulation path) — pure graph/block-execution plumbing Shepherd structurally lacks, which makes the literal feature DONT_ADD. But the underlying operator NEED — "spawn me a session that previews/plans without spending compute or touching anything" — is real and on-model, which pushes it to ADAPT rather than DONT_ADD. Crucially, Shepherd already satisfies most of it: `plan` is in M.MODE_CYCLE and is a real `--permission-mode plan` launch flag exposed in two spawn UIs, and L2 explicitly queues read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only starter bundles that autoDeny the mutating gate set — that pairing IS a preview/no-side-effect session. Shepherd also already uses "dry-run" semantics for its OWN actions (ORCH_DRY_RUN, spawn.live=false→describeSpec, queue s-q-dry, route DRY-RUN), so a separate session-level dry_run flag would duplicate vocabulary, not extend a primitive. The honest ruling: reinforce L2 (read-only/no-Bash bundles) and lean on the existing plan-mode preset; do not build AutoGPT's executor-branch flag. Effort is S because it's at most a labeled spawn preset over capabilities that already exist or are already queued — no new mechanism. Category is Blocks because the candidate's core (run_block/run_agent simulation branch) is squarely block-execution machinery; the transferable slice lands in Library/spawn-preset + gate territory, but the feature as proposed is a Blocks-executor concern.
- **How (Shepherd):** Do NOT add a session-metadata dry_run flag or any run_block/run_agent simulation branch (Shepherd has no executor to branch). Instead: (1) lean on the EXISTING `plan` permission mode — `M.MODE_CYCLE` includes "plan", `--permission-mode plan` is wired in core.spawnFlags and exposed in the New-session modal + detail-panel Mode select — a plan-mode spawn already previews "what would this do" without applying edits. (2) Fold the "no side effects, no spend beyond reasoning" half into L2's already-queued read-only / no-Bash starter bundles (todos.md L2: "ship 2-3 named bundles — read-only (autoDeny the mutating gate set), no-Bash, MCP-only"). (3) Optionally surface a one-click "Preview" spawn preset that = plan-mode + the read-only L2 bundle, reusing Shepherd's existing dry-run vocabulary (spawn.live OFF→describeSpec, queue s-q-dry, route DRY-RUN). No new primitive; this is a labeled preset over plan-mode + L2.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L2 + existing plan-mode) ruling holds. I verified every load-bearing claim in the codebase. The LITERAL AutoGPT feature is indeed the executor mismatch (path a): dry_run only has value because it short-circuits run_block/run_agent into a simulation branch — pure in-process graph/block-execution plumbing Shepherd structurally lacks. That makes the feature-as-written DONT_ADD, and the judge correctly routed AROUND it rather than building it. The transferable operator NEED ("spawn a session that previews/plans without spending compute or touching anything") is real and on-model, and Shepherd already satisfies it through capabilities I confirmed exist: (1) `plan` is in M.MODE_CYCLE (cc-core.lua:3013) and `--permission-mode plan` is a real launch flag emitted by M.spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:2994-3009), exposed in BOTH spawn UIs — the New-session modal `n-permmode` select with a Plan option (claude-dashboard.lua:3274-3276) and the detail-panel `mode` select (claude-dashboard.lua:3087). (2) L2's read-only/no-Bash/MCP-only starter bundles are explicitly queued in todos.md (L2: "ship 2-3 named bundles — read-only (autoDeny the DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS mutating set), no-Bash, MCP-only"). (3) Shepherd already owns dry-run vocabulary for its OWN actions — ORCH_DRY_RUN (claude-dashboard.lua:115), spawn.live=false→core.describeSpec (cc-core.lua:3143; claude-dashboard.lua:1553-1561), queue s-q-dry/queue.dryRun (claude-dashboard.lua:3137,5500), route DRY-RUN (claude-dashboard.lua:5642). I tried the redundancy refutation (everything already exists/is queued → push to DONT_ADD), but it actually only justifies the ADD→ADAPT downgrade the judge ALREADY made; it does not reach DONT_ADD because there IS a genuine, minimal reinforcement: the operator pattern maps cleanly and the optional one-click "Preview" preset = plan-mode + read-only L2 bundle is a thin label over existing-plus-queued primitives, not a parallel system. No new mechanism, no session-level dry_run metadata flag, no run_block/run_agent simulation branch. Correct verdict: ADAPT, reinforcing L2 and the existing plan permission mode.

#### Approve-with-edit + edit-payload validation/provenance  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** Shepherd's core IS human-in-the-loop approval, and AutoGPT's "pending human review" is the closest structural cousin to the gate. Two transferable nuggets are real and small: (1) deny-with-reason — let the human attach a free-text reason when they deny a gated request; the gate ALREADY supports this end-to-end (cc-approve.sh emit_deny wires permissionDecisionReason, lines 40-43), so it's just surfacing a reason field on the panel deny branch and ledgering it. (2) Provenance of human override — record who/what decided. Shepherd's ledger already stamps {outcome, by} on every decision (ledger_decision, cc-approve.sh:127), so "the human decided this" provenance is largely present; the AutoGPT pattern reinforces keeping the human-vs-auto distinction crisp. Both fold cleanly into the already-queued L2 transparency work (todos.md:369-371 explicitly names the deny-reason → gateDecisionSummary render).
- **Against:** The feature's actual payload — approve-WITH-EDIT — cannot exist in Shepherd. AutoGPT's value is mutating the node's input JSON (reviewed_data) so an EDITED value flows into graph execution on resume (wasEdited=true fed into node input). Shepherd has no payload to edit and no resume-with-modified-input path: the gate emits only permissionDecision allow|deny (cc-approve.sh:37-43), never updatedInput; tool_input is read-only, consumed solely to build a display SUMMARY string (cc-approve.sh:103-115). Claude Code owns the conversation and the tool arguments; Shepherd cannot intercept and rewrite them. The validation machinery (SafeJson, 1MB cap, depth<=10) guards a stored payload Shepherd doesn't have. So the headline feature is a DOUBLE MISMATCH: it depends on the in-process graph executor holding node input state AND on a server-side stored-payload model. Only the deny-reason/provenance crumbs survive, and those are already queued.
- **Ruling:** Applying the double mismatch: AutoGPT's approve-with-edit derives its entire worth from (a) the graph executor holding mutable node input that resumes with the edited value, and (b) a server-side stored payload that must be validated/capped. Shepherd has neither — its gate is binary allow/deny on a Claude-Code-owned permission prompt and never emits updatedInput to rewrite tool arguments (verified in cc-approve.sh: emit_allow/emit_deny only). So the headline capability is DONT_ADD. The verdict is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD, because the candidate itself flags two legitimate operator nuggets that map onto Shepherd's existing primitives: deny-with-reason and human-override provenance. Both are already queued under L2 (todos.md explicitly lists the deny-reason → handleAction deny branch → permissionDecisionReason → ledger → gateDecisionSummary chain), and the ledger already records {outcome, by} provenance. This reinforces existing queued work rather than proposing a parallel system — exactly the ADAPT/reinforces case. Net: take the reason+provenance, drop payload-editing and JSON validation.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2's transparency work, not a new feature. (1) Deny-reason: add an optional free-text reason input on the panel deny action → cc-core.lua handleAction deny branch (~:133) → fx.writeDecision(key,"deny",reason) → cc-approve.sh emit_deny already passes it as permissionDecisionReason; ledger the reason and render it in gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752) as "denied — <reason>". (2) Provenance: the ledger_decision {outcome, by} stamp (cc-approve.sh:127) already records human-vs-auto; ensure a human panel allow/deny is tagged by='human' so the "operator steered this" distinction is auditable in insights/shift-report. DROP entirely: tool-argument interception, reviewed_data storage, SafeJson/1MB/depth<=10 validation, wasEdited, and any resume-with-edited-input path — Shepherd does not own or rewrite tool payloads.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT ruling holds. The headline AutoGPT capability is correctly DONT_ADD, but the verdict survives as ADAPT because two real operator nuggets reinforce already-queued L2 work — verified in the code, not just asserted. WHAT I TRIED TO REFUTE WITH (and why it failed): - "It needs tool-argument interception / a resume-with-edited-input path Shepherd doesn't have, so it should be a clean DONT_ADD." This is true of the PAYLOAD-EDITING half and the judge already drops it. But the judge did not rest ADAPT on payload-editing; he rests it on deny-reason + provenance. Those are independently real, so the mismatch doesn't sink the whole verdict. - "The nuggets are redundant with existing features, so DONT_ADD." Partially true for provenance (already shipped) but deny-reason is NOT yet shipped — it's queued, which is exactly what ADAPT/reinforces means. VERIFIED FACTS: 1. No tool-payload rewrite exists. cc-approve.sh emit_allow (line 40) and emit_deny (lines 41-46) emit only {permissionDecision: allow|deny} (+ permissionDecisionReason on deny). grep across *.lua/*.sh for updatedInput/modify-tool_input/SafeJson/wasEdited/reviewed_data returns nothing. #### Permission gating policy: allow/deny tools, named bundles, recursive narrowing, graduated grant scope  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** Shepherd's whole reason for existing is human-in-the-loop permission control over a fleet of Claude Code sessions, and this is the most mature permission-policy design in the corpus. The operator NEEDS — and has already queued in L2 — exactly what this offers reshaped: turn the single flat anonymous policy (policies.patterns {autoAllow[],autoDeny[]} + the per-session gate.tools override) into NAMED, ATTACHABLE bundles with a clean precedence resolver. AutoGPT supplies battle-tested patterns that map onto Shepherd's primitives with no graph/server/credential dependency: (1) two-level (global=workspace, per-agent=session) first-match-wins resolution is literally L2's planned "session override > attached bundle > fleet default" resolver mirroring resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537); (2) the deny-by-default secrets/destructive starter set (.env/.pem/.key/rm -rf/sudo) is concrete prior art for L2's already-spec'd read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only starter bundles (todos.md:357-358); (3) the recursive "child can only ever be MORE restrictive than parent" rule is a genuinely valuable invariant for spawn/delegation that maps onto L4 parent/delegation lineage (todos.md:487-491) + L1's config-by-ref resolver-on-spawn; (4) "grants only widen, never silently narrow (re-config merges as a superset)" is a clean merge semantic worth stealing for bundle re-attach. It also directly serves the user's #1 stated priority — saved agents carry a default policy bundle (L1 profile.policyBundle, todos.md:256), so a permission bundle is the connective tissue between the saved-agents registry and the gate.
- **Against:** The candidate's headline mechanism — a tool ALLOW-LIST / WHITELIST (CopilotPermissions blacklist-or-whitelist of ToolName) — is structurally inverted from Shepherd's gate, which is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL/escalation list: tools NOT in gate.tools run freely (cc-approve.sh's `*) exit 0`), so adding a tool to the list RESTRICTS it, the opposite of granting (todos.md:236-239, cc-core.lua:2524-2547). Porting the whitelist verbatim would invert the safety model. Large parts are pure double-mismatch and DONT_ADD: builder-bound sessions auto-blacklisting "graph-minting tools" presumes an in-process graph BUILDER Shepherd does not have; block-level allow/deny (by block UUID/partial/name) presumes blocks/the DAG executor Shepherd does not run — the candidate's own note marks block-level allow/deny DONT_ADD. The Classic Forge layer brings command-string glob matching against a live agent loop's tool calls (CommandPermissionManager.check_command intercepts execution), but Shepherd executes no agent loop and sees no live command stream — it watches status files and steers via keyboard, so there is nothing to intercept at call time except the existing hook-driven approval gate. The graduated ApprovalScope (Once / This-agent / All-agents / Deny) overlaps heavily with what Shepherd already ships (per-session autopilot, approveRepeats, the nonce-bound gate), risking a parallel system. And "enforced/locked fields the session can't loosen" is L2's own weakest/deferred piece — a single-user console rarely needs to defend a preset against itself.
- **Ruling:** This is a textbook ADAPT that reinforces an already-queued item rather than a new system — the L2 spec was effectively written in anticipation of this exact AutoGPT feature, including the very inversion the candidate flags. Decompose it: the NEED (named, attachable, inheritable permission bundles with a deterministic resolver and safe defaults) is real, central to Shepherd's human-in-the-loop core, and explicitly queued as L2. The transferable patterns are clean and primitive-compatible: named bundles (policies.bundles), context-matched attachments (policies.attachments by project/group/provider/sessionKey), a single pure resolver mirroring resolveGateTools precedence, deny-by-default starter bundles, the recursive child-more-restrictive invariant on spawn/delegation, and superset-merge-on-reattach. The crux is the gate-semantics inversion: Shepherd's gate is escalation, not capability — so adopt the named-bundle + inheritance + merge SEMANTICS, but realize "read-only" via autoDeny of DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS (Bash Write Edit MultiEdit NotebookEdit), never via a whitelist. The genuinely NEW idea worth importing beyond current L2 text is the explicit monotonic-restriction guarantee for child sessions (intersect-with-parent) and the superset-merge-on-reconfig rule — both are pure cc-core invariants that fit L4 lineage and L1 spawn. The double-mismatch parts (builder graph-minting blacklist, block-level allow/deny, live command-stream glob interception, the in-loop executor enforcement) are DONT_ADD and the candidate's own note already concedes block-level is out. Net: ADAPT into L2, confidence high because the target structure, the resolver, the precedence, the starter bundles, and even the inversion caveat are already documented in todos.md.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2 (Named policy/guardrail bundles + attachments), enriching the existing spec rather than adding a system. Concretely: (1) policies.bundles = {name -> {autoAllow[], autoDeny[], gateTools, autopilot, lockedPermMode?, toolLimits?}} with optional per-rule {action: deny|allow|flag, tier: pre|observe} — already in todos.md:342-343. (4) NEW from this candidate — add a monotonic-restriction invariant: on spawn/delegate, the child's effective bundle is the parent's bundle UNION the child's autoDeny / autoAllow-removals (children can only narrow), enforced in L1's config-by-ref resolver-on-spawn and stamped in L4 parent/delegation lineage (todos.md:487-491); plus a superset-merge rule so re-attaching/editing a bundle widens-only unless explicitly tightened. #### Pending-review inbox + per-run review panel (count badge, live polling, batch approve/reject)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** This is the single strongest HITL fit AutoGPT offers, and most of it already exists in Shepherd — the operator pattern transfers almost 1:1 onto local primitives. (b) The "N Reviews Pending" badge + poll-while-active + auto-hide maps onto the existing ~1s refresh loop and the notification/escalation badge machinery (unseenNotificationCount, escalation 🔴). (c) Batch Approve-all/Reject-all is exactly the existing bulk-action engine — BULK_RULES + selectActionable already do approve-all/stop-all/nudge-all over the visible set, scoped by GROUPS. (d) auto-approve-future per node is a finer-grain AUTOPILOT: Shepherd already has per-session time-boxed autopilot and approveRepeats (remember an approved request and auto-allow it next time); a per-tool/per-rule "auto-approve future Read calls in this session" keyed by a stable id is a natural extension and is already specified as L2's per-rule action:allow. (e) Reject-with-a-note is ALREADY designed in L2's deny-reason work (handleAction deny -> fx.writeDecision(key,"deny",reason) -> cc-approve.sh emit_deny sets permissionDecisionReason, ledgered, rendered by gateDecisionSummary). All five pieces run on the nonce-bound gate over local status files — no server, no executor, no credentials. It directly serves the watch/steer console's core HITL purpose and the operator's real pain (being the approval bottleneck across many sessions), which Shepherd already measures (approvalBlockedSeconds).
- **Against:** Almost everything of value here Shepherd ALREADY HAS, so the risk is re-adding a parallel "review queue" system instead of extending the gate/bulk/autopilot/L2 it already ships — the verdict must be ADAPT-by-reuse, not a fresh build. The AutoGPT implementation specifics are pure double-mismatch plumbing that must NOT come along: Prisma find_many filtered userId+WAITING with pagination assumes a multi-user DB Shepherd structurally lacks (single-user, reads ~/.claude/cc-status/*.json off the local FS); the React Query refetchInterval is just a web-poll Shepherd's 1s Lua loop already covers; and most importantly the API's all-or-nothing rule — "the POST /action requires ALL reviews for a run together, same graph_exec_id" — is a GRAPH-EXECUTION-STATE constraint (a node can't proceed until every pending review on that run is resolved) with no analog in Shepherd, where each session's approval is independent; importing it would be actively wrong, which is why the candidate correctly says "drop the all-or-resume-blocked rule." The auto_approve special DB row and check-before-creating-a-review live inside the executor's review-creation path Shepherd doesn't run. So if you tried to "build it close to as-is" (ADD), you'd be hauling in DB/executor assumptions; the only honest port is reshaping the operator UX onto existing gate + bulk + autopilot + L2 deny-reason primitives.
- **Ruling:** The double mismatch cleanly separates plumbing from pattern. The plumbing (Prisma userId+WAITING query, the same-graph_exec_id all-reviews-together constraint, the auto_approve DB row, React Query) all assumes AutoGPT's multi-user DB + in-process graph executor that holds node/graph execution state — none of which Shepherd has, and the all-or-nothing rule is a graph-blocked-until-resolved semantic with no Shepherd analog. But the OPERATOR pattern — a fleet-wide pending-review inbox with a count badge, poll-while-active + auto-hide, batch decide, per-tool auto-approve-future, and reject-with-a-note — is precisely Shepherd's HITL core, and Shepherd already implements ~80% of it: RANK-sorts approvals first, filterTiles, approvalStale wait-time, BULK_RULES/selectActionable batch actions over GROUPS, the 1s poll, escalation/notification badges, time-boxed autopilot + approveRepeats. The two genuinely-new pieces — per-tool/per-rule auto-approve keyed by a stable id, and free-text reject reason fed back via the gate — are BOTH already specified as L2 work (per-rule action:allow; the crewAI-corroborated deny-reason wired through fx.writeDecision -> cc-approve.sh emit_deny -> permissionDecisionReason -> ledger -> gateDecisionSummary). So this reinforces L2 rather than being a new system. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L2, high confidence. Category Human-in-the-Loop. Not DONT_ADD because nothing of value depends on the executor/DB/marketplace; not ADD because building it as-is would import the executor/DB constraints and duplicate existing gate/bulk/autopilot machinery.
- **How (Shepherd):** Inbox = add an "approval" state FILTER to the existing tile grid (reuse filterTiles + the RANK approval=0 sort + approvalStale/`since` for wait-time ordering) with a fleet count badge driven off the existing unseenNotificationCount/escalation machinery and the ~1s refresh loop (no new poller, no auto-hide infra needed beyond "hide when count==0"). Batch Approve-all/Reject-all = extend the existing BULK_RULES{approve=match"approval"} + selectActionable over the visible/grouped set; explicitly DROP AutoGPT's all-reviews-same-graph_exec_id rule (no analog). auto-approve-future per node = L2 per-rule action:allow keyed by a stable id (autoAllow a specific tool for a session/bundle), routed through the existing cc-approve.sh autoDeny->autopilot->autoAllow->approveRepeats pipeline (KEEP cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh in sync per the gate-tools contract). So the one executor-bound piece is correctly DROPPED and is not load-bearing. No server/DB/OAuth survives: the reshaped feature reads local cc-status files and writes decision files on the nonce-bound gate. (a) Executor — NOT triggered: the value is the HITL inbox/badge/batch-decide/reject-note, not running graphs; the only executor-coupled constraint is dropped. #### Stop / cancel a run (queued-vs-running distinction, cascade to children, clean teardown)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** Three sub-ideas inside this candidate are genuinely useful for a steer console and aren't fully realized today. (1) The queued-vs-running distinction is real and cheap: Shepherd already separates "a queued task" (a line in the projectKey queue file, removable via the queue-remove handler at claude-dashboard.lua:1832) from "a running turn" (the escape keystroke in handleAction "stop", cc-core.lua:136). Today the Stop button only interrupts a working turn; cancelling a not-yet-dispatched queued task is a separate queue edit the operator has to find. Unifying them under one "Stop" affordance (cancel-before-start flips to idle with zero compute; interrupt-while-running sends escape) is a clean UX win that costs almost nothing because both primitives exist. (2) Stopping a session that is parked in approval state should atomically deny the pending gate prompt AND record "stopped by user" in the gate decision log — right now stopping mid-approval leaves the armed nonce/decision and the queued permission ambiguous. The gate log, FX.writeDecision, and removeStatus all exist, so wiring "stop while gate=waiting → write deny('stopped by user') then teardown" is a small, well-fitting refinement that prevents orphaned approvals. (3) The orphan/stuck-reclamation framing (force-complete fallback for a turn that never leaves running) is the same need Shepherd's stuck-session watchdog + auto-respawn/drain already serve, and the candidate is a useful checklist for hardening that teardown path.
- **Against:** The headline mechanic — recursively cascade a stop down a delegation tree by walking ChildExecutions via a parent FK — structurally does not exist in Shepherd and was already explicitly rejected. Shepherd's "lineage" is a ledger-derived ROLLUP of respawn/clear/spawn churn (cc-core.lua lineageByProject ~1061), NOT a live parent-child execution graph with a foreign key to recurse over. There is no in-process executor holding a tree of running nodes to cancel. More decisively, todos.md L4 already made the call: "DROP cascade-delete (never auto-kill a child the human is steering)" and "call the new edge parent/delegation" — a human-in-the-loop console must never silently kill a sub-session the operator may be actively steering, which is exactly what recursive cascade-stop would do. The "stop a graph run stops every active execution" + "update_many WAITING->REJECTED then TERMINATED" + RabbitMQ/Redis cancel-event fanout with status polling all presuppose the AutoGPT executor and message broker — the double mismatch: there's no graph to stop and no server/worker to publish a cancel event to. Bulk-stop-a-group, the legitimate operator need underneath cascade, is ALREADY shipped (session groups + the bulk action over visible keys at claude-dashboard.lua:2197, which routes each through the same handleAction "stop"). So most of the candidate is either redundant with shipped features or barred by a prior design decision.
- **Ruling:** Apply the double mismatch. The transport (broker fanout + status poll) is DONT_ADD — no server, no worker. The "stop the graph run, terminate every node, atomic update_many over execution rows" is DONT_ADD — no graph executor, no execution table. The recursive cascade-to-children is barred not just by missing primitives (lineage is a rollup, no parent FK) but by an explicit prior decision in L4: never auto-kill a child the human is steering. Bulk-stop-a-group, the real need cascade gestures at, is already shipped via groups + bulk. What survives the filter is small and good: the queued-vs-running unification (both primitives exist — queue-remove + escape-stop), and the approval-state teardown (stop while gate=waiting → atomic deny with "stopped by user" recorded in the existing gate decision log). The orphan/force-complete framing reinforces the existing watchdog/drain rather than adding a system. So the underlying NEED (a clean, state-aware Stop that knows whether work is queued vs running and tears down a parked approval cleanly) is real and must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives — that is ADAPT, and it sits inside the L4 delegation/lineage decision (which already owns the parent/child stance), so reinforces=L4. The cascade headline itself is DONT_ADD-flavored, which keeps confidence high on the overall ADAPT-not-ADD ruling.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reshape into two concrete, scoped refinements, both inside L4's parent/delegation lineage work. (A) State-aware Stop: in cc-core.handleAction (cc-core.lua:136) branch on session state — if the session is idle but has queued tasks for its projectKey, route "stop" to the existing queueRemove path (drop the head / clear queue) and ledger a queue_edit op="cancel" instead of sending escape; if working, keep KEY_STOP (escape). Surface one Stop affordance in the panel that does the right thing per state. (B) Approval-state teardown: when handleAction "stop" fires while item.gate == "waiting", first FX.writeDecision(item.key, "deny") with reason "stopped by user" so the parked nonce/approval is resolved and logged in the gate decision log (claude-dashboard.lua:2099), THEN do teardown — no orphaned approval. For the "stop a whole cohort" need, point at the SHIPPED bulk-stop over a group (groups + claude-dashboard.lua:2197 bulk → handleAction "stop"); do NOT add recursive cascade — honor L4's "DROP cascade-delete / never auto-kill a child the human is steering," at most offer a manual "stop parent + its delegation children" as an explicit operator-confirmed bulk over the parent/delegation edge once that edge exists. Drop the broker fanout, status-poll, and execution-row update_many entirely (no server/executor).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; ADAPT holds, reinforces=L4. The double-mismatch parts are correctly excluded and I verified the primitives, so there is nothing left to downgrade. WHAT I TRIED TO REFUTE: The strongest path to DONT_ADD is "redundant — Shepherd already has stop." The code disproves it. handleAction "stop" (cc-core.lua:136-137) is state-BLIND: it unconditionally calls fx.actOnWindow(tgt, M.KEY_STOP) (escape). It does NOT branch on idle/working, does NOT touch the queue, and does NOT write a gate decision when item.gate=="waiting". So two genuinely-missing LOCAL behaviors remain: (A) Stop on an idle session that has queued tasks should cancel the queue head (the queueRemoveAt primitive exists at cc-core.lua:1906; queue-remove action + queue_edit ledger op exist at dashboard:1832 / cc-core.lua:663) instead of firing a useless escape into an idle window; (B) Stop while gate=="waiting" should resolve the parked nonce-bound approval with a logged "stopped by user" deny (FX.writeDecision at dashboard:1121 + decisionContent nonce-binding at cc-core.lua:2882 + gate decision log at dashboard:2099/2771) BEFORE teardown, so no orphaned approval. Both are small, single-user, no-server, no-executor refinements onto shipped primitives — a real improvement to the local console, so it cannot honestly be downgraded to DONT_ADD. WHY NOT ADD: it is reinforcement, not a new system. The headline (cascade-stop across a graph, broker/RabbitMQ fanout, status poll, execution-row update_many) is correctly stripped as DONT_ADD — Shepherd runs no graph/blocks and has no execution table. Recursive cascade-to-children is doubly barred: lineage is a pure ledger ROLLUP with no parent FK (cc-core.lua:1050-1081, "deliberately NO new persisted lineage chain"), and todos.md:491 records the explicit L4 decision "DROP cascade-delete (never auto-kill a child the human is steering)." The "stop a whole cohort" need is already shipped via groups + bulk-stop (dashboard:2197; selectActionable + BULK_RULES.stop={match="working"} at cc-core.lua:395). Orphan/force-complete maps onto the existing watchdog/drain, not a new daemon. REINFORCES=L4: the surviving teardown/parent-stance work sits inside L4's parent/delegation/lineage decision, which already owns the parent/child policy — so it reinforces L4 rather than spawning a parallel system. ADD->ADAPT escalation is inapplicable (already ADAPT). Ruling stands at ADAPT, confidence high. Relevant files (absolute): /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (handleAction:119-198, stop branch:136-137, queueRemoveAt:1906, BULK_RULES/selectActionable:395-419, decisionContent:2882, lineageByProject rollup:1050-1081); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (FX.writeDecision:1121, bulk handler:2197, gate decision log:2099); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L4 cascade-delete drop:491).

#### Sub-agent / nested-agent execution + delegation (run a saved agent as a step / spawn a child)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The operator pattern at the heart of this feature — "reference a saved agent by id, hand work to a child, track its result, stop it on failure, and notice when a newer version of that referenced agent exists" — is precisely the user's stated #1 priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory") and it lands squarely on two backlog items already designed for Shepherd's primitives. L4's "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" already specifies: addressable identity from L1's cc-agents.json (the graph_id/version reference), a rendered L3 task as the handoff payload, enqueue-onto-the-named-agent's-queue delivered by the router, dry-run-able and ledgered. L4's "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage" already specifies stamping the spawn/route ledger event with parent_session/spawnedBy and drawing parent->child edges in the topology view — which is exactly "parent waits, child runs, result rolls back up" minus the in-process plumbing. The "newer version of an embedded sub-agent" detector maps cleanly onto L1's optional versions[] field on an agent profile as a passive "this saved agent has a newer version" tile/detail notice. The "child is never more permissive than parent" rule is already expressible via L1's config-by-ref resolver + L2 bundle attach + gate.tools narrowing on spawn (resolveGateTools), so inherited-but-narrowed gating costs nothing new. Crucially the local claude CLI present on this box supports --agent/--agents/--mcp-config/--append-system-prompt, so a Shepherd "Delegate to agent X" genuinely CAN spawn a scoped child Claude Code session — the delegation half is real and buildable, it just needs no new engine.
- **Against:** The feature's defining VALUE in AutoGPT is the execution engine, and every load-bearing mechanism is something Shepherd structurally cannot host. AgentExecutorBlock's core is add_graph_execution: it starts a CHILD GRAPH EXECUTION inside the in-process DAG executor, with a parent_execution_id FK, an event-bus that STREAMS the child's OUTPUT block results back into the parent node, merge_stats that ROLLS UP cost/step counts, and stop-the-child-on-failure orchestration — Shepherd runs no graph, executes no blocks, and holds no execution state, so there is literally nothing to stream up or roll up. OrchestratorBlock "discovers downstream blocks via topology" — there is no block topology in Shepherd. CoPilot's run_sub_session is even further out of scope: it enqueues a CoPilotExecutionEntry onto a WORKER QUEUE, the parent BLOCKS on the child's Redis stream via a session_waiter, then polls get_sub_session_result — a server-side job queue + Redis pub/sub + a waiting parent process, none of which exist in a single-user Hammerspoon panel that has NO network server, NO Redis, NO worker queue, and never runs an agent loop. The "cost rollup" depends on AutoGPT's billing/credit ledger (Shepherd makes no API calls and never sees token cost as money). Building any of this AS-IS would mean importing the executor and a job-queue runtime — a category error. So ADD is impossible and even the synchronous "parent waits up to N seconds then collects result" semantics don't fit a panel that watches status files on a ~1s tick rather than blocking on a stream.
- **Ruling:** The candidate self-bisects, and that bisection is correct. The DOUBLE MISMATCH knocks out the entire engine half: add_graph_execution / child graph execution state / event-bus output streaming / cost rollup / CoPilot worker queue + Redis stream wait / merged-in-process permissions all require the in-process graph-and-block executor and a server-side job queue that Shepherd explicitly forbids (no server, no Redis, no agent loop, no block topology, no billing). That is textbook DONT_ADD for the implementation. But the candidate's own SHEPHERD note is right that the CONCEPT — reference a saved agent, hand work to a scoped child, track it, stop it, collect its result later, be told when the referenced agent has a newer version — is a real operator need that is already designed into the backlog. It is not a new system: L4 "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" + "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage" already specify the delegate-render-enqueue-route flow and the parent->child ledger edge, and L1 already carries the saved-agent-by-id reference (cc-agents.json), the versions[] field for the update notice, and the config-by-ref + gate-narrowing resolver for inherited-but-never-more-permissive permissions. So per the rubric (the underlying need is real but must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives AND it extends something already queued), this is ADAPT with reinforces=L4. The cross-session nudge already exists as the fire-and-forget "ask coworker" variant; the synchronous result-collection is replaced by Shepherd's native model — the child is a real Claude Code session, its status/done/outputs surface as its own tile, and the parent->child edge in the topology view plus a task_done ledger event is how "result rolls up." No real-time stream needed; the ~1s status loop and the ledger ARE the rollup. Effort is M because L4's pure dispatcher/topology/lineage scaffolding is the bulk and most of it is already specced (routeTask/routePick/lineageByProject), with the delegation extension + parent_session stamp + the L1 versions[] notice the incremental work; it depends on L1's cc-agents.json shipping first. Net: keep the delegation/handoff + parent-child-lineage + saved-agent-version-notice pattern, drop the executor, the event bus, the cost rollup, and the Redis worker queue entirely.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4 (reinforces L4), drawing on L1: (1) "Delegate to agent X" = an opt-in, dry-run-able action that resolves an L1 cc-agents.json profile by name (the graph_id reference), renders an L3 {{var}} task as the handoff payload, and either enqueues onto that named agent's projectKey-keyed queue (delivered by the shipped router, ledger by:"router"/delegation) or spawns a fresh scoped child via the local `claude --agent/--mcp-config/--append-system-prompt` CLI with gate.tools narrowed by resolveGateTools + the profile's L2 bundle (inherited-but-never-more-permissive). (2) Parent/child tracking = stamp the spawn/route ledger event with parent_session/spawnedBy (already specced under L4 "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage"); show parent->child edges in the L4 routing-topology view and collapse children under the parent via session groups. The child's "result" is its own live tile + a task_done ledger event rolled into fleetStandup / the Shift tab — no event-bus stream, no in-process output object. (3) "Stop the child on failure" = the existing per-session Stop action, NOT cascade-delete (L4 explicitly DROPs cascade-delete: never auto-kill a child the human is steering). (4) "Newer version of referenced agent" = passive notice off L1's optional versions[] field — a detail-panel line / off-by-default tile badge, sibling to stale/hung; no compatibility-checked auto-update engine. DROP entirely: add_graph_execution, parent_execution_id child-graph execution, event-bus output streaming, merge_stats cost rollup, OrchestratorBlock topology-discovery, CoPilot run_sub_session worker queue + Redis session_waiter + get_sub_session_result polling, and the in-process merged-permission object.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute and could not. The feature cleanly bisects, and the codebase confirms both halves. (1) The DONT_ADD-engine half hits the AutoGPT double mismatch: AgentExecutorBlock's add_graph_execution / parent_execution_id child-graph execution state, OrchestratorBlock topology-discovery, the event-bus OUTPUT streaming, merge_stats cost rollup, and CoPilot run_sub_session's Redis worker queue + session_waiter + get_sub_session_result polling all require the in-process graph/block executor AND a server-side job queue. Shepherd runs no graph, executes no blocks, has no server/Redis/multi-user (context.md L13: "no network, no multi-user, no secrets"). So it cannot be a clean ADD. (2) The transferable CONCEPT does NOT secretly need that engine, and the cited Shepherd primitives all exist or are specced: reference-saved-agent-by-id = L1 cc-agents.json (agentList/agentPush specced, todos L254-258); spawn scoped child = L1 spawn-from-agent (spawnSpec cc-core.lua:3061, spawnFlags:3001) + L4 delegation (routeTask:2062 / routePick:2041 / sessionFree:2020 verified); never-more-permissive = resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537) + L2 resolver; parent->child tracking = L4 sub-conversations/parent-delegation lineage (todos L487-492) on lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1061); stop child = existing per-session Stop with cascade-delete explicitly DROPped (todos L490-491); collect-result-later = child's own live tile + task_done ledger event (todos L473-477), no in-process output object; newer-version notice = L1 versions[] passive badge (profile schema L257-258). (3) It's not redundant — these are QUEUED not shipped, and the rubric maps "real underlying need that reshapes onto primitives AND extends something already queued" to ADAPT with reinforces=L4. Every refutation path (secret executor dependency -> DONT_ADD; full redundancy -> DONT_ADD; verbatim fit -> ADD) fails. ADAPT (reinforces L4, drawing on L1) is correct.
