Templates exist as {name, text} (cc-core.lua:3590 templateList / 3604 templatePush), but carry NO var schema today; templateVars/renderTemplate/missingEnv do not exist yet (grep returns nothing) — they are proposed in L3/L1, so this is in-flight enrichment, not already-built. (4) Already QUEUED — which makes it ADAPT, not a parallel system. todos.md L3 line 423-426 already specifies the exact 'Input-schema collection' pattern: 'the New-Session modal renders one field per var, disables Spawn/Feed until required are filled.' L3 line 416-419 already enriches the template record past {name,text}. L1 line 287-294 already specifies requiredEnv:[{name,description,required,default}] plus a pure pre-flight validator family (sibling to presetList ~3658) that 'refuses spawn/attach with the full list before emitting any flag' — precisely the get_field_errors-style aggregation. SchemaField's hint taxonomy (advanced/placeholder/ge-le/length/choice) is the field-metadata vocabulary that enriches these existing items. So: neither pure ADD (literal form depends on marketplace web-builder + server settings) nor DONT_ADD (stripped of secrets + JSON-Schema web-rendering, the metadata vocabulary + introspect-and-validate pattern lands squarely on existing backlog). That is ADAPT. reinforces=L3 is correct — the form-widget-from-declared-schema and input-schema-collection pattern is most directly L3's (line 423), with a secondary feed into L1's validator (line 290-294). Hand-render the 4-5 widget types (string/number-bounded/bool/choice/path); drop SECRET widget, .env read-write, Pydantic, the jsonschema emitter, and discriminator/depends_on beyond a trivial show-if. Effort M (enrichment, not a new subsystem). Ruling unrefuted.

---

## ❌ DON'T-ADD — 25 items

### Library & Organization (5)
- **Special graph-interface blocks (Input / Output / Note / typed input variants)** — The four block families fail the double mismatch decisively. INPUT/OUTPUT exist solely to define a graph's external interface (Jinja2 output formatting, advanced flags) — value depends on running the graph, which Shepherd has none of. Typed-input variants are SchemaField, already covered by L3's input-schema. StoreValue/TypeConverter/ReverseList/FileStore are stateless DAG primitives requiring the in-process block executor Shepherd cannot host. The candidate's own read is right: at most one crumb, NoteBlock. But that crumb is both minor and already-settled: Shepherd has relabel (setLabel) and groups (setGroup, cc-groups.json), and the pad-mirror batch explicitly DECLINED "durable conventions+notes" as domain creep / CLAUDE.md duplication (todos.md line 150). Adding a free-text note now would re-open a decision the team just made against, for a feature whose bulk is graph plumbing. DONT_ADD with high confidence; if a note ever lands, it should ride L1 (annotating saved agent profiles) rather than being introduced from this AutoGPT block.
- **Launch a saved agent + per-agent run-history/status rollup** — Apply the double mismatch: the candidate's worth does NOT depend on running graphs/blocks, executing block code, storing OAuth creds, or multi-user/marketplace serving -- so it clears the DONT_ADD bar. It is descriptive analytics over events Shepherd already records, which is precisely the transferable AutoGPT pattern (run-history/library management reshaped onto Shepherd primitives: graph run ~ session, library agent ~ saved profile). The "launch" half is redundant with L1, but the rollup half is a real, additive need: today you can roll up by project and by session, but not by SAVED AGENT, which is the unit the user cares about ("which of my pre-configured agents is reliable?"). The build is a per-profile slice of the existing fleetStats/lineage aggregation plus a card on L1's agent registry, gated by the opt-in ledger. The one real prerequisite -- stamping the originating profile name onto the spawn ledger event / session status so events can be grouped by profile -- is a thin addition inside L1's already-specced spawn-from-agent path (it already plans a by:"batch" ledger event and per-session usage aggregation into insights), not new infrastructure. Drop avg_correctness_score (no local scoring), keep run-count, success-rate (best-effort from done/error terminal states), recent-runs, status badge, and new-output indicator. This is a clean ADAPT that REINFORCES L1 rather than a parallel system. The faint scheduling/dashboard flavor doesn't change the home: the core need is the agent-card rollup, which belongs to L1.
- **Adopt-into-my-list (one-click add-and-use, with undo)** — Apply the double mismatch: the AutoGPT impl is squarely the (b) multi-user/marketplace/library-serving half — resolve a remote store_listing_version_id, copy a graph across user libraries, cache-invalidate, fire analytics. Shepherd has no server, no store, no per-user library to copy INTO, so the "install/adopt from a remote source" plumbing is structurally impossible and correctly DONT_ADD (the candidate concedes this). What survives the mismatch is purely an operator interaction pattern — one-click "make this stored thing usable now," with toast + Undo + a forward path — which is legitimately transferable per the rubric's instruction to judge the operator pattern, not the plumbing. That pattern does not stand alone: it is exactly the polish layer for L1's already-queued "spawn from a saved agent" action, so the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L1, never ADD (no remote install) and never a parallel system. The user's stated #1 priority — a directory of pre-configured agents to hand work off to — weighs favorably, and a frictionless one-click adopt-and-open is what makes that directory feel like a launchpad rather than a form. Effort is S: spawn-from-agent already lives in L1's plan (spawnSpec, queue-seed, spawnFlags); close/jump/tile-filter already exist for Undo and "See runs"; the only net-new primitive is a generic toast+undo affordance, small and reusable. I deliberately do not let the absent remote-copy half inflate the score — the kept slice is a thin, high-leverage UX win on top of an existing backlog item, not a new capability.
- **Pending-message queue (type a follow-up while busy, drain when idle)** — Apply the double mismatch: AutoGPT's value here is NOT the executor/graph or the marketplace — it's an operator pattern (don't drop the human's follow-up while the agent is mid-turn; replay it when free). That pattern transfers. But the autogpt_impl (Redis buffer, 409 turn-in-flight contract, replay submit payload) is server/request plumbing Shepherd cannot and should not host — so this is ADAPT, never ADD. It maps cleanly onto Shepherd's primitives: the existing per-session queue is the buffer; "turn in flight" = status != `done` (already encoded in sessionFree/routePendingDone); "drain when idle" = the level-triggered dispatcher on the ~1s refresh tick. It is not DONT_ADD because none of its worth depends on running a graph, executing blocks, storing OAuth creds, or multi-user serving — and it reinforces, rather than duplicates, an item already on the backlog. The unique copy-worthy nugget is the non-consuming peek that survives a webview refresh to re-show the queued-message badge, plus consume-on-completion semantics. Set reinforces=L4 because the docs already file the pending-message queue under L4; the per-session follow-up-while-busy variant is the operator-facing complement to L4's project-keyed queue-before-ready item. Effort is S: it's a tile badge, a peek (read-only) accessor over existing queue state, and reusing the existing dispatcher/in-flight guard — no new subsystem.
- **Draft autosave + recovery + undo/redo authoring history** — Double mismatch applies cleanly to the canvas core (no graph, no executor) → those parts are DONT_ADD. But the candidate's own framing correctly isolates a transferable operator pattern that maps onto a NEED Shepherd has already QUEUED: not losing / being able to undo in-progress edits to a saved agent profile (L1) or task template (L3). That is reshapeable onto Shepherd's primitives — flat JSON records, the existing modal/persist path, and the webview localStorage already in use — so the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L1, not a parallel system and not DONT_ADD. Drop everything graph (Dexie/IndexedDB, graph-diff recovery, node/edge undo, copy/paste-with-internal-links); keep a small localStorage-backed draft autosave + "recover unsaved changes" prompt + a shallow undo stack scoped to the L1/L3 authoring modal.

### Integrations & Credentials (5)
- **MCP tool integration: dynamic discovery + dynamic schema + saved per-agent MCP config** — The candidate decomposes into three parts and the AutoGPT double mismatch sorts them cleanly. (1) The dynamic-schema input-form-rebuild that lets the block CALL the tool is the in-process block/graph executor — DONT_ADD; Claude Code, not Shepherd, invokes MCP tools at runtime, and the CoPilot run_mcp_tool OAuth path is the forbidden credential store. (2) Saved per-agent MCP config / config-by-ref is already fully L1 (mcpServers[], the config-by-ref resolver, --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config) — reinforcement, not new. (3) The one genuinely new, transferable piece is DISCOVERY: enumerate an MCP server's real tools so the authoring UI can present them. L1 today assumes hand-typed allowedTools[]; the discovery action removes that guess-work and feeds both the profile editor and the gate escalation wiring. Because the claude CLI is local and exposes `claude mcp` subcommands, discovery is an FX shell-out — no in-process MCP protocol code, no server. So: ADAPT, reinforces L1, with the discovery action as the concrete add and the executor/credential half explicitly dropped. Effort S because it slots one FX call + one picker-population step into the already-L-effort L1 MCP-registry work rather than standing up anything new.
- **Per-user credential store + auth types + OAuth login/refresh/scoping (the credential subsystem)** — This is the canonical AutoGPT double mismatch on both axes at once. The credential subsystem is (a) deep graph-execution plumbing — resolve-by-id and lock-for-duration inject creds INTO a block at run time, refresh fires when a node is about to call an LLM/external API — and (b) multi-user-platform plumbing — OAuth login/callback routes, managed/system org keys, per-user provisioning, marketplace-grade delete guards. Shepherd is neither an executor nor a server: it WATCHES status files and STEERS GUI sessions, and Claude Code (not Shepherd) handles any actual auth via the spawned shell's $ENV. context.md's "no secrets storage" is a hard, explicit veto on the core artifact. Crucially, the user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory) is already served by the L1 registry, which solved the credential problem the RIGHT way for a local single-user console: store the env-var NAME (authTokenEnv / requiredEnv[]), never the value, and let the login shell expand it at spawn — giving "this connection needs CREDENTIAL_X" preflight checks with zero secret custody. So the operator NEED (know which connections an agent requires, validate they're present, apply host-matched config) is real and already addressed; the credential-custody machinery is the part that doesn't transfer. The four design echoes are all pre-existing/queued, not net-new, so there is nothing to ADAPT here that isn't already captured. Verdict DONT_ADD with high confidence — the part with value can't be hosted, and the part that could is already shipped or queued.
- **Scoped API keys + third-party OAuth-app authorization (programmatic access)** — The feature is two server/IdP capabilities fused: programmatic-caller API keys and an OAuth2 authorization server. Both depend on the things Shepherd structurally cannot host — an inbound server, multiple users/tenants, and stored credentials/tokens — which context.md forbids outright ("No network, no multi-user, no secrets"). That is a clean DONT_ADD on the multi-user/credential-serving prong of the double mismatch. The only piece worth mining, the permission-enum-as-named-capabilities, is not new value: Shepherd already ships per-session tool gating plus a nonce-bound approval gate, and the generalization into named/attachable/revocable bundles is already the queued L2 work — which has even pre-emptively rejected the auth/identity/token axes this candidate brings ("Drop credentials/impersonation/delegation-token (no-secrets/no-auth scope)"). So there is no incremental concept to add and no primitive to reshape onto. I do NOT set reinforces, because the feature does not extend L2 — L2 already exists and already declined precisely this feature's distinctive (auth/credential) content; calling it a reinforcement would overstate the candidate's contribution. Confidence is high: the scope conflict is explicit in context.md and the adjacent need is demonstrably already covered.
- **Built-in command/ability catalog as composable components (with per-config tool toggle)** — Apply the double mismatch: the components (Docker exec, Playwright, image-gen, git ops) are graph/block-executor capabilities Shepherd structurally cannot host — those are DONT_ADD and stay there. But the prompt says to judge the operator pattern, not the block plumbing, and two operator patterns survive the filter and both reinforce already-queued L1 work rather than proposing a parallel system. (1) Per-config tool scoping on a saved agent IS L1's "per-task tool restriction at spawn" (todos.md 372-375), correctly reshaped onto gate semantics — and the gate-semantics caveat (236-239) already records the inverse-direction subtlety so this won't be mis-built as an allow-list. (2) Command name/description/JSONSchema introspection IS L1's slash-autocomplete + skills-card surfacing (311-322). Because the real need maps onto something Shepherd already has queued, the rubric makes this ADAPT with reinforces=L1, not DONT_ADD (which is reserved for features whose worth depends on the executor/credentials/marketplace). Confidence is high because todos.md already documents both mappings verbatim, including the inverse-direction gotcha. No net-new build — these are field/enrichment confirmations on L1, so effort is small.
- **External chat-platform bots (Discord/Slack adapters) + per-user usage metering/rate-limit** — The candidate bundles two things that both fail Shepherd's scope, plus one already-done sliver. The bot adapters violate all three of Shepherd's hard limits simultaneously (network server, multi-user, secrets storage for bot tokens) AND front a server-run agent loop — the AutoGPT executor that Shepherd explicitly is not. The billing/metering machinery (microdollar caps, Redis counters, percentage-to-hide-spend, rate-limit tiers, spend-to-reset credits) is multi-user SaaS economics with no analog in a single-user local console. The token-tracking piece the candidate notes as a possible reinforcement is already fully shipped (per-tile context bar + official 5h/7d plan-window usage, parsed locally at zero cost), so it is a no-add, not even an ADAPT — and I verified Shepherd deliberately carries NO dollar/pricing logic, so adding an Anthropic rate-card USD estimate is the only conceivable additive crumb, but it largely duplicates the plan-window % already displayed and isn't a real user need. Nothing here matches L1-L6 or the emerging scheduling idea; the dominant value is network ingress + billing. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note self-classifies MOSTLY DONT_ADD, and the codebase confirms it. Verdict DONT_ADD, high confidence. Effort moot, but were one to attempt the bot ingress it would be L-sized and require breaking the no-server invariant, which is a non-starter.

### Scheduling (3)
- **Unix-cron day-of-week normalization (correctness shim)** — Double mismatch, executor side: this is translation plumbing for AutoGPT's in-process scheduling engine (APScheduler), which Shepherd structurally lacks. Verified Shepherd has zero cron parsing and zero scheduler — os.date/os.time appear only for ledger date bucketing, never for triggering. With no cron input and no scheduler library, there is literally nothing for the shim to correct, so it is not code to copy. The real signal is a caution for the emerging "scheduling" idea (scheduled spawn): IF cron syntax is ever accepted, use a Unix-semantics cron library or normalize DOW (incl. ranges/wrap/steps) so spawns don't fire a day late. Knowledge to retain on the scheduling backlog item, not a feature to build.
- **Self-healing / orphan schedule & trigger cleanup** — Double mismatch splits this candidate cleanly down the middle, and the candidate's own SHEPHERD note already calls it: the LOCAL "fire target gone → auto-remove/surface-as-broken" half is ADAPT, the provider-deregistration/graph-node-walking half is DONT_ADD. The local half is not a new system — it is a fire-time existence predicate that rides on primitives Shepherd already has (shouldPrune/staleDuplicateKeys orphan cleanup, the router's dispatch-time "no window match" skip, the config-by-reference resolver, the validator family) and on two things that are queued/emerging (the cline-derived scheduled-spawn idea and L6's rule registry with its built-in ACTIVE/DISABLED/ERROR lifecycle). Because Shepherd already auto-prunes orphan tiles and already skips dispatch when the target vanished, the genuinely new work is: when a scheduled spawn or event rule references a deleted saved-profile (L1) or a vanished folder, auto-deactivate it and ledger it — a small pure addition, not a parallel engine. Verdict ADAPT, reinforcing the emerging "scheduling" idea (and overlapping L6). Effort S: one fire-time predicate plus a status transition and a ledger event, contingent on the scheduling/L6 host code existing. Confidence high: the split is unambiguous and the local pattern already has working precedents in the codebase.
- **Unified 'Scheduled / triggers' management page (list / inspect / cancel)** — Apply the double mismatch: AutoGPT's value here splits cleanly. The EXECUTOR/credential/server half (webhook presets receiving HTTP, hidden trigger-agents invoking a parent graph, the in-process scheduler running graph DAGs) is structurally impossible in Shepherd and is DONT_ADD — and the prompt itself flags the underlying graph-invocation as DONT_ADD. The MANAGEMENT half (merge all automations into one list with kind/cadence/next_run/target/active + jump + cancel) is a transferable operator pattern that maps perfectly onto Shepherd primitives Shepherd already has (jump-to-window, multi-tab overlay, listable queue, tile board). That makes this ADAPT, not ADD (must be reshaped: webhook→L6 event-rule, scheduled-poll→scheduled spawn, copilot follow-up→scheduled nudge) and not DONT_ADD (the operator need is real and the plumbing it needs is local). It is best treated as a CONSUMER of the emerging "scheduling" idea plus L6 rather than a standalone system — hence reinforces=scheduling. It is the natural "inventory face" for scheduled spawns once that lands, and it also surfaces L6 rules, so it is a small additive view on top of two queued items, not a parallel mechanism. Confidence is high that this is ADAPT/scheduling; the only real caveat (captured in against) is sequencing — build the scheduled-spawn primitive (and ideally L6) first, then this list is a thin tab.

### Executions & Monitoring (3)
- **Periodic-aggregate compute-and-serve pattern (materialized views + TTL caches)** — The candidate is explicitly self-marked DONT_ADD for the DB/materialized-view machinery and framed as prior-art validation, and code reading confirms that fully. Double mismatch: the feature's worth depends on Postgres + pg_cron + a FastAPI route layer exposing Prometheus metrics — i.e. the multi-user web-platform half of AutoGPT that Shepherd cannot host. The transferable operator pattern (cadence-precompute + cheap cached reads) is not just adaptable but already present: cc-core.lua's ledgerCacheStale/bucketEvents/fleetStats plus dashboard's ledgerSnapshot and the OFFICIAL_TTL/scan-cache TTLs are a one-to-one analog reshaped onto the local filesystem. Nothing new fits a watch/steer/spawn console; it doesn't extend L1-L6 or the emerging scheduling idea (the refresh cadence is an internal performance loop, not a user-facing scheduled spawn). Hence DONT_ADD as redundant with what Shepherd already has — high confidence.
- **Resumable streaming / reconnect (SSE/WebSocket live updates)** — The candidate brief itself pre-flags this as ALREADY HAVE / DONT_ADD, and the codebase confirms it. The "live updates" value prop is the entire point of Shepherd and is achieved by a 1s tail of local status files — strictly simpler and more robust than SSE/WebSocket for a single-user local console (no connection to drop, so reconnect/replay/heartbeat are non-problems; the file is the source of truth on every tick). Every sub-capability the feature lists already exists: live status (poll loop), subscribe-one vs subscribe-all (detail panel vs grid), and elapsed-time-of-in-flight-turn (per-tile `since` stamp + fmtDuration). What is NOT in Shepherd — the SSE/WebSocket transport, Redis Streams bus, and per-user socket ConnectionManager — is exactly what the hard scope forbids (no network server, no multi-user) and what the double mismatch rejects: the publisher side is AutoGPT's graph executor emitting exec/node updates (mismatch a), the subscriber side is multi-user socket fan-out (mismatch b). Adopting the transport would add a network attack surface and a server to a deliberately serverless local app for zero new capability. Nothing to ADAPT either, since the underlying need is fully met; the only thing this could have justified — surfacing in-flight elapsed time — is already shipped. Verdict DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Agent Protocol HTTP server (tasks/steps/artifacts lifecycle)** — Double mismatch hits both axes: the server SERVES (Shepherd has no network server — explicit hard scope limit) and each task RUNS an agent (Shepherd runs no agent loop; its "agent" is a GUI session Claude Code drives). The candidate's own SHEPHERD note concedes the server is DONT_ADD and only the data model is interesting. But that data model is already realized locally and the SQL/pagination/HTTP shape was deliberately rejected: the ledger (JSONL + capped overlay) + L4 task_done + L5 export/PR-status/git-changes + lineage = task/step/artifact run-history, and todos.md:562 says verbatim "No new SQL/pagination — reuse JSONL + the overlay's cap." No residual need to ADAPT: the kernel exists, the wrapper is structurally inadmissible. Hence DONT_ADD, high confidence. Not even a reinforces — it would duplicate, not extend, the ledger/L4/L5 line.

### Graphs & Builder (2)
- **Visual DAG builder canvas (drag-drop nodes + connect edges)** — Apply the double mismatch: the feature's worth depends entirely on executing the graph/blocks (typed edges carrying data between block ports, per-node config for block params, cycle/single-input validation for a runnable DAG). That is exactly the DONT_ADD trigger — "needs the in-process graph/block executor / block-code execution." There is no operator pattern to salvage here that isn't already covered: the management/observability value of "see the fleet as a graph" is already owned by L4's routing topology view (read-only, derived), and authoring of orchestration is already owned by L4's declarative route labels/process modes/join barriers/delegation, which intentionally avoid a visual DAG authoring layer. The seductive FOR (React Flow as the UI for routing topology) is a surface-level idiom match, not a need match — and even that idiom is a rendering choice for an already-specced read-only view, not a reason to import a block-composition canvas. ADAPT was considered and rejected: it would not reshape onto Shepherd's primitives, it would re-pose the rejected in-process-graph model; the genuinely transferable sliver (visualize routing as nodes/edges) is already L4 and needs no new authoring system. So DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Selectable reasoning / prompt strategies per run (PROMPT_STRATEGY)** — The feature's worth depends entirely on running the agent loop — the seven strategies ARE the executor's iteration policy (reflexion/ToT/LATS/debate are reasoning loops, not config). Shepherd structurally cannot host an agent loop (no executor, no in-process engine; Claude Code drives its own reasoning), so the strategy implementations are non-portable: DONT_ADD per the Double Mismatch rule. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note already grades it "Largely DONT_ADD," and my read of the codebase confirms it. The single thin pattern-crumb — "a labeled selectable config knob saved on a profile, like Shepherd's per-profile effort/model preset" — is not net-new: Shepherd already ships live /effort (EFFORT_LEVELS/effortCommand) and /model, and queued L1 already turns presets into saved agent profiles with model/effort/permMode/seedPrompt fields. So even the crumb is redundant/already-covered; if anything it mildly reinforces L1's existing per-profile config surface rather than warranting its own work. No category cleanly fits a non-graph-execution Shepherd here, so I tag it Graphs & Builder because the strategy is fundamentally a how-the-graph-executor-runs concern.

### Human-in-the-Loop (2)
- **Idempotent / race-safe review processing & ownership isolation** — Decompose the candidate. userId tenant isolation, DoS-bounded JSON validation, and shared-DB optimistic concurrency / status-409 are the multi-user-web-platform half of the AutoGPT double mismatch — Shepherd has no server, no DB, no users, no untrusted payload, and exactly one reviewer, so there is no race and no tenant to isolate. The prompt itself concedes "DONT_ADD (mostly)" and isolates one micro-nugget worth checking: guard against double-acting on a stale approval. I verified that nugget against the code: it is already implemented in the nonce-bound gate (cc-approve.sh 216-273 — per-request NONCE match, PID-owned CLAIM atomic mv so two waiters can't both consume, atomic-hardlink restore, parked collisions), and the comment at line 220 explicitly names "a double-clicked Approve whose first write was already consumed." So the only relevant behavior is redundant with shipped functionality, and everything else is structural mismatch. Clean DONT_ADD at high confidence.
- **CoPilot HITL via synthetic execution IDs** — Kernel already shipped as the nonce-bound PreToolUse gate; rest is executor/DB plumbing, so redundant: DONT_ADD, reinforcing L2.

### Notifications (2)
- **Notification batching/coalescing per-type window (anti-spam)** — DOUBLE MISMATCH applied: the candidate's IMPLEMENTATION (UserNotificationBatch model, QueueType.BATCH/BACKOFF, get_batch_delay timedeltas, email.py chunk-splitting, RabbitMQ) is pure multi-user-web-platform plumbing — per-user batch rows, email delivery limits, a broker — none of which Shepherd can or should host (no server, no users, no email). That half is DONT_ADD and the candidate already concedes it. What survives is the operator PATTERN: coalesce noisy per-type events into a windowed rollup. That pattern is real for a noisy fleet, but it is mostly ALREADY SATISFIED by Shepherd's per-episode push dedup. The genuinely new sliver is cross-SESSION coalescing (one ping summarizing "5 sessions done, 2 errored in the last 10m") rather than per-session episode dedup — a modest enhancement, not a new system. That makes it an ADAPT, and it belongs squarely inside the already-queued L6 event-callback rule engine (a `notify` processor with an optional debounce/window), not as a parallel notification subsystem. It is NOT a fresh Lx and NOT a scheduling item. Important design caveat that lowers its priority: any window delay must be opt-in and category-scoped, because Shepherd's whole reason for an alert (approval/error needs you NOW) is defeated by holding it — so coalescing should apply only to low-urgency rollup categories (done/info), never to the approval gate. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L6, high confidence (the source-side dedup is verifiable in the code and the L6 home is already specced).
- **Operator-only alert lane + email-channel delivery health (unsubscribe/bounce)** — Three-part feature, each part failing the fit test. (a) Operator-vs-user lane: Shepherd is the SOLE operator with no user audience, so "separate ops from user mail" has no second side; the "mirror to my Discord/ntfy topic" subset is already fully covered by FX.push→ntfy and the notification-history Alerts tab. (b) Billing/refund/low-balance alerts: pure money/marketplace events Shepherd structurally lacks. (c) Email delivery health (Postmark send, List-Unsubscribe headers, bounce webhook, email-verification flip on HTTP 406, recipient registry, batch purge): this is multi-user web-platform plumbing requiring a network server + outbound email service + stored recipient identities, all explicitly forbidden by context.md hard scope limits — and grep confirms none of it exists. The only genuinely transferable lesson, "back off a failing delivery channel," is already embodied: FX.push is pcall-wrapped best-effort and L6 generalizes escalation into opt-in rules. This is the textbook double mismatch (multi-user platform + money/marketplace) with a redundant kernel, so DONT_ADD at high confidence.

### Blocks (1)
- **Direct single-block execution endpoint (run one block, see its schema)** — Both AutoGPT mismatch halves fire at once. (a) Executor: the feature's substance is execute_graph_block → Block.execute building an ExecutionContext and streaming outputs — in-process block-code/LLM execution that Shepherd has no engine for and explicitly excludes (no agent loop, no API proxy; memory and context.md line 13). (b) Platform: charge_for_direct_block_execution is per-call billing/credits, which Shepherd (single-user, no secrets, no billing) cannot host. The only non-plumbing idea — inspect-then-invoke via empty input — is a generic capability-discovery pattern that Shepherd already realizes through non-executing means: it enumerates skills/agents/MCP servers from the local filesystem and validates a spawn's inputs BEFORE acting (L1 pre-flight validator, L3 template input-schema), rather than by executing the thing with empty input. So even the crumb is redundant, and the feature itself depends on the in-process block executor + credits Shepherd structurally cannot run. Clean DONT_ADD; not even an ADAPT, since adapting would mean reproducing the schema-discovery Shepherd already has queued without taking on any executor work.

### Store & Marketplace (1)
- **Public marketplace social/identity surfaces (creator profiles, ratings, public sharing, auto-gen media)** — This is the cleanest possible DONT_ADD: it hits the multi-user/marketplace half of the Double Mismatch on all five sub-surfaces simultaneously, with no graph-execution residue but also no salvageable operator pattern. Profiles/creator pages = identity for an audience that doesn't exist single-user. Ratings/reviews = cross-user aggregation that is a no-op at N=1, and the only local-meaningful slice (a private score) is already covered by the favorite/score idea, NOT by review-upsert + Creator SQL views. Auto-gen/uploaded media = image-gen API calls + object store, both structurally absent (Shepherd makes no API calls, hosts no media). Tokenized public share links (enable_execution_sharing/get_shared_execution with NO auth + SharedExecutionFile allowlist + autoShareExecutions) are a public HTTP surface — the single most explicitly prohibited thing in context.md's opening line. None of this reshapes onto a tile board / gate / queue / ledger because its worth depends entirely on serving multiple users over a network. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note already reaches DONT_ADD; I concur with high confidence. The only adjacent local ideas (private per-profile favorite/score; local session/profile export) are already noted as covered elsewhere and need no hosted link or second viewer — so nothing even reaches ADAPT.

### Copilot & Chat (1)
- **Conversational chat-to-build/run agent (turn loop + LLM authoring/repair)** — Both AutoGPT mismatches fire on the candidate's center of gravity. (a) Executor: every build tool (create/edit/customize/fix/validate_agent, decompose_goal) and the in-builder chat panel exist to author/repair a graph of blocks that the in-process engine RUNS; run_agent's payload is a GraphExecution. Shepherd runs no graph/block engine and makes no API calls — there is literally nothing for these tools to author or execute. (b) Library/persistence: create/edit save JSON into the graph library and BuilderChatPanel reverts to prior graph versions — a versioned graph store Shepherd doesn't have (it keeps only lightweight session lineage). The tutorial teaches a canvas/graph UI Shepherd lacks, so it is pure dead weight. The only Shepherd-shaped sliver is run_agent's operator pattern (resolve saved agent by name + gather inputs + fire/schedule) and decompose_goal's plan card — but those are NOT new: resolve-by-name + spawn-from-profile is L1, input-schema collection is already specced under L3 (todos.md L423-426), plan/TODO on a tile is L5, and fire-or-schedule is the emerging scheduling idea. So the transferable parts are fully covered and the irreducible value depends on the graph/block executor + graph library Shepherd cannot host. The candidate's own SHEPHERD note reaches the same DONT_ADD; I concur with high confidence. Categorized under Copilot & Chat since that is the feature's identity (a chat CoPilot tool-loop), even though its mechanism is graph authoring.
